# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be summarized in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

----
## About the CFWheels → Wheels Rebrand

**Note:** Starting with version 3.0.0, the project has been renamed from "CFWheels" to "Wheels" as part of our evolution and modernization efforts. This rebrand includes:

- **Project Name**: CFWheels → Wheels
- **GitHub Organization**: `cfwheels/cfwheels` → `wheels-dev/wheels`
- **Domain**: `cfwheels.org` → `wheels.dev`

All historical references to "CFWheels" in this changelog have been preserved for accuracy. When you see "CFWheels" in entries below, that was the project name at the time of that release.

# [4.2.0](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/releases/tag/v4.2.0) => 2026-10-12

### Added

- `transaction="savepoint"` runs `invokeWithTransaction()` or any persistence method as a nested unit: inside an open transaction it rolls back only its own writes when the method returns `false` or throws, and the outer transaction carries on. With no transaction open it behaves like `commit`. Records in a rolled-back unit get `afterRollback` right away and never `afterCommit`. Each unit runs a primary-key read before setting its savepoint, because Lucee drops a savepoint set before a transaction's first query. Inside a raw CFML `transaction {}` block a unit also nests as a savepoint on Lucee, BoxLang and Adobe ColdFusion; on RustCFML, which can't see the raw block, it joins it like `commit` (#3958, #4068)
- A `transactionIsolation` setting sets the default isolation level of the transactions Wheels opens for model writes and `invokeWithTransaction()` (default `read_committed`, unchanged). Set it to `read_uncommitted`, `repeatable_read` or `serializable`, or to `""` to send no isolation attribute and use the engine's or driver's default. An `isolation` argument on the call still wins (#4059)
- On SQL Server 2016+ (database compatibility level 130 or higher), a query whose IN lists would bind more than the 2097 parameters SQL Server accepts now runs: its largest lists of integers, plain decimals, strings and uniqueidentifiers are bound as one parameter each and expanded on the server with `STRING_SPLIT`, every value still bound. Queries within the limit keep their SQL. Any other list (dates, times, float, real, bit, text or binary values, or numbers such as `+5`, `.5` or `1E5`), and databases below compatibility level 130, keep the `Wheels.TooManyParameters` error, whose details now say which lists SQL Server runs as one parameter; long date and time lists are tracked in #4318 (#4103)
- Added `$snapshotRoutes()`, `$restoreRoutes()`, and `$clearRoutes()` to `wheels.WheelsTest` so tests that redefine the route table can snapshot and restore every route-derived structure (routes, staticRoutes, namedRoutePositions, the urlFor caches, and the router index) in one call, instead of hand-rolling a partial save/restore that leaks stale route state into later specs. ([#4194](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4194))
- `wheels migrate unlock` reports who holds the migration lock: the host, owner id, how long it has held it and when its lease expires. While a live instance holds it, the command exits non-zero and names `wheels migrate unlock --force`, which removes the lock and prints what it removed. The `migrate` MCP tool has the same `unlock` action, and it only removes the lock when `force` is true. In the framework, `migrationLockStatus()` returns the same details and `releaseMigrationLock(force)` removes the lock: without `force`, only an expired lease. The `Wheels.MigrationLockTimeout` error now points at the command (#4209).
- `$testClient().get()` accepts an optional `timeout` (seconds, default 30) that bounds the underlying HTTP request, so a request that may hang fails the asserting test case rather than timing out the whole test run (#4219)
- On SQL Server 2016 and later, a statement whose date or timestamp IN lists push it past the parameter limit now runs: each long list binds as one `STRING_SPLIT` parameter, its values compared the way the same list is when it fits (in the column's own type on a `DATETIME`, `SMALLDATETIME` or `TIME` column, otherwise as `datetime2(7)` / `date`), so it matches exactly the same rows. Values must be written as `yyyy-mm-dd` with an optional time, `HH:mm` with optional seconds for a time, or be CFML dates; dates in other forms still throw `Wheels.TooManyParameters` (#4318)
- Background jobs can call `heartbeat()` from inside `perform()` to signal that they are still running. Once a job has heartbeated, it is reclaimed when its heartbeats stop for its heartbeat grace, even inside its timeout: the job's own `this.heartbeatGrace`, or else `set(jobsHeartbeatGraceSeconds = n)` (default 300; `0` turns it off; values below 30 are raised to 30). Heartbeat at least every grace / 3. The stale-job reaper measures from the latest heartbeat (`COALESCE(heartbeatAt, updatedAt)`), so a long job that keeps heartbeating isn't reaped and run a second time. A job that never heartbeats is reclaimed only after its claim window (its timeout plus the larger of 60 seconds and its timeout), as before. A stopped heartbeat isn't proof the worker died: a `perform()` stuck in a long call that can't heartbeat is reclaimed too, so heartbeat around such calls or raise the grace. `heartbeat()` never extends a job's timeout, which stays the hard cap on one attempt. An exclusive job's heartbeat renews its lease by its grace window, so a job that stops heartbeating loses its lease on the same clock it's reclaimed on. A heartbeat after the job's claim was reaped and re-issued throws `Wheels.Job.Fenced`, telling the old attempt to stop. Each new claim clears the previous attempt's heartbeat. `wheels_jobs` gains a nullable `heartbeatAt` column, added to existing tables automatically (never inside a transaction); without it, `heartbeat()` renews `updatedAt` instead. ([#4477](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4477), [#4502](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4502))
- Jobs gain `this.idempotent` (default `true`). After a reap, a job is retried only when its class is idempotent, which is today's behaviour. A class with `this.idempotent = false` is not re-run: the reaped attempt ends with the new status `interrupted`, counted by `queueStats()` and `JobWorker.getStats()` and purgeable with `purge(status = "interrupted")`. ([#4477](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4477))
- `enqueue()`, `enqueueIn()` and `enqueueAt()` accept `uniqueKey`: only one job is written per key, including when two enqueues race each other. A duplicate writes nothing and returns `{enqueued: false, duplicate: true, status: "duplicate"}` with the existing job's `id`; a key stays taken while its row exists, until it is purged. Every enqueue result now includes `enqueued` and `duplicate`. `wheels_jobs` gains a nullable `uniqueKey` column (VARCHAR(255)) with a unique index (filtered to non-NULL keys on SQL Server). It is added to existing tables automatically under the migration lock, taken without waiting, and backfilled from `id`; servers still on the previous version keep enqueuing during a rolling upgrade. `enqueue(uniqueKey=...)` throws `Wheels.Job.UniqueKeyUnavailable` when the column can't be added, and `Wheels.Job.InvalidUniqueKey` for a key over 255 characters. ([#4478](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4478))
- Background jobs can now be made exclusive: set `this.exclusive = true` and a second run of that job class can't start on any server while one is running. A job can instead set `this.concurrencyKey = "..."`, or define `concurrencyKeyFor(struct data)` (which takes precedence), to share one lease among the jobs with the same key (for example one run per account). A job that finds its lease busy waits as `pending` without using up an attempt, and counts as skipped. The lease is held in a new `wheels_job_locks` table, created on first use, through `wheels.LeaseLock`, the cross-server lease the migrator already used, now extracted for reuse. Exclusivity is only as strong as the lease: it lasts the job's claim timeout plus a grace period, and a run that outlives it (for example one stuck in a blocked database call) can overlap a second run; that is logged and counted as a lost lease. Servers sharing the table need synchronised clocks. ([#4479](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4479))
- Background jobs gain per-host control for apps running on more than one server.
  - `set(jobsMaxConcurrentPerHost = n)` caps how many jobs one server runs at once. The default `0` means no cap, which is today's behaviour. The cap is enforced in the worker, so `wheels jobs work`, `processNext()` and the new `JobRunner.tick()` all respect it. The count and the claim happen under one lock per host, so two polls can't both take the last slot.
  - `new wheels.JobRunner().drain(expiresInSeconds)` / `.resume()` stop a server from starting new jobs while the jobs it is already running finish (for deploys). Reaping continues, and an expired drain lifts itself.
  - `JobRunner.tick(queues, maxConcurrent, timeout, maxJobs)` is one round for a scheduled task: it registers the server in the new `wheels_job_hosts` table (auto-created, never inside a transaction), reaps, and runs up to `maxJobs` jobs unless the server is draining or full. `JobRunner.status()` reports the server's running jobs, cap and drain.
  - A server is identified by the host name recorded in `wheels_jobs.claimedBy`. Set `set(jobsHostName = "...")` when several app servers share one machine, so each gets its own cap and drain. `processNext()` results gain `capped` and `draining`. ([#4480](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4480))
- `wheels jobs drain` stops the running server from starting new background jobs while the jobs it is already running finish, and `wheels jobs resume` lifts it. For deploys, `wheels jobs drain --wait` blocks until the server has no running jobs (up to 600 seconds, or `--wait=<seconds>`) and exits with an error if jobs are still running, so the server can be stopped safely. `--expires=<seconds>` sets when the drain lifts itself (default 3600; 0 = until resumed). `wheels jobs status` now also shows this server's running jobs, cap and drain. ([#4480](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4480))
- Recurring background jobs.
  - **Defining schedules.** Define schedules in `config/schedules.cfm`:
    - `schedule("digest").job("DigestJob").cron("0 7 * * MON").timezone("America/New_York")`
    - `schedule("canary").job("CanaryJob").every(15, "minutes")`
    They are synced to a new `wheels_job_schedules` table at application start. Apps can also insert their own rows at runtime (e.g. from an admin page); the sync never changes those, and a code schedule removed from the file is disabled, not deleted.
  - **Running them.** Call `new wheels.JobScheduler().enqueueDue()` regularly on one or every server. Each due slot is enqueued once, with `uniqueKey = "<schedule>:<slot time, UTC>"`, however many servers call it.
  - **Expressions.** Cron takes the standard 5 fields with lists, ranges, steps and month and day names, the `@hourly` to `@yearly` aliases, and an IANA time zone. A local time skipped by a DST change runs at the next valid instant, and a repeated one runs once. Intervals are minutes or hours, aligned to UTC.
  - **Missed slots.** `catchUp("latest")` (default) runs only the newest missed slot within `catchUpWindow()` (default 3600 seconds); `catchUp("none")` runs only a current one. A slot never runs twice.
  ([#4481](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4481))
- A token-gated jobs tick route, `/wheels/jobs/tick`, lets an engine scheduled task, cron job or systemd timer run background jobs inside the app server without a worker process. Each call runs one `JobRunner.tick()` (reap dead workers' jobs, then run jobs up to the per-host cap) and returns a JSON summary; an overlapping call on the same server is skipped. The route is off (404) unless `set(jobsRunnerToken = "...")` is set; every call must send the token in the `X-Wheels-Jobs-Token` header (compared in constant time), or as `?token=` only with `set(jobsRunnerTokenInQuery = true)` for schedulers that can only set a URL (the token then ends up in access logs). Requests carrying a forwarding header (`X-Forwarded-For`, `Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP`, `CF-Connecting-IP`) are refused: the token, not the client's address, is the boundary. It works with `enablePublicComponent = false`. `wheels jobs tick` runs one tick from the command line. ([#4482](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4482))
- Background jobs gained lifecycle hooks. `beforePerform(data)` and `afterPerform(data, result)` run with `perform()`, under its timeout, and a throw from either fails the attempt. `onSuccess(result)` runs once the job is recorded as completed, and `onFailure(error, attempt, isFinal)` after each failed attempt, with `isFinal = true` when the job won't be tried again (including when a crashed worker's last attempt is reaped). Both are best-effort: a throw is logged and never changes the job's outcome. `set(jobsOnFailure = "component.path.method")` adds an app-wide failure hook, called with one struct (`jobId`, `jobClass`, `queue`, `attempt`, `maxRetries`, `isFinal`, `error`) as its `event` argument; job data is never passed to it. `perform()`'s return value is now stored in a new `wheels_jobs.result` column (added automatically): simple values as-is, anything else as JSON, cut short at 4000 bytes with a `...[truncated]` marker. ([#4483](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4483))
- `wheels jobs install` writes a migration that creates the background-job tables (`wheels_jobs`, `wheels_job_hosts`, `wheels_job_schedules`, `wheels_job_locks`), so an app can apply the jobs schema once, reviewed and under migration control, instead of having every server create and alter it at startup. The migration adopts tables Wheels already created automatically, adding only what they lack, and running it again changes nothing. Apps that use it set `set(jobsAutoCreateTables = false)`, which stops the framework from creating or altering the job tables at runtime; a missing table then raises `Wheels.Job.SchemaMissing` from enqueue and from a worker's poll, with a pointer to `wheels jobs install`. Auto-create stays the default (`jobsAutoCreateTables = true`). The migration's `down()` drops the job tables, including ones it adopted, and every job in them. The job schema is now defined once (`wheels.JobSchema`), so the migration and auto-create build the same tables. ([#4484](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4484))
- Added `afterCommit` and `afterRollback` model callbacks. `afterCommit(methods, on)` runs its methods only after the database transaction actually **commits** — the right place for side effects that must not happen on a rollback (enqueue a job, send mail, bust a cache, call an external API). `afterRollback(methods, on)` runs when the transaction **rolls back**. Both cover `create`, `update`, and `delete`, support an optional `on="create,update,delete"` filter, and (for nested writes or an `invokeWithTransaction` block) fire once, together, when the **outermost** transaction resolves. With no transaction (`transactionMode="none"`) `afterCommit` fires immediately after each write. A callback that throws runs after the commit, so the commit stands; the error is logged to `wheels.log` and propagated. These callbacks require a Wheels-managed transaction (`transaction()` / `invokeWithTransaction`): a write placed inside a raw CFML `transaction {}` block is skipped (with a one-time `wheels.log` warning) on Lucee and BoxLang, and is not detectable on Adobe CF or RustCFML — there the behaviour inside a raw `transaction {}` is left to the engine and is not guaranteed.
- In the `development` environment with `showErrorInformation` on (its default there), an error response asked for as JSON (`Accept: application/json` or `?format=json`) is now the structured payload the error page's Copy button builds: exception type and message, suggested action, location, source snippet and classified stack, plus `statusCode`. `Accept: text/markdown` (without `application/json`) or `?format=md` returns the same content as Markdown. Both use the error page's status code and drop anything the request wrote before it failed. Testing, maintenance and production responses are unchanged. The payload's location now picks an app frame that is the throw site itself, as the error page does, and its JSON keys keep their case on every engine.
- `appCacheFetch()`, `appCacheRead()`, `appCacheWrite()`, `appCacheExists()`, `appCacheDelete()` and `appCacheClear()` cache your own computed values in the application cache. `appCacheFetch(key, callback, time)` returns the cached value or computes, stores and returns it. They work in every environment. A cached `false`, `0` or `""` counts as a hit. Keys are case-sensitive strings or a struct or array of values. Entries live in their own `data` category, so `appCacheClear()` never empties the action, page, partial or query caches. `$addToCache()` now returns whether it stored the value.
- `travelTo(moment, callback)` on `wheels.WheelsTest` freezes the framework clock for the callback, so time-dependent behaviour is testable deterministically without sleeping. Framework code reads the clock through a `$now()` / `$tick()` seam that returns the live clock in production (no behaviour change) and the frozen instant under `travelTo()`: cache expiry and culling, the `RateLimiter` rate-limit windows, and model `createdAt` / `updatedAt` timestamps honour it. The override is request-scoped and restored in a catch-free finally, so it never leaks into another spec (#4387)
- `wheels deploy` now mounts a top-level `volumes:` list on every app container (Kamal's format: `host:container` or `host:container:ro|rw`, a host path or a named volume), so a SQLite app can keep its database on the host across redeploys. Entries are validated, and accessory `volumes:` / `directories:` arguments are now quoted like the other docker arguments, so paths with spaces or special characters work. Because they are no longer passed through the remote shell unquoted, a `~` or `$VAR` in an accessory volume's host path is no longer expanded on the host: write the absolute path (for example `/home/deploy/data` instead of `~/data`) (#4018)
- `wheels deploy` can run the database migrations once per deploy, on one host. Add `migrate: true` (or `migrate: {host: <host>, timeout: <seconds>}`) to `config/deploy.yml`. The migrate host (default: the first host of the first role, which must be proxy-fronted) boots and cuts over first, with `WHEELS_MIGRATE_ON_BOOT=true` and a longer health-check timeout (default 300s). Every other app container gets `WHEELS_MIGRATE_ON_BOOT=false`, so hosts sharing a database no longer all migrate at start. A migration that fails stops the deploy before any host cuts over. Without the block nothing changes. Apps with only job/worker roles are refused for now (#4063).
- New `set(migrateOnBoot=true)`: migrate to the latest version when the application starts, and fail the start (`Wheels.BootMigrationFailed`, written to the wheels log) when a migration is still pending afterwards. A boolean `WHEELS_MIGRATE_ON_BOOT` environment variable overrides both `migrateOnBoot` and `autoMigrateDatabase`: `true` migrates strictly, `false` never migrates at start. Without it, `autoMigrateDatabase` behaves exactly as before (#4063).
- `escapeForLike()`, a global string helper that escapes the `LIKE` wildcards (`%`, `_`, `[`) and the `\` escape character in a search term, so a user's input is matched literally in a `LIKE '...' ESCAPE '\'` comparison instead of being read as wildcards (#4355)
- `$testClient().fromAddress(ip)` sets the client address (`REMOTE_ADDR`) for subsequent requests, so per-client behaviour such as rate limiting and IP rules can be exercised in specs. In the isolated test application the address reaches middleware through the request context; it never changes the `cgi` scope and is ignored outside the test context (#4343)
- `forgetCachedQueries()`, a public helper to drop the per-request finder cache that `cacheQueriesDuringRequest` fills: `model("Post").forgetCachedQueries()` clears one model's slot (and returns the model for chaining), `forgetCachedQueries("Post")` clears it by name from anywhere, and `forgetCachedQueries(all = true)` clears every model's. A bare call outside a model throws rather than silently wiping everything. Replaces the old advice to delete the reserved `request.wheels["$queryCache"]` key directly (#4364)
- `wheels framework install` installs the CLI's bundled framework into `vendor/wheels/` for an app that has none (for example one moving off CommandBox), creating `vendor/` if needed and pinning `box.json`'s `wheels-core` when present. It refuses when a framework is already installed (use `wheels upgrade apply` to replace one), and `--to=<version>` must match the bundled version (no downloads)
- New controller helper `isSafeRedirectUrl(redirectUrl)` returns `true` when a URL stays on this site (a relative URL, or one on the current host), the same rule `redirectTo(url=…)` applies, so a return-to URL can be checked before it's stored or linked. Like every framework helper it can't be routed as an action: an app with a controller action named `isSafeRedirectUrl` must rename it.
- `enqueue()`, `enqueueIn()` and `enqueueAt()` accept `transactional=false`, and a job class can set `this.transactional = false`: the job is then written after the outermost Wheels-managed transaction ends, whether it commits or rolls back, so failure notices and audit records survive a rollback. With no transaction open it's written immediately; the default (`true`) is unchanged. A raw `transaction {}` block and a request that ends with `abort` aren't covered
- `wheels jobs enqueue <JobName>` adds a job to the queue from the command line: now, after `--in=<seconds>`, or at `--at=<ISO 8601 time>`, with `--data` (a JSON object), `--queue` and `--priority`. It accepts only a component under `app/jobs/` (or a `jobClassPrefixes` path) that extends `wheels.Job`, checked before the class is loaded. Scheduling here is a one-off delayed run; there is no recurring schedule.
- `wheels jobs work --stop-when-empty` exits once a poll finds no job ready to run, so one-shot batches from cron or CI finish. `--max-jobs=N` alone still waits for N jobs; combine the two to cap a batch.
- The jobs tick route (`/wheels/jobs/tick`) takes its batch from settings: `jobsRunnerTickMaxJobs` (most jobs per call, `1`-`1000`, default `1`), `jobsRunnerTickTimeout` (seconds per job, `1`-`86400`, default `300`) and `jobsRunnerTickQueues` (comma list, default every queue); a fractional or out-of-range value falls back to the default. The defaults keep today's behaviour of one job per call. They are settings rather than request parameters, so a caller holding the token can't widen the work one request does.
- `LocalDisk` storage driver: an opt-in `resolveSymlinks` config flag (default `false`) that adds a stricter containment check on top of the always-on lexical guard (#3912). When enabled, `$resolve()` canonicalises paths through the filesystem so a symlink planted under `root` that targets outside it is **rejected** with `Wheels.Storage.InvalidKey` instead of followed. The disk runs a one-time behavioural capability probe at construction and **fails closed** with `Wheels.Storage.InvalidConfiguration` on a runtime that can't resolve symlinks (the JVM-free RustCFML engine, or Windows without symlink privilege), rather than silently enabling an ineffective strict mode. Detection is a capability probe, never an engine-name check. ([#4020](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4020))
- `belongsTo(polymorphic=true)`, `hasMany(as=...)` and `hasOne(as=...)` accept a `foreignType` argument naming the polymorphic type column, so an underscore-shaped schema (`commentable_id` / `commentable_type`, as `t.references(polymorphic=true)` writes it with `useUnderscoreReferenceColumns`) works when both `foreignKey` and `foreignType` are passed. The default stays `<name>type`
- `renderNotFound(message, type, extendedInfo)` — a public controller helper for producing a 404 from app code: it sets the 404 status, shows the typed error in development (when `showErrorInformation` is on) and the `onmissingtemplate.cfm` page in production. Generated scaffolds now call it instead of the internal `$throwErrorOrShow404Page`, whose signature was never a public contract (that internal still works for apps generated by 4.1.2). It is a controller mixin (not a `wheels.Global` helper), so it is scoped to controllers and not added to models. Because it is a public controller helper it is now a reserved action name — an existing app with an action literally named `renderNotFound` would get `Wheels.ActionNotAllowed` instead of dispatching (no such action exists in the shipped examples or guides). An app that already defines its own `renderNotFound` controller helper transparently overrides this one, and generated scaffolds then call that helper with `message=`. ([#3900](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3900))
- Specs can assert how many SQL statements a call sends: `assertQueries(count, callback)`, `assertNoQueries(callback)`, `assertQueriesMatch(pattern, callback, count)`, `assertNoQueriesMatch(pattern, callback)` and `recordQueries(callback)`, in every `wheels.WheelsTest` spec. They count what the model layer sends (a query-cache hit sends nothing), take an optional `dataSource` filter, nest, return the callback's result, and list the statements on failure with each bound value as `?`
- Models report what their last successful save wrote: `savedChanges()`, `hasSavedChange(property)`, `savedChangeFrom(property)` and `savedChangedProperties()`, the saved counterparts of `allChanges()`, `hasChanged()`, `changedFrom()` and `changedProperties()`, which a save resets. They're available from the save's `after*` callbacks on, and each `afterCommit` sees the save that queued it, so an `afterCommit` can act on what a commit changed (for example, announce a record once each time it ends up published) without a hand-kept flag. An `afterRollback` sees the save that was rolled back, after which the previous state is restored. `reload()` and `clearChangeInformation()` clear it
- Job schedules (`config/schedules.cfm`, `schedule()`) now run from the normal job processing: every worker poll (`wheels jobs work`), `processQueue()` and `JobRunner.tick()` enqueue due schedule slots, at most once every `jobsScheduleCheckSeconds` (default 15; `0` turns it off), so no separate scheduler process is needed. Any number of servers can do it at once: each slot is still enqueued exactly once. An app without schedules is unaffected: the check only runs when the schedules table exists. `JobRunner.tick()` reports the slots it enqueued as `scheduled`.
- New `strictArguments` setting reports an argument that a framework declaration doesn't know, instead of ignoring it: a typo or another framework's name such as `foreign_key`, `orderby` or a migration column's `nullable`. It checks associations, validations, callback registration, `property()`, `table()`, the finders (including dynamic finders), controller `caches()` / `protectsFromForgery()` / `provides()` / `usesLayout()` and migration column functions. `"warn"` (the development default) logs one `wheels.log` line per model or controller, function and argument, with a suggested name; `"throw"` raises `Wheels.UnknownArgument`; `"off"` (the default elsewhere) keeps the old behaviour
- `TestClient` (`$testClient()`) sends the session's authenticity token to forgery-protected actions the way a browser does. It picks up the token from a page it fetched (the `csrf-token` meta tag from `csrfMetaTags()`, or a form's `authenticityToken` field) and sends it on POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE as the `X-CSRF-Token` header and, on a form body, the `authenticityToken` field. The token is dropped when the session cookie changes, as at login, and a token or field the test passes itself wins. New methods: `csrfToken()`, `fetchCsrfToken(path)`, `withCsrfToken(token)` and `withoutCsrfToken()`.
- `withAdvisoryLock()` now accepts an opt-in `transaction = true` argument that pins the lock acquire, the callback, and the release to one connection inside a transaction, so the lock genuinely covers the callback's own queries. On PostgreSQL the lock is transaction-scoped and auto-releases when the transaction ends; on MySQL and SQL Server it is a session lock released before the transaction closes. Supported on PostgreSQL, MySQL, and SQL Server; other databases throw rather than silently running session-scoped. The lock is released even when the callback ends the request with `abort` or `redirectTo()`. Recommended for short critical sections — a long-running callback holds its row locks and a pooled connection (and blocks PostgreSQL VACUUM) for the whole call. Defaults to `false` (the existing behaviour). ([#4198](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4198))
- `wheels upgrade check` checks the 4.1 → 4.2 items when an app below 4.2.0 targets 4.2.0 or later, and points at the 4.1 → 4.2 guide instead of reporting "no new breaking changes". Items that fail at runtime on 4.2 (an environment selected without `WHEELS_ENV`, an invalid `joinType`, `condition`/`unless` shapes 4.2 rejects, a `renderNotFound` route, `tinyInt1isBit=false`) fail the check; the rest (event templates, `/vendor` in `.gitignore`, `returnAs="structs"`, `X-Forwarded-Proto` without `trustProxyHeaders`, and others) are advisory.
- `wheels upgrade check` (4.1 → 4.2) adds three advisories: app code that reads `persisted` from a job result (4.2's `enqueue()` throws `Wheels.Job.EnqueueFailed` instead), a `tests/runner.cfm` that doesn't include the built-in `wheels/tests/app-runner.cfm`, and, as lines to review, `where` conditions, query-builder calls and dynamic finders that compare with an empty string, which 4.2 binds as `''` instead of NULL. Each names the file and line, with comments stripped first.
- `wheels upgrade check` names each framework fix that lives in the app-owned `public/Application.cfc` and is missing from the app's copy (session cookie settings, the `onError` DI guard and running-app message, the test-context include, the jBCrypt load path, the `.env` boolean parser, the reload helpers, header fallback and subfolder redirect), with the guide section for each. A missing `onApplicationEnd()` / `onSessionEnd()` `arguments.applicationScope` routing fails the check, because on Adobe ColdFusion it can leave the whole site erroring after a shutdown.
- `wheels lookup <query>` looks up the Wheels API reference and the guides offline, and so does the new `lookup` MCP tool. A function name (`findAll`, `model.findAll`) prints its full entry: signature, parameters, example and link. A phrase (`nested resources`) lists the best-matching guide sections and functions, each with a link and an id that prints the whole entry. The CLI ships a docs index for its own framework version, built at release and snapshot time, so no network, server or project is needed.

### Changed

- On MySQL, `t.boolean()` / `addColumn(columnType="boolean")` now create a `BIT(1)` column instead of `TINYINT(1)`. A DSN with `tinyInt1isBit=false` reported `TINYINT(1)` as a plain integer, so the model validated boolean properties as numbers ("is not a number"). Existing `TINYINT(1)` columns are unchanged: drop `tinyInt1isBit=false` from the DSN, or convert the column with `changeColumn(columnType="boolean")`. `BIT(1)` values read back as `1` on Lucee and Adobe and as `true` on BoxLang; both are truthy and compare equal in CFML (#3897)
- On Oracle 23ai and later, `t.boolean()` / `addColumn(columnType="boolean")` now create a native `BOOLEAN` column instead of a numeric column, and the model maps a `BOOLEAN` column to `cf_sql_bit`. A numeric column reached the model as a number, so automatic validation rejected `true` ("is not a number"). Oracle releases before 23ai get `NUMBER(1)` (#4097). Existing numeric boolean columns are unchanged: Oracle cannot change the datatype of a non-empty column, so convert one by adding a `BOOLEAN` column, copying the values and dropping the old column. A `BOOLEAN` value reads back as `1` / `0` on Lucee and Adobe CF and as `true` / `false` on BoxLang (#3897)
- An empty string in a `where` value now binds as a real empty string instead of SQL NULL, so `count(where="col = ''")` matches rows that store `''`, and `col <> ''` / `col != ''` match every other non-NULL row. Before, all three compared against NULL and matched nothing. This applies to `findAll()` / `count()` and the other finders, `updateAll()` / `deleteAll()` `where` conditions, dynamic finders such as `findOneByName("")` and the query builder, so `updateAll()` and `deleteAll()` with such a condition now change those rows. Use `IS NULL` / `IS NOT NULL` (or `whereNull()` / `whereNotNull()`) to match NULL. Lookups keyed on request values, such as `findOne(where="token = '#params.token#'")` or `findOneByToken(params.token)`, now match a row that stores `''` when the value is empty, so check required values first. The unquoted `NULL` keyword still binds as NULL; an empty value whose column binds as a number, date, time or boolean still matches nothing (SQLite binds its text datetimes as strings, so they follow the new behaviour); and Oracle, which stores `''` as NULL, is unchanged (#4055)
- A migration that uses a column type the database adapter doesn't map (for example `t.column(columnName="payload", columnType="jsonb")`) now fails at migrate time with `Wheels.Migrator.UnsupportedColumnType`, naming the type and the adapter and listing the supported types, instead of emitting a column with no type; that typeless column was a DDL syntax error on most databases, a raw missing-key error on Oracle, and on SQLite a column whose model failed to load (#4095)
- Instances that share a database now run its migrations one at a time: `migrateTo()`, `migrateToLatest()`, `migrateIndividual()` and `redoMigration()` hold a cross-process lock, a lease row in the new `wheels_migrator_locks` table, on every supported database. An instance that waits re-reads which migrations are pending once it has the lock, so it doesn't run them again. It gives up after `migrationLockTimeout` (default 300 seconds) with `Wheels.MigrationLockTimeout`, naming the host holding the lock. The lease (`migrationLockLease`, default 3600 seconds) is renewed before each migration step, and a holder that died frees the lock when its lease runs out; a single migration step that runs longer than the lease can lose the lock to another instance (`Wheels.MigrationLockLost`). To clear a stuck lock at once, run `wheels migrate unlock --force` or delete its row from `wheels_migrator_locks` (#4134)
- `singularize()` returns `psychoanalysis` for `psychoanalyses` instead of the misspelled `psychoanalyasis`; other `-ses` words such as `bases`, `theses` and `analyses` are unchanged (#4187)
- `singularize("databases")` returns `database` instead of `databasis`, so a name such as `Databases` or `userDatabases` now singularizes to `Database` / `userDatabase`. An app that relied on the old `databasis` output, for example a model class or table named after it, needs to rename it. That's unlikely, but check before upgrading. Other `-ases` words (`bases`, `cases`, `phases`, `purchases`, `vases`) are unchanged (#4189)
- On MySQL and PostgreSQL, `withAdvisoryLock()` on a tenant (non-shared) model takes its lock on the active tenant's datasource instead of the application's default one, like the model's queries, SQL Server and `withAdvisoryLock(transaction = true)`. Two tenants on different databases using the same lock name no longer exclude each other, and no longer wait for each other inside one application (on MySQL, whose lock names are server-wide, tenant databases on the same MySQL server still share them); for one lock across all tenants, call `withAdvisoryLock()` through a shared model. The lock queries also build their connection options like other model queries, leaving out an empty username and password (#4223)
- On SQL Server, an `=`, `<>`, `IN` or `NOT IN` condition on a `DATETIME` or `SMALLDATETIME` column now compares in the column's own type. A value with a fraction of a second, such as `createdAt = '2026-01-02 10:00:00.123'` on a `timestamps()` column, matched no row, because every engine sends the value as `DATETIME2(7)` while the column stores 1/300-second (or one-minute) steps. An `=` or `IN` condition with such a value can now match the row that holds it, and a `<>` or `NOT IN` condition now leaves that row out, as SQL Server does with a literal: `createdAt <> '2026-01-02 10:00:00.001'` used to return a row stored as `10:00:00.000` and now doesn't, because `.001` rounds to `.000` in a `DATETIME`. Whole-second values, ranges (`<`, `>=`, `BETWEEN`) and `DATETIME2` / `DATE` columns compare as before (#4326)
- Soft-deleting a record no longer destroys or unlinks `dependent=` children that a restore would need. Children whose model has a soft-delete column are soft-deleted with it, as before. Other children are left alone: `dependent="delete"` / `"deleteAll"` no longer hard-deletes them and `dependent="remove"` / `"removeAll"` no longer sets their foreign keys to `NULL`. On a soft delete, `dependent=` now applies to them only on a real delete (#4370)
- `delete()`, `deleteAll()`, `deleteOne()` and `deleteByKey()` with `softDelete=false` are always real deletes. They now also remove rows that were already soft-deleted, and the `dependent=` children of a deleted record, without `includeSoftDeletes=true`. An explicit `includeSoftDeletes=false` no longer keeps them. With `instantiate=true` those rows now run their delete callbacks too (#4371)
- `reload()` throws `Wheels.RecordNotFound` when the object's row no longer exists, instead of silently blanking every property. Uncaught in a controller action, that renders the 404 page. An object with no key (unsaved, or saved on a database that returns no generated key, such as Oracle) is left as it is, where it used to have every property blanked (#4372)
- When a Wheels-managed transaction rolls back because its method threw, `afterRollback` callbacks now run after the transaction block has ended, as they already did for a rollback without an exception. A callback's database writes on this path are kept instead of being discarded with the transaction, and a callback that throws doesn't replace the method's exception
- App test runs keep the runner's 30-minute request timeout: the `tests/populate.cfm` that `wheels new` creates no longer lowers it to 300 seconds, and the runner restores its own limit after an app's `populate.cfm` runs. A run that is stopped before it finishes reports an error (and says when the request timeout stopped it) instead of 0 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors.
- `wheels browser install` now runs `wheels browser setup` (an alias, as `wheels packages install` is of `add`). It used to print that LuCLI intercepts the command and it won't reach Wheels, then do nothing; it does reach Wheels, since LuCLI intercepts `install` only as the first argument.
- Browser specs that do not run no longer count as passed. Without Playwright (or in CI without `WHEELS_BROWSER_CI_ENABLE`), each `browserDescribe()` spec is reported as skipped, with the reason. When Playwright is installed but the browser cannot start, each one is reported as an error with the launch message, unless `WHEELS_BROWSER_SKIP_LAUNCH_FAILURES=true` asks to skip them. `wheels test` now counts skipped specs (`3 passed, 1 skipped`, not green) and lists each skip reason.
- `insertAll()` and `upsertAll()` now honour their `transaction` argument like `save()`. With the default (`commit`), all of a call's batches run in one transaction, so a large import is all-or-nothing and a failing batch no longer leaves the earlier batches behind. Pass `transaction="none"` for the previous per-batch commits; `rollback` keeps nothing
- The `CLAUDE.md` that ships with apps (from `wheels new`, the starter app and the core package) is now short enough for AI coding tools that cap or skip the middle of long files: conventions, common mistakes, testing and a topic index, under 8 KB (it was over 32 KB). The reference topics (models, routing, views, caching, middleware, DI, auth, storage, packages, migrations, jobs, SSE) moved unchanged to `.ai/<topic>.md`, and `AGENTS.md` tells agents to open a topic file when the task reaches it. Existing apps can copy `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `.ai/` from a new `wheels new` app. The build check now enforces the size budget and that every topic file is linked from the index. (#3960)
- Global helpers (`app/views/helpers.cfm`) are now available to a controller once Wheels initializes it, rather than as soon as the object exists. A controller built directly with `new` or `CreateObject()`, or code in a controller's own pseudo-constructor (outside its functions), no longer sees them. Call global helpers from `config()` or an action instead.
- When `config/settings.cfm` sets `dataSourceName` and not `coreTestDataSourceName`, `coreTestDataSourceName` now defaults to that `dataSourceName` instead of a datasource named after the folder of the app's front controller. An app that ran the framework test suite on a datasource with that folder's name must now set `coreTestDataSourceName` to it explicitly.
- When a run of the framework's own test suite (`/wheels/core/tests`) would use the app's primary datasource, it now needs `<datasource>_test` and ignores `allowTestsAgainstPrimaryDatasource`: without the test datasource the run is refused and `wheels.log` notes that the setting was ignored, because the suite's `populate.cfm` drops and recreates tables. An explicit `useTestDB=false` still runs it on the primary datasource. Runs that pick another datasource (`?db=`, or a `coreTestDataSourceName` that isn't the primary) are unchanged, and app test runs keep honouring the setting. (#4254)
- From Wheels 4.2, CSRF protection accepts a valid `X-CSRF-Token` header on any non-GET request. Earlier versions also required `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest`, which Turbo and `fetch()` don't send. When a request carries both a form field and a header, it passes if either holds a valid token, as in Rails. `startFormTag()` and `buttonTo()` gain `authenticityToken=false` to leave the hidden field out. Use it for every form in cached markup that other visitors receive, and supply the token at request time from `csrfMetaTags()`; the caching guide shows how (#3959)
- Authenticity tokens are now compared exactly on every engine: the submitted value must be the whole session token (#3959)
- **Breaking:** `wheels deploy` now requires an accessory `port:` to name a bind address (the rule Kamal's unreleased `main` adopted): `"<address>:<host port>:<container port>"` with an IPv4 or bracketed IPv6 address, optionally ending in `/tcp`, `/udp` or `/sctp` (e.g. `"10.0.0.20:5432:5432"` or `"127.0.0.1:5432:5432"`). A bare `port: 5432` or `"5432:5432"` now fails validation with a message naming the accessory; before, it went to `docker run --publish` as written and published on a random host port or on every interface. The published value is also quoted like the other docker arguments (#4067)
- Migrations: an empty `default` on a column that is not string-like (`bigInteger`, `binary`, `uniqueidentifier`, `integer`, `decimal`, `float`, `date`…) now means "no default". It renders as `DEFAULT NULL` when the column allows null and as no `DEFAULT` clause on a `NOT NULL` column, in `createTable()`, `addColumn()` and `changeColumn()` alike. Before, `bigInteger` and `uniqueidentifier` produced a bare `DEFAULT` (a syntax error on every database for `NOT NULL` columns, and on PostgreSQL in every case), `binary` produced `DEFAULT ''` (rejected by MySQL), and `DEFAULT NULL NOT NULL` was rejected by MySQL.
- **Breaking:** `findAll(returnAs="structs")` (and `"struct"`) now returns an array of structs, as documented, instead of a struct keyed by row number (`{1: {...}, 2: {...}}`). It serializes to a JSON array, and `ArrayLen()` works on it. Code that indexes rows (`rows[1]`) keeps working. Code that called `StructCount()`, `StructKeyList()` or `StructKeyArray()` on the result, or looped over its keys (`for (key in rows) { rows[key] }`), must switch to `ArrayLen()` and `for (row in rows)`. `findInBatches(returnAs="structs")` batches change the same way. (#4003)
- `findOne(returnAs="struct")` and `findByKey(returnAs="struct")` return the record's struct instead of a `{1: {...}}` wrapper, and `findEach(returnAs="struct")` now calls its callback for each record (it used to process nothing). With no match they return an empty struct, as before, and so does the query builder's `findOne(returnAs="struct")` after `offset()`, including an offset past the end, which used to return `false` (#4003)
- `wheels generate auth --strategy=token` indexes the `apiTokenDigest` column it looks every request's token up by, and its next steps say how to create the first account and how to protect actions. The app's `.ai/auth.md` gains a "Token APIs" section with a seed for the first account and a filter that requires a bearer token.
- `wheels generate migration` still writes a blank migration, but a name that reads like a column change now gets a next step: `AddEmailToUsers` (or `add_email_to_users`) points to `wheels generate property User email:string`, which writes the `addColumn()` for you, and `RemoveXFromY` shows the `removeColumn()` call. Its usage example is now `BackfillUserSlugs`.
- Guide links in framework and CLI messages (the plugin deprecation notices, the `wheels.Test` deprecation, the `/wheels/mcp` deprecation, `wheels upgrade check`, `wheels mcp setup` and the install hints) are built from the running version, so a 4.1 app links to the 4.1 guides instead of always the 4.0 ones. Links never point at a version whose guides haven't been published yet, and fall back to the latest guides for a dev build. (#3931)
- The background-job stale-processing reaper now reaps each job on **the timeout of the worker that claimed it**, not the polling worker's. The claiming worker records its `timeout` in a new `wheels_jobs.claimTimeout` column (added to the table automatically, including to tables created before this change — probed on every `$ensureJobTable`, never a cached flag), and `checkTimeouts` builds each row's grace window from that value. Workers configured with **different `timeout` values can now share a queue** — a short-timeout worker won't reap a job a longer-timeout worker is still legitimately running. A row with no recorded `claimTimeout` (claimed before this change, or on a database where the column can't be added) falls back to the polling worker's timeout, so nothing breaks. Builds on #3888/#3984. ([#3989](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3989))
- Job schedules: a cron step on a single value (`N/S`) now runs from N to the end of the field, as in Vixie cron and cronie. `5/1` in the minute field was minute 5 alone and is now `5-59`. In day-of-week, `N/S` now runs through `7` (Sunday), so `1/2` adds Sunday, and `7/2` (Sunday) is accepted instead of refused as a reversed range. `5/15`, `N-M/S` and `*/S` are unchanged.
- A job enqueued inside a Wheels-managed transaction on another datasource than the job store's (a tenant datasource, or a model with its own `dataSource()`) is now written to the job store when that transaction commits, and dropped if it rolls back. Before, depending on the engine, such a job could outlive a rolled-back transaction (BoxLang), be written to the tenant's database where no worker reads it (RustCFML), or not be written at all (Adobe ColdFusion). `enqueue()` returns `{status: "deferred", deferred: true, persisted: false}` in that case. On the application's own datasource, a job still joins the open transaction.
- `enqueue()`, `enqueueIn()` and `enqueueAt()` throw `Wheels.Job.EnqueueFailed` when the job cannot be written to the job store, instead of returning `persisted: false`, which callers rarely checked, so failed enqueues went unnoticed. One cause is enqueueing inside a raw `transaction {}` block on another datasource (a tenant's), which some engines refuse; inside a Wheels-managed transaction the write now waits for the commit. If an enqueue is best-effort in your app, wrap it in `try` / `catch (Wheels.Job.EnqueueFailed e)`.
- Background-job timestamps (`runAt`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `completedAt`, `failedAt`, `heartbeatAt`, and the host registry's times) are now stored in UTC, read from the database's own clock, so every server that shares the job tables uses one clock: a server whose clock runs ahead or behind no longer reaps a live job early or picks up a delayed job late. The database's UTC time is read at most once a minute per application (about one-second precision); if a read fails the last good reading is kept. Timestamps are built by the database from integers, so servers in time zones with daylight saving are handled across the clock changes. H2 and SQLite use the server's own clock (both are single-host databases). **H2 requires the JVM to run on UTC:** H2 1.4 does timestamp arithmetic in the JVM's time zone. The API stays in local time: `enqueueAt()` takes a local date/time, and `JobRunner.status()` and the queue stats report local times. **Upgrading:** on SQL Server, MySQL, PostgreSQL, CockroachDB, Oracle and H2, rows written before this release hold the server's local time, so on servers that don't run in UTC either upgrade once the queue is empty (no pending or processing jobs; otherwise a delayed job runs early or late by the offset, and on a server behind UTC an in-flight job looks stale and is reclaimed), or set `this.convertLocalOffsetMinutes` in the `wheels jobs install` migration to the old servers' offset from UTC to convert existing rows once (in one transaction; running it again changes nothing). SQLite rows and servers already running in UTC need neither.
- The Wheels CLI's runtime is LuCLI 0.6.2.2. `wheels start` and `wheels server run` now listen on `127.0.0.1`, so a dev server is reachable only from your own machine. To reach it from a phone, a VM or another computer, start it with `--host 0.0.0.0`, or set `"bindAddress": "0.0.0.0"` in `lucee.json`; the startup output then warns that the server is reachable from your network. JMX monitoring (`monitoring.enabled`) listens on the same address, and LuCLI refuses to start unauthenticated JMX on a non-loopback address. Clients that resolve `localhost` only to `::1` need `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`
- The Wheels CLI's runtime is LuCLI 0.6.2.3. `wheels <command> --env=<name>` (and `--envfile`) now reach the command instead of being dropped; `wheels start --dry-run` no longer writes a default `lucee.json` and its preview shows `--name`; `wheels --timing cfml '…'` (a root option before a root subcommand) runs the subcommand; REPL history and `settings.json` stay inside the CLI's home instead of leaking into `~/.lucli`; `run <file>.cfm` no longer prints the built-in variables setup; hints name `wheels` rather than `lucli`. Over MCP, `generate type=app` is now refused as intended: LuCLI tells the Wheels module that a call came from an MCP tool (see #3980).
- The stdio MCP server no longer lists the `create` tool (hidden like `new`, so an MCP call to it is refused), and `generate` no longer advertises `app` as a `type` choice. A client that ignores the advertised schema can still send `generate` with `type=app`; refusing that server-side is tracked in #3980. `create`'s advertised type+name schema also never matched the `new` options it forwards (such as `port`). `wheels create app` and `wheels generate app` on the command line are unchanged (#3910)
- `wheels generate model` / `scaffold` now treat columns as **required by default**: an unmarked `name:type` column is created `allowNull=false` and added to `validatesPresenceOf`, so the generated model and migration agree. Mark a column nullable with `name:type:optional` (excluded from `validatesPresenceOf`), or give it a default with `name:type=value` (also excluded — the default fills an absence); the two combine as `name:type=value:optional`. A `belongsTo` foreign-key column is required by default. Previously every column was added to `validatesPresenceOf` even though the migration made it nullable, so a freshly generated model rejected any row that left a blank column. `:optional` uses the word rather than `?` because zsh (the macOS default shell) treats a bare `?` as a glob.
- On MySQL, `t.bigInteger()` now creates a signed `BIGINT`, like every other database, instead of `BIGINT UNSIGNED`, so the column accepts negative values and validates as an integer (it used to bind as a decimal and accept fractions). Existing columns are unchanged, and `changeColumn()` keeps a column's current signedness. `t.bigInteger()` and `t.integer()` take a new `unsigned = true` option (MySQL only; ignored elsewhere) for a foreign key to an existing unsigned id, which MySQL otherwise rejects as incompatible (#4121)
- `wheels new` apps now commit `vendor/`: the generated `.gitignore` no longer ignores it, so a clone, CI run or image build has the framework (`vendor/wheels/`) and every package added with `wheels packages add`. Before, a fresh clone had no framework and no command to restore it. In an existing app, remove the `/vendor/` line from `.gitignore` and commit `vendor/`.
- `config/routes.cfm` in a new app (`wheels new`) declares the root route before `.wildcard()`, with the wildcard last, as the routing docs recommend. Existing apps are unaffected; the root route matches either way.
- `wheels new` (and `wheels create app`) now writes `.mcp.json` and `.opencode.json` in the new app, the files `wheels setup agents` writes, so an AI assistant opening the app finds the Wheels MCP server without a second command. Pass `--no-agents` to skip them. If they can't be written, `wheels new` warns and still creates the app.
- On Oracle, new `t.integer()`, `t.references()` and `id` columns are created as `NUMBER(10)`, new `t.bigInteger()` columns as `NUMBER(19)`, and new `t.boolean()` columns before Oracle 23ai as `NUMBER(1)`, instead of a bare `NUMBER`. They bind and validate as integers, so a new integer column no longer accepts a fractional value. Existing bare `NUMBER` columns keep their current typing, and `changeColumn()` without `precision` keeps a column's current precision, so changing a default or `allowNull` never re-types it (Oracle cannot narrow a populated column); the migrations guide shows how to convert one (#4097)
- **Breaking:** an absolute URL's `http`/`https` scheme (`urlFor(onlyPath=false)`, asset URLs, anything through `$prependUrl`) now honours the client-controlled `X-Forwarded-Proto` header **only** when `set(trustProxyHeaders=true)` is enabled — matching `isSecure()`. In 4.1.x the header set the scheme regardless of the opt-in, so an app not behind a trusted proxy could be induced to emit `https://` absolute URLs from a spoofed header. The real socket TLS state (`SERVER_PORT_SECURE`) still always wins, unconditionally. **Apps behind a trusted TLS-terminating reverse proxy must set `set(trustProxyHeaders=true)`** before upgrading, or their absolute URLs will fall back to `http://`. The one-time deprecation warning added in 4.1.2 ([#3835](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/pull/3835)) is removed. ([#3836](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3836))
- Production error pages (`showErrorInformation` off, the default outside development) now return the correct HTTP status for Wheels errors — `404` for a client-triggerable `Wheels.*NotFound` / `ActionNotAllowed`, `403` for `NotAuthorized` / an invalid CSRF token, `406` for `FormatNotAcceptable` — instead of `500` for every error. The status classification previously ran only on the development (`showErrorInformation` on) rendering path (follows #3075, which fixed the same mapping for development)
- Test runs no longer use the app's primary datasource through a project `tests/runner.cfm`: a runner of your own, including one copied from an older Wheels release, now gets the same `<datasource>_test` requirement as the built-in runner. The run uses `<datasource>_test`, or is refused unless `useTestDB=false` (`wheels test --no-test-db`) asks for the primary datasource.
- A date passed to the query builder (`where()`, `whereBetween()`, `whereIn()`) or a dynamic finder (`findOneByPublishedAt(date)`) now compares as a date on SQLite. It used to reach the SQL as its CFML literal (`{ts '...'}`), which never matched a stored date, so the query silently returned no rows. Other databases already parsed that literal; they now receive the plain date text too.
- The starter app's `public/Application.cfc` is synced with the app template, picking up the template's current request, error-page and session-cookie handling and the jBCrypt load path.
- The `wheels-starter-app` release zip now includes the framework in `vendor/wheels/` and runs on the Wheels CLI with an embedded SQLite database: unzip, copy `.env.example` to `.env` and set its passwords, then `wheels start`, `wheels migrate latest` and `wheels reload`. Under CommandBox (`box server start`) it still uses H2, with no `box install` step.
- A test run that switches the datasource in the live application scope, because the app's `public/Application.cfc` lacks the `vendor/wheels/events/testcontext.cfm` include (or `WHEELS_ENV` isn't development or testing), now logs a warning to `wheels.log` once per run: until the run ends, other requests to the app read and write the test database. The warning names the fix, and the `wheels upgrade check` advisory for the missing include now says the same
- `wheels upgrade apply` now parks the old framework at `.wheels/backups/wheels.bak-<timestamp>/` in the project instead of `vendor/wheels.bak-<timestamp>/`. Inside `vendor/`, the backup was loaded as a package and logged a skipped-package error on every boot, and it was committed along with `vendor/`. The backups directory carries its own `.gitignore`. The output names the new location.
- `wheels upgrade check` names the file it checked when it reports missing code, for example `public/Application.cfc (no occurrences found)`, instead of a bare `(no occurrences found)`.
- A failed `verifies()` that declares no `handler` and no redirect now ends the request with **HTTP 400** instead of a silent `200` with an empty `text/html` body — the declared precondition was not met. A JSON-format request receives a `{"error":"Verification failed"}` body; any other format receives an empty body (like a bare status). Verifications that declare a `handler` or redirect arguments are unchanged.

### Removed

- The "Convert dot to format parameter" rule is removed from the shipped `urlrewrite.xml` files (the app, the `wheels new` template and the examples). Its regex had doubled backslashes, so it never matched a normal URL; format suffixes such as `/posts.json` keep working, because the router reads them from the path. Existing apps can delete the rule from their own `public/urlrewrite.xml` (#4132)

### Performance

- The RustCFML engine adapter now pins `stringIndexIsLinear()` to false explicitly, so the WHERE-clause literal masker always takes the linear char-array path on RustCFML. Previously this relied on the Base adapter's `java.lang.String` capability probe failing there; because RustCFML already shims part of the `java.*` surface, a future build that shimmed `java.lang.String` would have flipped the probe and silently reverted RustCFML to the O(n²) index scan. The explicit override removes that risk (follow-up to #3903)
- Binding a long string value in a `where` string, a finder or the query builder no longer slows down sharply with the value's length: the WHERE literal scanner now jumps between quotes instead of reading one character at a time. On RustCFML a long value is linear again; a long run of doubled quotes or a very large IN list is still super-linear there because of the engine's own string indexing (#3903)
- The WHERE-clause literal scanner (`$maskWhereLiterals`, run by every dynamic finder, key finder, chainable query-builder call and uniqueness validation) is now linear on every engine. It previously walked the string one character at a time with `Mid()`, which costs O(index) on RustCFML, so a long bound value — or one with many quotes, or a large `IN` list — was O(n²): a 30,000-character value took ~620ms and 100,000 characters ~22s. It now reads the string once into a character array and walks that with O(1) indexing on the engines whose `REMatch` round-trips the input faithfully (Lucee, Adobe, RustCFML), and falls back to a single-pass `Find()`-jump scan on engines where it does not (BoxLang drops whitespace matches). The character-array path builds its short ODBC-date lookahead window inline rather than passing the whole array to a helper, because Adobe CF copies an array argument by value on every call, which would reintroduce O(n²) there. Such values now bind in linear time on every supported engine (#3903)
- Creating a controller instance, which happens on every request, no longer mixes the framework's controller and view methods in one at a time. Each controller class works out once which methods to add and which `super<name>` aliases to create, and every later instance applies that result with two `StructAppend` calls. Measured per instance in the same process: 4.8 ms → 1.4 ms on RustCFML (the CI-pinned v0.693.0), 2.3 ms → 0.9 ms on Adobe ColdFusion 2023, 1.7 ms → 0.8 ms on Adobe ColdFusion 2025, 0.58 ms → 0.33 ms on Lucee 7 and 4.7 ms → 3.6 ms on BoxLang. Development keeps the old per-method path, so controller methods edited there still take effect without a reload; the new `cacheControllerIntegration` setting controls it (#4149)
- `pluralize()` and `singularize()` cache their results per application, so a repeated word no longer rebuilds the irregular and uncountable lists and the rule table, or runs the rule regexes. Measured per call: 86 µs → 5 µs on RustCFML, about 300 µs → 10 µs on Adobe ColdFusion 2023 / 2025, 152 µs → 12 µs on BoxLang and 25 µs → 5 µs on Lucee 7. The cache is cleared when `set()` changes `uncountables` or `irregulars`, notices a word added to or removed from those settings directly (change an existing irregular through `set()`), is skipped when the current tenant overrides them, keeps a case variant of a word separate, caches only words of up to 100 characters and holds at most 2,000 entries (#4150)
- `linkTo()` and `URLFor()` do less work per call on JVM engines (Lucee, Adobe, BoxLang): `URLFor` skips a redundant argument-normalisation pass when `linkTo` already resolved the arguments, route-variable substitution uses literal string operations instead of a regular expression per variable, and `$tag` resolves the HTML-attribute-encoding setting once per tag rather than once per attribute. The generated output is byte-for-byte unchanged. ([#4151](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4151))
- Creating any model object or controller no longer calls `GetMetadata(this)` while the object is being constructed. The memo of include-injected functions to promote to `this` always ended up under one entry (`wheels.Global`) on every supported engine, so it now uses that key directly. On RustCFML the call cost about 225 µs per object; model `new()` went from 1.30 ms to 1.13 ms there, and from 0.31 ms to 0.25 ms on Lucee 7 (#4174)
- An app without plugins no longer pays for plugin mixins on every model and controller instance: `$initializeMixins` now returns early when the component's class has no mixins, instead of copying the whole `variables` scope into `variables.core` for nothing. `new()` and each row of a `returnAs="objects"` finder are about 12% faster on Lucee 7 and 8% faster on RustCFML (#4382)
- Route matching scans only the routes that can match a request: those allowing its HTTP method whose first two path segments match the request's, plus routes keyed less specifically, in declaration order. Which route matches is unchanged. On RustCFML, whose regex cache holds 256 patterns before v0.711 and 1,024 from v0.711, this also keeps an app with more routes than that from recompiling a regex per route on every dynamic-route request.

### Fixed

- A bare `true` or `false` in a finder `where` string (`where="isActive = true"`) is now bound as `1` / `0` instead of failing with "cfqueryparam cannot be determined", and the query builder and dynamic finders (`where("isActive", true)`, `findAllByIsActive(true)`) now work against native boolean columns such as PostgreSQL's `boolean` (#3896)
- An IN list longer than 1000 values (`whereIn()`, `whereNotIn()` or a hand-written `IN (...)`) now runs on Oracle 19c and earlier, which accept at most 1000 values per list (`ORA-01795`): Wheels splits it into parenthesised groups, OR-joined for `IN` and AND-joined for `NOT IN`, with every value still bound. On SQL Server, which binds at most 2100 parameters per request (2097 once the driver has taken its own), a query that would bind more than 2097 now fails before it runs with `Wheels.TooManyParameters`, naming the limit and the count, instead of the driver's "too many parameters" error (#3906)
- A long integer `IN` list (`whereIn("id", keys)` or `where="id IN (1,2,...)"` with a few thousand values) no longer throws `java.lang.StackOverflowError` while the `where` string is parsed (#3907)
- On Oracle, comparing a date or timestamp column with a value written into the SQL text (`parameterize=false`), such as `where="createdAt > {ts '2000-01-01 00:00:00'}"` or `where="createdAt > '2000-01-01'"`, now returns the matching rows: an ISO value is written as an ANSI `TIMESTAMP '...'` / `DATE '...'` literal, which reads the same under any session date format, instead of a quoted string that fails with `ORA-01843` (#4084)
- A nested-properties struct key outside the range of a SMALLINT or TINYINT primary key (for example `40000` for a SQL Server SMALLINT key) is now treated as a new row, as it already was for INTEGER keys, instead of being looked up with `findByKey()` and rejected at bind time on Adobe ColdFusion. Each adapter gives the range for its column types: SQL Server's TINYINT is 0 to 255, and MySQL covers signed and UNSIGNED columns (#4087)
- On SQLite, `t.date()`, `t.datetime()`, `t.time()` and `t.timestamp()` now declare `DATE` / `DATETIME` / `TIME` columns instead of `TEXT`, and the model validates columns with those declared types as dates. Automatic validations used to see a string and accept a value like `"not a date"`. Values are still stored as ISO-8601 text. A declared `DATE` or `TIME` column now binds as text too, instead of being stored by the driver as epoch milliseconds. Existing `TEXT` date columns are unchanged and keep the column-name check; convert one with `changeColumn(columnType="datetime")`. A column declared `DATE` or `TIME` outside the migrator may hold old epoch-millisecond values: convert them first, as the 4.1 to 4.2 upgrade guide shows (#4093)
- The `channel` `DatabaseAdapter.poll()` no longer returns events out of publish order when two events share a `createdAt` timestamp — common on SQL Server, MySQL, and Oracle, whose datetime columns round to coarse ticks, and possible anywhere on a same-millisecond publish. Events now carry a database-native monotonic sequence (SQLite uses its built-in `rowid`; an unrecognised database falls back to `createdAt` + `id`), `poll()` orders by it, and `lastEventId` resumes strictly after the referenced event — which also stops a same-tick event published before it from being re-delivered, and falls back to the `since` window when the referenced event has already been cleaned up. Existing `wheels_events` tables gain the column automatically (#4099)
- The Postgres/CockroachDB readiness probe for the `wheels_events` monotonic `seq` column now scopes its `information_schema.columns` lookup to the current schema (`AND table_schema = current_schema()`), so a same-named `wheels_events` table in another schema of the same database can no longer make the probe read the wrong table's column metadata and misreport readiness (follow-up to #4106)
- `wheels <command> --help` prints only the command's summary line and its `Usage:` / `Examples:` sections. Internal notes no longer appear in the help for `reload`, `map`, `upgrade` and `packages`, `wheels coverage --help` and `wheels engines --help` print a one-line summary, and Usage/Examples lines are indented.
- A `DECIMAL` / `NUMERIC` value with more than 15 significant digits is now stored and matched in a `where` exactly on PostgreSQL, SQL Server, MySQL and CockroachDB under Lucee and BoxLang, which bound it through a double and so wrote or compared a different number; such a value now binds in each database's exact form, and values with up to 15 significant digits keep their existing bind (#4172)
- `pluralize()` no longer throws `Index -1 out of bounds` on BoxLang for words ending in `-ex`, `-lf` or `-rf` (`vertex`, `index`, `half`, `shelf`, `wolf`), including model names that derive their table name from them. Output on Lucee and Adobe ColdFusion is unchanged (#4185)
- Fixed `$testClient()` and `BrowserTest` base-URL auto-detection so a loopback test request reaches the running server when the host port is mapped to a different internal port (e.g. inside a container). When a mapping is detected the base URL now uses the server's local listener — its scheme and port, via the loopback address — and is byte-for-byte unchanged otherwise. Explicit `testClientBaseUrl` / `browserTestBaseUrl` overrides still take precedence. ([#4195](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4195))
- `withAdvisoryLock()` now keeps a second caller out on Lucee and BoxLang with MySQL or PostgreSQL. The database lock belongs to the pooled session that took it, and a second caller in the same application could borrow that idle session, take the lock too, and leave it held after both finished. Callers in one application now wait on an application-server lock first, and the wait for the database lock gets only the time left of `timeout`, so the whole call waits about `timeout` at most. The release is verified against the session recorded when the lock was taken: retried for up to 5 seconds when it ran on another session, then reported with `Wheels.AdvisoryLockReleaseFailed` naming the lock. Another server taking the lock once ours is gone is not reported as a failure (#4197)
- `wheels packages` help and messages no longer say `wheels packages install` doesn't work. `install` is an alias of `add`, and the help now says so; `add` stays the documented verb.
- On SQL Server, `withAdvisoryLock()` works outside a transaction. It failed with "The statement or function must be executed in the context of a user transaction", because `sp_getapplock` took the lock for a transaction; the lock now belongs to the database session, as on MySQL and PostgreSQL, with the same check that the release freed it (#4220)
- `wheels docs fetch` gives the bundle's checksum download 30 s instead of 300 s (the bundle keeps 300 s), and the CLI's server-detection commands (`lsof`, `netstat`) are stopped after 15 s instead of waiting indefinitely (#4232)
- On SQL Server, a condition on a `TIME` column works on every engine. It errored on Lucee ("The data types time and datetime are incompatible") and BoxLang, and on Adobe ColdFusion it dropped the value's fraction of a second, so only whole-second values matched. A long time list past SQL Server's parameter limit now runs too, split like a long date list (#4327)
- An object whose row is soft-deleted behaves consistently (#4372):
  - After a soft `delete()`, the object's `deletedAt` holds the timestamp written. It goes back to its earlier value if the delete is vetoed or rolled back, so a retry works.
  - A second soft `delete()`, or one from a stale copy of a row soft-deleted elsewhere, returns `false` and changes nothing. It no longer rewrites `deletedAt`, and it no longer soft-deletes the dependents of a row it didn't delete.
  - `reload()` reloads a soft-deleted row instead of blanking every property.
- `order="table.column"` (and `order="table.column DESC"`) is now quoted the same way as `order="column"`, so a table or column whose name is a reserved word works in the qualified form too. The table and column are matched, case-insensitively, against the model and its included associations and written under their real names; a qualifier that isn't one of those tables, such as an alias, is still passed through as written (#4374).
- `wheels upgrade check` dates the `renderPage()` / `renderPageToString()` rename correctly: it happened in 2.0 (to `renderView()` and `renderView(returnAs="string")`), not 4.0. It also now flags a route that targets an action named `isSafeRedirectUrl`, which 4.2 made a framework helper: a request for that action gets `Wheels.ActionNotAllowed` (#4376).
- `wheels migrate diff` on SQLite no longer reports DATETIME, DATE and TIME columns as changed to `string`, which made `--write` produce a migration converting them to strings (#4405)
- The all-models `wheels migrate diff` covers every model file, not only the models a request had already loaded (#4406)
- `wheels migrate diff --write` names its migration with the same 14-digit version as `wheels generate migration`, moving on a second while that version is taken (#4419)
- `wheels db status` and `wheels db version --detailed` show when each migration was applied. The migrator stored the time, but the status report always left it blank, so the CLI printed `-` / `applied unknown` (#4407)
- `wheels test --core` now reports the runner's own error (for example a missing core-test datasource) instead of "no test bundles ran for this scope", and the `--db`-on-app-suite warning names the real `--test-db` flag instead of a non-existent `--useTestDB` (#4408)
- `wheels browser <unknown>`, `wheels browser test` without Playwright installed, `wheels setup agents` outside a project, and `wheels generate snippets <unknown>` now exit non-zero instead of printing a refusal and exiting 0, so scripts and CI can detect the failure (#4409)
- The console's `/routes` command no longer fails with a syntax error; it lists routes as `pattern -> controller#action` (#4410)
- `wheels start` no longer changes `lucee.json` when it has nothing to start. Starting a server that is already running only reports it; it used to move the shutdown port on every repeat. `--dry-run` passes `--port` to LuCLI's dry run instead of writing it, and skips the registry clean-up, JDBC bundles, rewrite config and start token. `-p N` / `-p=N` work like `--port=N`, so the shutdown port is moved off the chosen port. `--dry-run` with `--engine=rustcfml` is refused, where it used to start the server (#4411)
- `wheels start --env=<name>` / `-e <name>` use the `lucee.json` environment profile, as `--environment=<name>` already did. LuCLI takes `--env` / `-e` as its own root option, so `wheels start` now forwards the value (#4412)
- `wheels new --setup-h2` no longer leaves SQLite datasource entries in `lucee.json`; the embedded-database choice in `config/app.cfm` and `lucee.json` now agree (#4413)
- `wheels generate controller <Name>` with no actions now writes an empty controller instead of an `index()` stub with no matching view, which 500s at runtime (#4414)
- `wheels deploy build push` honours `builder.arch` (`--platform`, default `linux/amd64`, so an image built on an arm64 workstation runs on amd64 hosts), `builder.args` (`--build-arg` per entry) and `builder.remote` (builds on the service's buildx builder, which `build create` now creates on that endpoint). The image is labelled `service=<service>`. `builder.arch` takes a list or a comma-separated string, and an entry that isn't a plain platform name (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `/`, `-`) fails with `Wheels.Deploy.InvalidInput` (#4415)
- `wheels deploy app logs` without `--container` tails each role's newest running container instead of emitting a `docker logs` with no container. `wheels deploy prune images` removes every unused image of the service (`--all`), not only dangling ones, matched by the label `build push` now sets; images built before this release carry no label (#4416)
- `wheels deploy` argument handling (#4417):
  - A space-separated flag value works like the `=` form: `--release v8` no longer reads `v8` as the subcommand, and `init --service myapp2` no longer falls back to the directory name. When several space-form values can't be told apart, or a value flag has no value, the command asks for `--flag=value` instead of guessing.
  - An unknown `--role` fails with `DeployAppCli.UnknownRole` and names the roles in `deploy.yml`, instead of doing nothing and exiting 0.
  - `deploy config` prints `ssh.port` and `proxy.app_port` as whole numbers (`22`, not `22.0`).
  - `rollback` without a version and `exec` / `server exec` without a command throw the typed `DeployMainCli.MissingVersion` / `DeployServerCli.MissingCommand`.
- CLI help and hints match the commands. `wheels coverage --help` lists `--top` and `--[no-]test-db`. `wheels destroy --help` says `--force` is what makes it delete (without it, destroy only lists). `wheels new --help` gives the lowercased app name as the datasource default. The `wheels packages` synopsis shows `remove <name> --yes`. The seed-data snippet hint says to move the files into `app/db/` before running `wheels seed` (#4418)
- The per-request finder cache (`cacheQueriesDuringRequest`) is now cleared across every model on any ORM write and on a transaction rollback, so a cached `include=` query no longer returns a joined model's pre-write columns — or a rolled-back transaction's phantom rows — for the rest of the request (#4429)
- On RustCFML, with `flashStorage="cookie"`, a flash message stored with `flashInsert()` can be read with `flash()` in the same request. RustCFML reads the flash cookie back as the attribute struct it was assigned, rather than its value; the flash reader now uses the value. On every engine, an empty flash cookie now reads as an empty flash instead of one with an empty `action` entry (#4433)
- The all-models `wheels migrate diff` also covers models in subfolders of the model path, named in dot notation (`admin.User`) as `model()` takes them (#4434)
- `wheels generate snippets seed-data` writes `app/db/seeds.cfm` and `app/db/seeds/development.cfm`, where `wheels seed` reads them, instead of `app/snippets/`; existing seed files are kept unless `--force` is given (#4434)
- The dependency injector no longer throws a spurious `Wheels.DI.CircularDependency` when two of a request's `cfthread`s resolve the same transient (or request-scoped) service at the same time; the circular-dependency guard is now keyed per thread while keeping its per-request scope (#4448)
- A `.cfm` page in a subfolder of `public/` no longer fails with `can't find component [wheels.events.EventMethods]`. `public/Application.cfc` built its mappings, and `config/app.cfm` its SQLite and H2 paths, with `expandPath("../...")`, which resolves against the requested page's directory. The `wheels new` template now builds them from `this.wheels.projectRoot`, derived from `Application.cfc`'s own path. Existing apps make the same edit: see the 4.1 to 4.2 upgrade guide (#4470)
