- `Job.processQueue()` now runs jobs through the job worker, so the in-app path the guide documents is as safe as `wheels jobs work`. Every call first reaps jobs a dead worker left in `processing`, which it never did before. Each job is claimed with its own class's timeout recorded as `claimTimeout`, so a server polling with a shorter timeout no longer reaps it while it runs. Jobs still run with their own timeout, as before. The new optional `processQueue(timeout = n)` caps it. `queue` accepts a comma-delimited list. ([#4475](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4475))
- A background job whose claim was reaped as stale can no longer overwrite the attempt that replaced it. Every claim now writes a fresh `wheels_jobs.claimToken` (and the claiming host to `claimedBy`, both added to existing tables automatically), and a worker's completion, retry or failure applies only while the row still carries its own token. Before, a stalled worker that finished after its job was requeued and claimed again matched the new attempt's `processing` row: it marked the job `completed` (or requeued it) while the second attempt was still running, and that attempt's own result then matched nothing. A fenced result is discarded and logged as `Wheels.Job.Fenced`. `JobWorker.processNext()` returns `fenced: true` and `processQueue()` reports a `fenced` count. Covers both `JobWorker` and `processQueue()`. Fencing holds once every worker sharing the table runs this version. ([#4476](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4476))
- When the `wheels_jobs.uniqueKey` upgrade can't finish, the warning and the `Wheels.Job.UniqueKeyUnavailable` error now say which step failed (adding the column, the backfill, or building the unique index) and give the manual fix for the database in use. On SQL Server below compatibility level 100, where the filtered unique index can't be built, they name the level and the real options: raise it, or create a plain unique index once every server runs 4.2 or later. Previously both messages advised the filtered index, which fails the same way. ([#4489](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4489))
- On RustCFML, converting epoch timestamps to app-local time no longer goes wrong by the host's UTC offset when the engine runs outside UTC. The jobs clock (`enqueueAt()`, `JobRunner.status()`, queue stats) and other epoch-to-date reads now count from the epoch instant instead of local midnight on 1970-01-01. JVM engines were not affected (#4525).
- On Adobe ColdFusion, a model write (`create()`, `save()`, `update()`, `delete()`) inside a raw `transaction {}` block no longer fails with "Nested cftransaction tag should specify same isolation level as the parent". When Adobe rejects the default `read_committed` level of a nested model transaction, the write now joins the outer block at its isolation level. An `isolation` you pass yourself is never dropped: if it differs from the outer block's, the call throws `Wheels.TransactionIsolationMismatch` explaining the fix (#4045)
- On Adobe ColdFusion, Wheels now detects every model write inside a raw `transaction {}` block, not only one that is the block's first database work. `afterCommit`/`afterRollback` are skipped for those writes with the usual warning (instead of `afterCommit` firing for a write the block then rolls back). RustCFML still can't see a raw block (#4068)
- The `AGENTS.md` that `wheels new` ships no longer tells assistants to prefer MCP only "when `.mcp.json` is configured", a file a new app doesn't have. It now says to prefer the Wheels MCP tools when the client exposes them, and how to enable them (`wheels setup agents`, or the config snippet by hand), with the `wheels` CLI until then (#3971)
- `wheels generate api-resource` controllers:
  - answer 400 with a clear message when the body has no `{"<model>": {...}}` wrapper or is not valid JSON, instead of a 500;
  - answer 404 for a key that cannot exist (`/api/products/abc`) instead of a 500;
  - return the index as a JSON array of records instead of query columns;
  - quote their top-level keys, so they are lower-case on every engine (`products`, `product`, `error`) rather than upper-case on Lucee.
  Existing generated controllers are unchanged; regenerate or copy the pattern (#3881)
- `wheels generate api-resource` no longer adds its route inside another generator's marked block in `config/routes.cfm` (such as `wheels generate auth`'s `api` namespace, which `generate auth --force` rewrites); it opens its own `.namespace("api")` block instead, and the route it adds to an existing block is indented inside it without moving that block's `.end()`.
- An association's `joinType` now accepts `left` and `left outer` as aliases of `outer` (all emit `LEFT OUTER JOIN`), and any other unknown value throws `Wheels.InvalidJoinType` when the association is registered. `joinType="left"` used to emit `LEFT JOIN`, which the nested-include grouping didn't recognise, so a nested include under it (`include="classifications(tag)"`) was silently dropped and the query failed with an unknown column. The grouping now throws instead of dropping a nested join it can't place (#3936)
- The `wheels-base-template` download ships the application-developer AI docs (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.ai/README.md`), like the core and starter-app packages, instead of the framework maintainers' `CLAUDE.md`, `.ai/` and `.claude/` commands. Existing apps installed from the 4.x base template can replace `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `.ai/` with the copies from a new `wheels new` app and delete `.claude/`. (#3947)
- A SQLite column declared `BIGINT` (or `INT8`) and an Oracle `NUMBER(11..19, 0)` column (such as `NUMBER(19)`) are now typed `cf_sql_bigint`, so their 64-bit values bind correctly on Adobe ColdFusion, and `migrate diff` no longer reports a spurious `integer` to `biginteger` change for a SQLite `BIGINT` column. Wheels' own `t.bigInteger()` still creates `INTEGER` on SQLite, which stays 32-bit (#4086)
- `wheels browser setup` joins the Playwright jars with the platform's path separator, so the classpath it passes to Java works on Windows (`;`) as well as macOS and Linux (`:`).
- `wheels browser setup` now launches the browser once before it reports ready, and exits non-zero when the install or the launch fails. On a host missing the OS libraries the browser needs (a bare Linux install), it prints Playwright's `install-deps` command for this install instead of saying "Browser testing ready." A launch that doesn't finish within 120 seconds is stopped, along with everything it started.
- `insertAll()` and `upsertAll()` no longer fail on SQL Server for batches whose rows × columns exceed the parameter limit. They used to send 1000 rows per statement whatever the column count, so a 5-column write of more than about 400 rows threw `Wheels.TooManyParameters` before writing anything; batches are now sized to fit (at least one row per statement)
- `save(callbacks=false)` and `delete(callbacks=false)` now skip `afterCommit` and `afterRollback` too, like every other callback. They used to queue them. With callbacks on, a save that changes nothing still queues `afterCommit`
- `t.char()` now creates a `CHAR` column on SQLite, H2, MySQL and Oracle. Those adapters had no `char` type mapping, so the column was created with no type, and on SQLite every model on the table then failed to load (#4092)
- The background-job table upgrade no longer re-fires the `wheels_jobs.claimTimeout` `ALTER` on every poll when the database user lacks `ALTER` privilege. `wheels jobs work` builds a fresh `JobWorker` each poll, so a blocked `ALTER` was retried every cycle (log noise plus a failed statement each time). A failed `ALTER` is now recorded in an application-scoped, **time-bounded** memo and the retry backs off for a window (re-probed and re-attempted once it elapses, so a later `GRANT` or manual `ALTER` is still picked up) — never a permanent "gave up" flag. The column-existence probe still runs every call, and a missing/`NULL` `claimTimeout` continues to fall back to the polling worker's timeout. ([#4071](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4071))
- `clearChangeInformation(property = "x")` no longer resets change tracking for every other property. It reset the whole change baseline and refilled only `x`, so every untouched property read as changed, was written by the next `save()`, and over-reported in `savedChanges()` / `changedProperties()`. It now refreshes only the named property's baseline, leaving the rest intact (#4381)
- `wheels upgrade apply` prints its report once; it used to appear twice in a terminal.
- `wheels new --help` (and `wheels help new`) lists the options and examples; it used to print only a one-line summary. A bare `wheels new` prints the same help.
- A source checkout of the CLI no longer prints the raw `@build.version@` token. `wheels version`, `wheels info`, `wheels --help` and `wheels console` show `<monorepo version>-dev` (for example `4.2.0-dev`), the same derivation the framework uses for a checkout, or `0.0.0-dev` outside the monorepo. Stamped release versions are unchanged (#3891)
- `wheels generate` (every type except `app`) and `wheels destroy` now refuse outside a Wheels project with `Wheels.NotAWheelsProject` and a non-zero exit, as `wheels start` does. With no `vendor/wheels` found, the project root fell back to the current directory, so `wheels generate model Foo` in any directory wrote `app/models/Foo.cfc` and a migration there, and `wheels destroy` could delete a stray app file whose name matched (#3909)
- `wheels setup --help` shows the setup command's own help again, instead of the general command list.
- `wheels test` on an app with no specs yet, such as a fresh `wheels new` app, now prints a "No specs yet" notice with a hint (`wheels generate test model <Name>`) and exits 0. Before, it failed with "no test bundles ran for this scope". This applies only to the default scope: an explicit `--filter`, `--directory` or path that runs no bundles still fails, and so does any run with failures, errors, a rejected scope or specs that failed to load (#3921).
- `wheels test --help` (and `--help` for any command that declares its options) now lists every option, with its accepted values and default, followed by examples for running one folder and one spec file. Before, it printed a single line. The list is rendered from the same declaration the parser and the MCP tool schema use (#3962).
- The app test runner now clears Lucee's compiled-page pool before every run, not only under `wheels coverage`. A spec edited just before `wheels test` is no longer run in its previous version while Lucee's background template check catches up (#3962).
- When no server is running, `wheels test` (and the MCP `test` tool) now says it needs the project server and to start it with `wheels start`. It suggests `bash tools/test-local.sh` only inside the Wheels framework repository, the one place that script exists (#3972).
- `wheels test` accepts a file or folder path as its scope: `tests/specs/models`, `tests/specs/models/BookSpec.cfc`, a trailing slash, a leading `./`, a path relative to `tests/specs`, or `BookSpec.cfc`. Each resolves to the dotted scope the runner takes.
- `wheels test` refuses a scope that names no folder or spec file, or that the runner would reject (including the MCP `test` tool's `directory`). It prints one message naming the scope and the accepted forms, before anything runs. Previously it ran the full suite and then reported that no test bundles ran (#3963, #3893).
- The `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` that `wheels new` writes now show `wheels test tests.specs.<area>` and `--filter=<SpecName>`.
- An unknown command (`wheels status`, `wheels bogus`) now prints `Unknown command: <name>. Run 'wheels --help' for the commands.`, with a "did you mean" for near misses (`wheels generat` suggests `wheels generate`), and exits non-zero with `Wheels.UnknownCommand`. It used to print Lucee's raw "Component [modules.wheels.Module] has no function with name [status]" (#3890)
- On CockroachDB, `t.string()` now creates a `VARCHAR(n)` column instead of `STRING(n)`. The two are the same type in CockroachDB, but a `STRING(n)` column reports no length over JDBC, so automatic validations never enforced its limit; `VARCHAR(n)` reports it. Columns created by earlier versions keep the old behaviour until they are re-declared, for example `changeColumn(table = "users", columnName = "name", columnType = "string", limit = 100)` with the column's existing limit (#4101)
- The migration templates (`wheels generate migration`, the development migrator, new apps' `app/snippets/dbmigrate/`) no longer show `default=''` / `default=""`, which the migrator has rejected on string, text and char columns since 4.1.0. The example header in `create-table` and `change-table`, and the `changeColumn` / `addColumn` calls in `change-column` and `remove-column`, now omit the default.
- `wheels console`: bare `exit` and `quit` now end the session like `/exit`. After a failed expression they exit non-zero (`Wheels.ConsoleFailed`), as end of input does, so a piped `bad expr; exit` is still reported as a failure. They used to be evaluated as CFML and fail with "variable [EXIT] doesn't exist", so a piped session that ended in `exit` exited 1 (#3892)
- Controllers include the application's global helper file (`app/views/helpers.cfm`) when each controller object is set up, and before `config()` runs, instead of in `wheels.Controller`'s pseudo-constructor. Every controller now gets the global helpers on engines that reuse a class's pseudo-constructor include across instances (RustCFML v0.721.0 and later), including a controller first created during application start-up. Global helpers still come before a controller's own helpers, so a controller helper can override one. A missing global helper file is skipped, and whether it exists is cached like the controller helper files when `cacheFileChecking` is on.
- The framework test suite (`/wheels/core/tests`) runs in an app made with `wheels new`. `coreTestDataSourceName` used to default to a datasource named after the app's folder (`public`) before `config/settings.cfm` set `dataSourceName`, so the run failed with `Datasource [public] doesn't exist`. It now defaults to the `dataSourceName` the settings set, so the run uses `<datasource>_test`. A `coreTestDataSourceName` that names a datasource that does not exist now gets a 409 explaining how to fix it (set `coreTestDataSourceName`, create the datasource, or pass `?db=`) instead of the engine's error.
- A `coreTestDataSourceName` that differs from `dataSourceName` only in letter case is treated as the app's primary datasource, so the framework suite still uses `<datasource>_test` (datasource names are case-insensitive).
- The 409 responses of the test runners have lowercase keys (`success`, `error`, `message`, `datasource`, `candidate`); on Lucee they were serialized in uppercase.
- With `csrfStore="cookie"`, a valid form post was occasionally refused with 403 (about 3% of new CSRF cookies on Lucee 7.0.1 behind Tomcat). That engine reads a cookie value whose only encoded character is `%2B` back with the `+` as a space, which made the base64 ciphertext undecryptable. The cookie now carries base64url (`-`/`_`, no padding), which has nothing to encode; cookies issued before, including ones already read with a space, still decrypt
- On RustCFML, a request that sets the cookie-store CSRF cookie and reads the token back in the same request (for example a page rendering `csrfMetaTags()`) no longer fails with an HTTP 500. RustCFML reads a cookie assigned as `{value, httpOnly, ...}` back as that struct rather than its value; the token reader now uses the value
- A `csrfStore="cookie"` app on Adobe CF returned HTTP 500 the first time it generated an authenticity token: the CSRF cookie was set with `encodeValue` and `preserveCase` of `""` (their defaults), which Adobe rejects for these boolean attributes. They are now left out unless configured.
- `wheels info` and the `wheels test` preamble now read a single-quoted datasource in `config/settings.cfm` (`set(dataSourceName='name')` and `env('WHEELS_DATASOURCE', 'name')`), which CFML accepts; only double quotes were read (#3952)
- `delete(callbacks=false)` now also skips the callbacks of the dependent records it deletes or removes through `hasMany()` / `hasOne()` `dependent=`, including their `afterFind` / `afterInitialization` and transaction callbacks; a `hasOne` with `dependent="deleteAll"` or `"removeAll"` (which loads the record) is covered too. `deleteAll(instantiate=true)`, `deleteOne()` and `updateOne()` with `callbacks=false` no longer run `afterFind` / `afterInitialization` on the records they load, like `updateAll(instantiate=true)`. An invalid `dependent` value on `hasMany()` or `hasOne()` now throws `Wheels.InvalidArgument` when the association is declared, instead of at the first delete
- `wheels deploy` now applies the `boot:` block (Kamal's rolling boot) instead of only validating it: with `boot:` present, each role's hosts boot in batches of `limit` (a count, or a percentage of the role's hosts) with `wait` seconds between batches, and `--dry-run` shows where each pause falls. Configs without `boot:` deploy as before (#3925)
- `wheels deploy` follow-ups: the `deploy init` Dockerfile no longer hard-codes `WHEELS_DATASOURCE=app` (the app's own datasource name applies) and links the current guides; the local `db/*.sqlite` files are no longer copied into the image (the image gets an empty `db/`, and the generated `.dockerignore` excludes SQLite files), and `deploy init` tells a SQLite app that production starts with an empty database and how to run migrations on boot; `deploy registry login` without `registry.username` fails with a clear error instead of running `docker login -u ''`; a missing or empty `config/deploy.yml` says so (and points at `wheels deploy init`) instead of a raw file error; unknown `proxy.*` keys and a non-boolean `proxy.ssl` are rejected; `deploy app details` / `app live` / `app maintenance` no longer require `--release`, and the verbs that do ask for `--release`; `deploy config` prints proxy, env (secret names only), accessories, ssh and builder too, and shows a `registry.password` entry only when it names a key in `.kamal/secrets`; and the deploy guides no longer call `setup` an alias of `deploy` (#3914)
- `wheels deploy` now refuses an app or accessory volume whose host path starts with `~` or `$` (which a shell used to expand, but which Docker would take literally now that volume arguments are quoted) with a message asking for an absolute path, and `wheels deploy init` shows a SQLite app the `volumes:` entry that keeps its database on the host across redeploys. `wheels deploy config` also prints `volumes` (#4018)
- `wheels docs fetch` now mounts an already-installed docs bundle into the current app's `public/wheels-docs` (a second app no longer needs `--force`), unpacks the bundle with Java instead of a shell `unzip` (so a CLI home path with a space works, and Windows needs no `unzip`), refuses archive entries that point outside the docs directory, and honours `--offline` / `WHEELS_OFFLINE=1` by refusing the download while still mounting an installed bundle (#3894, #3899). Thanks @PandaHUN777 (#3915) for the offline-mode fix.
- Generated apps no longer prepend blank lines to every response. The per-request and lifecycle event templates (`app/events/onrequeststart.cfm`, `onrequestend.cfm`, `onsessionstart.cfm` and siblings) are `$include`d straight into the response buffer, where a bare `cfscript` block and a trailing newline leaked whitespace before the action's output — enough to make `renderText()` of an XML document ill-formed, since the `<?xml …?>` declaration must be the first byte. The templates are now wrapped in `cfsilent` with no trailing newline (the `wheels new` generator templates, the demo app, and the published `starter-app` example). `cfsilent` suppresses only output, not execution, so event code — including `cfheader` — still runs; any output a hook deliberately produces must go through the view or `renderText()` rather than being written directly. **Existing apps:** these files are app-owned (`app/events/*.cfm`), so a framework upgrade does not touch them — if `renderText()` of XML (or any byte-exact response) shows leading blank lines, wrap each `app/events/*.cfm` placeholder in `<cfsilent>…</cfsilent>` with no trailing newline, or delete the empty placeholders you don't use. ([#3882](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3882))
- `wheels generate auth --strategy=token` and `--strategy=jwt`: the generated `api/Sessions` controller answers in JSON when the request names no format (no `.json` extension or `Accept` header), instead of an HTTP 500 for a missing view, and its response keys keep their case (`token`, `tokenType`, `expiresIn`, `error`, `revoked`, `message`).
- After `wheels generate scaffold` or `generate api-resource`, the Next steps now say to run `wheels reload` when the project's dev server is already running, instead of `wheels start`. A running server only picks up the new routes after a reload, so following the old hint led to a 404 on the new route and a 500 on its form (#3883). Thanks @kshivam4781 (#3935).
- Guide links in framework and CLI messages now reach the 4.2 guides. A link for a 4.2 project or a `--to=4.2.0` upgrade check resolved to the 4.1 guides, which have no 4.1 to 4.2 upgrade page, because the newest linkable guides version was still 4.1. The spec that guards that version now counts guide trees still in development, so it can't fall behind again
- Cache keys from `$hashedKey()` now keep argument names and the order of array elements and query rows, while still ignoring struct key order. Cached partials, and other cached output keyed on queries or arrays, are no longer reused for inputs that hold the same values in a different order. The unused `normalizeForHash()` engine-adapter method is removed.
- `Job.enqueue()`, `enqueueIn()` and `enqueueAt()` no longer mutate the `data` struct the caller passes. The framework's internal keys (`$wheelsJobTimeout` and, when present, the tenant context) were added to the caller's struct because CFML passes structs by reference; the data is now duplicated before those keys are added. ([#3887](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3887))
- The background-jobs guide (4.1 and 4.2) and the AI jobs reference now describe `maxRetries` as it works: it counts the retries after the first run, so the default `3` means up to four runs. The log line for a job that runs out of retries now reports how many times it ran.
- A job left in `processing` by a crashed or killed worker is now recovered during normal polling. `JobWorker.processNext()` (the loop behind `wheels jobs work`) runs the stale-processing check before claiming, so a job stuck in `processing` is requeued for retry (counting the attempt) or marked failed when retries are exhausted, instead of staying stuck forever. The reap is scoped to the queues the poll serves and only acts once a job has been idle past a grace margin (`timeout + max(60s, timeout)`), so a worker never reaps another worker's live job, and the requeue is an atomic conditional `UPDATE` guarded by the attempt count a claim bumps — two workers polling the same queue cannot double-requeue or double-count the same job. Recovery is at-least-once: workers sharing a queue should configure the same `timeout`. ([#3888](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3888))
- With `jobsAutoCreateTables = false` on PostgreSQL or CockroachDB, a job call made inside a database transaction while the job tables are missing now reports `Wheels.Job.SchemaMissing`, pointing at `wheels jobs install`. Before, the failed table probe aborted the transaction, and the caller saw "current transaction is aborted" instead.
- `wheels jobs work` no longer cuts every job off at 300 seconds: each job now runs with its own `this.timeout`, like `processQueue()`. The new `--job-timeout=<seconds>` option caps it. (#4517)
- The test runner's JSON report keeps its results when a spec errors with an exception that can't be serialized. Each spec's error, failure origin and bundle-level exception are reduced to plain values (type, message, detail, a bounded stack trace and template/line frames) before serializing; before, a JDBC exception on BoxLang sent the serializer into a `StackOverflowError` and the whole run came back with no results. If serialization still fails, the report keeps the totals and each spec's name and status. (#3905)
- On MariaDB 10.2.7 and later, column defaults are read correctly. MariaDB reports them as SQL expressions: a nullable column without a default as the text `NULL`, and a string default as a quoted literal (`'draft'`). Wheels took the text `NULL` as a real default, so `validatesPresenceOf` was skipped for an unset nullable column on create, and string defaults kept their quotes. The MySQL adapter now normalizes them on MariaDB; a literal string default `'NULL'` stays the text `NULL`, and MySQL is unchanged. (#3927)
- Over MCP, `generate` with `type=app` (or its alias `a`) is now refused with `Wheels.InvalidArguments` instead of scaffolding an application into the MCP server's working directory, even for a client that skips the advertised input schema; `wheels generate app <name>` and `wheels generate --type=app <name>` in a terminal keep working. This uses the runtime-owned `__lucliMcpCall` marker a LuCLI build with wheels-dev/LuCLI#17 adds to every MCP `tools/call`; Wheels drops that marker before any command parses its arguments, so it is safe with today's LuCLI too (#3980)
- MCP tool polish:
  - `wheels notes` refuses an annotation marker that isn't a word (a `.` used to match every character);
  - `wheels seed` advertises the `models` and `count` options its own "Pass models=…" message refers to, forwards them to the server, and refuses an unknown `environment` instead of silently running only the main seeds;
  - the `routes` tool now has a description, and `reload`'s is no longer cut off mid-sentence (#3893)
- A middleware registered by component path (`set(middleware=["app.middleware.TokenGate"])` or a route's `middleware=[...]`) no longer needs an `init()`: Wheels calls `init()` only when the component has one. A component with just `handle(request, next)`, which is all `wheels.middleware.MiddlewareInterface` requires, used to fail every request with "has no function with name [init]". An `init()` the component has, including an inherited one, still runs, and an object it returns is still what's used; when it returns nothing or a non-object, the component itself is used.
- What middleware attaches to the request now reaches the controller. The `AuthMiddleware` result is available as `request.auth`, as documented, including the failed result under `allowAnonymous=true`, and the whole middleware context as `request.wheels.middlewareContext`. Before, the controller was built from the request params alone, so a controller behind `AuthMiddleware` could not tell who had authenticated.
- `AuthMiddleware` created without an `authenticator` now uses the one registered in the DI container (what `wheels generate auth` and `enableSession()` register), so the documented `new wheels.middleware.AuthMiddleware(strategies="...")` form no longer throws `Wheels.Auth.NoAuthenticator`.
- The default `AuthMiddleware` 401 body now has lowercase `error` and `status` keys; on Lucee they were serialized as `ERROR` and `STATUS`.
- Migration column functions (`t.string()` and the others, `t.column()`, `t.references()`) read `null`, the option's name before Wheels 3.0, as a deprecated alias of `allowNull` again. Since 3.0 it was ignored, so an older migration's `null=false` created a nullable column. `allowNull` wins when both are passed, and the deprecation is logged once per migration run. `wheels upgrade check` lists migrations that use `null=false` or `null=true`; check `null=false` columns for `NULL` rows before tightening them
- Migrations on Lucee with CockroachDB: a migration step that runs any statement before a schema change (a check query, a data update, the `wheels jobs install` migration's catalog checks) no longer fails with "to use multi-statement transactions involving a schema change under weak isolation levels". Lucee leaves a pooled connection at read committed once a transaction on it has ended, and CockroachDB refuses a schema change after another statement at that level. On CockroachDB the migrator now runs each step's transaction at serializable, CockroachDB's own default. Other databases are unchanged.
- The migration templates under `vendor/wheels/migrator/templates/` no longer show `default=''`, which the migrator has rejected on string, text and char columns since 4.1.0.
- Specs that mock (`createStub()`, `createMock()`, `createEmptyMock()`, `prepareMock()` with `$()`) work in an app made with `wheels new`. MockBox writes its generated stubs to `public/testbox/system/stubs`, which such an app doesn't have, so every mocked method errored with "parent directory … does not exist". `WheelsTest` now creates the directory when it is missing.
- A nested controller (`app/controllers/<package>/X.cfc`) that declares `extends="Controller"` now logs a hint to `wheels.log` to use `extends="app.controllers.Controller"` — a bare `extends` name resolves relative to the controller's own package, so the base controller cannot be found. The original component-not-found exception is preserved unchanged (its stack and cause are intact); the hint is added only to the log, which the error page already points to
- `wheels new` creates what the guides and the framework expect:
  - `config/development/settings.cfm`, `config/testing/settings.cfm`, `config/production/settings.cfm` and `config/maintenance/settings.cfm` again, as commented stubs (the 3.x template shipped them; the 4.x one didn't). The stubs set nothing, so a new app behaves as before.
  - `.env.example`, with the same keys as `.env` and the secrets (reload and Lucee admin passwords) left blank.
  - `app/middleware/`.
  - `app/snippets/dbmigrate/`, which the development migrator UI needs to create a migration.
  - The `lucee.json` SQLite datasource now uses the `#project:path#` placeholder LuCLI resolves; the old `{project}` was never substituted.
  - The legacy plugins README moved to the root `plugins/` folder the framework actually loads plugins from, instead of an `app/plugins/` folder it never reads.
  - New apps no longer ship frozen copies of the generator templates in `app/snippets/`, which kept an app on old templates after a CLI update.
  - Template comments link to the 4.1 guides.
- `wheels new` also skips shutdown ports that another project pins in its `lucee.json` (projects you've started, plus folders next to the new app), even when that project isn't running, and warns when the requested `--port` is one of them.
- `wheels new --port=N` no longer pins the shutdown port to N+1 when another process is already listening there; it writes the first free port above N into `lucee.json` and says so. `wheels start` still moves a pinned shutdown port that is taken later.
- The `onError` fallback in the app's `public/Application.cfc` no longer reports "Wheels failed to initialize" for an error raised once the application is already running (for example a request timeout after startup); it renders and logs a running-app message instead. It also extends the request timeout (via `onErrorRequestTimeout`) before logging, so the `wheels.log` entry the minimal error page points at is written even after a request timeout. **Existing apps:** this lives in the app-owned `public/Application.cfc`, so it is not picked up automatically — copy the updated `onError`, `$renderMinimalError` and `$logStartupFailure` functions from the template (`cli/lucli/templates/app/public/Application.cfc`) into your app's `public/Application.cfc`, replacing the existing versions. ([#3965](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3965))
- On Oracle, a whole number beyond the signed 64-bit range (more than 9,223,372,036,854,775,807, or less than -9,223,372,036,854,775,808) written to or compared against a `NUMBER` column is now bound as text, which Oracle converts exactly. It was clamped (Lucee), wrapped (BoxLang) or rounded through a double, so it could be stored or matched as a different number (#4162)
- On Oracle, whole-number `NUMBER` columns with a precision of 11 or more, including `NUMBER(38)` (Oracle's `INTEGER`, common for identity ids), are now bound as 64-bit integers. They were bound as 32-bit, so an id above 2,147,483,647 was clamped or wrapped and a find, update or delete by it could act on a different row (#4089)
- A lowercase or mixed-case sort direction in an `order` string is now honoured: `order="createdAt desc"` (or `Desc`, or with extra spaces or a tab before it) sorts descending in `findAll()`, paginated finders, `table.column` items and the query builder's `orderBy()`. It used to sort ascending, and a `table.column desc` item threw `Wheels.InvalidOrderClause`
- `wheels packages` now checks a package's `wheelsVersion` range against the app's own `vendor/wheels` version, not the CLI's (falling back to the CLI version outside a project), and a dev CLI build (`0.0.0-dev`) treats every range as compatible, as documented. The manifest cache lives under the CLI home (so `LUCLI_HOME` isolates it), a registry other than the default gets its own `registries/<hash>/` cache, and `--offline` serves expired cached data or says plainly that nothing is cached yet (#3911)
- A `wheels packages update` / `add --force` killed mid-swap no longer leaves the package only as a hidden `vendor/.wheels-pkg-previous-*` backup: the next `wheels packages` command restores it when `vendor/<name>/` is missing or incomplete, drops it when the new copy is in place, and says how to restore one it cannot attribute. A cross-volume install now copies into a hidden sibling and renames it in, so `vendor/<name>/` is never half-written (#3902)
- A polymorphic association without `foreignKey` / `foreignType` now finds its columns on the child's table like other associations do: `<name>id` / `<name>type` when they exist, otherwise `<name>_id` / `<name>_type`, which `t.references(polymorphic=true)` writes when `useUnderscoreReferenceColumns` is on (the `wheels new` default). Before, those apps threw `key [<name>id] doesn't exist`. When both shapes exist, the names without the underscore win
- `Job.processQueue()` processes jobs again on BoxLang with PostgreSQL and CockroachDB. Its candidate query passed the `maxrows` option, which BoxLang's PostgreSQL path rejects ("setLargeMaxRows is not yet implemented"), so every call returned an error and ran nothing. It now bounds the batch in the SQL itself, as `JobWorker` does. ([#4487](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/pull/4487))
- `processRequest()` now restores the request method, transaction mode and the `sendEmail` / `sendFile` deliver flags on every exit, including when the action throws and the spec catches it. Previously it restored them only on the success path, so a caught throw leaked that state into later specs in the same run (reported by a downstream app) (#4350)
- `wheels reload` against a RustCFML server no longer fails every other run. On the first request after a reload restart, the framework sets `request.cgi` before `onRequestStart`, and RustCFML resolves `IsDefined("cgi.<key>")` against `request.cgi`. The `X-Wheels-Reload-Password` header the CLI sends therefore looked absent, and the reload check never saw it. `public/Application.cfc` now falls back to the raw request headers (`GetHttpRequestData()`) when the `cgi` copy is missing. The password stays header-only, and the reload gate, its rate limit and its constant-time comparison are unchanged. Existing apps: copy the updated header-mapping block from the `wheels new` template into your own `public/Application.cfc` (#3913)
- On an app served under a subfolder (`set(subpath=...)` or an IIS application), `?reload=true` now redirects back inside the app: `public/Application.cfc`'s `$buildRedirectUrl()` passes the path through the framework's `$reloadRedirectPath()`, which puts the subfolder back. **Existing apps** must apply the same edit to their own `public/Application.cfc`; the snippet is in the 4.1 → 4.2 upgrade guide (#3948).
- The redirect after a reload (`redirectAfterReload`, on by default in production and maintenance) now collapses a leading run of slashes or backslashes in the request path to a single `/`, and also ignores spaces and control characters in that leading run, so it always goes to a path on the application's own site; a subfolder install still gets its prefix.
- Route model binding now derives the model from the last segment of a namespaced controller, so `binding=true` on `admin.users` loads `User` instead of silently skipping a nonexistent `Admin.user` model. The development "Route Binding Hint" log line names that model too, and stays quiet when the controller has no model to bind (#3940)
- The Linux `.rpm` now unpacks cleanly with `rpm2cpio` on rpm 4.19 (Rocky Linux 10). It is rebuilt with `rpmbuild`, because nfpm wrote an archive-size header that rpm 4.19's `rpm2cpio` rejects (exit 1), which broke `rpm2cpio | cpio` installs in user space. The package contents and dependencies are unchanged (#3975)
- The Linux packages install where the system Java is newer than 21. The `.rpm` now requires Java 21 or newer (`java-21-headless` or `java-headless >= 1:21`), so `dnf install` works on Fedora 44, which ships only Java 25, and the `.deb` accepts OpenJDK 21 or 25, preferring 21. The `wheels` launcher also finds any Java 21 or newer under `/usr/lib/jvm` when `/usr/bin/java` points at an older JDK, and its error now asks for a Java 21 or newer runtime (#4237, #4243)
- RustCFML engine commands: `wheels info` names RustCFML (with its version and URL) while it serves the project instead of always printing Lucee; `--port 8931` (space form) now works for `wheels engines rustcfml start` and is no longer silently dropped by `wheels start`; `wheels start --engine=<unknown>` and `wheels engines <unknown>` / `wheels engines rustcfml <unknown>` exit non-zero instead of booting Lucee or exiting 0; and the first-run RustCFML download (~57 MB) is announced, with how long it took (#3895)
- RustCFML engine: `wheels start --engine=rustcfml` (and `wheels engines rustcfml start`) now refuses with `Wheels.EngineConflict` while the project's Lucee server is running, and `wheels start` refuses while its RustCFML server is running. Both engines used to run at once on different ports, and the CLI's HTTP commands targeted whichever one port detection picked. `wheels stop` now stops both: it used to stop RustCFML and leave Lucee running. RustCFML state also honours `-Dlucli.home` ahead of `$LUCLI_HOME`, as the rest of the CLI does, so `wheels engines rustcfml status` no longer misses a running server under a launcher that passes the property (#3913)
- Corrected the session-cookie (#3830) upgrade note's version range: the `this.sessionCookie` block has been in the app template since 4.0.4, so apps created with 4.0.4-4.1.1 (not only 4.0.6-4.1.1) need the `secure` update, and apps created before 4.0.4 have no block at all. Added a dedicated **Upgrading from 4.0 to 4.1** guide page listing every app-owned `public/Application.cfc` edit with its version and fail-safe status. ([#3967](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3967), [#3966](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3966), [#3968](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3968))
- On SQLite, `t.bigInteger()` now declares `BIGINT` instead of `INTEGER`, so the column is bound as a 64-bit integer. Values above 2,147,483,647 used to fail on Adobe ColdFusion and were silently changed on Lucee (clamped to 2,147,483,647) and BoxLang (wrapped). Existing columns keep `INTEGER` until re-declared with `changeColumn(columnType="biginteger")`; a `biginteger` primary key stays `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, SQLite's rowid (#4089)
- On Oracle, a calculated property declared with `dataType="bigint"` now maps to `cf_sql_bigint` instead of failing with "The column type bigint is not mapped to a CFML SQL type" (#4089)
- On SQLite, `INTEGER` columns, including `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` ids, are now bound as 64-bit integers, matching how SQLite stores them. A value or id above 2,147,483,647 used to fail on Adobe ColdFusion and was silently changed on Lucee (clamped to 2,147,483,647) and BoxLang (wrapped), so a find, update or delete by such an id could act on a different row. Existing `INTEGER` columns are protected without a migration (#4142)
- On SQLite, `updateAll()` now stores a date as ISO-8601 text, as `save()` does, instead of CFML's `{ts '...'}` string, which a `DATETIME` column then failed to read ("Error parsing time stamp"). `changeColumn()` to a date type converts such values left by earlier versions (`{ts}`, `{d}`, `{t}` literals and bare date-only or time-only text) to the form `save()` writes; the 4.1 to 4.2 upgrade guide has a query to find and repair them by hand (#4147)
- SSE channel resume now honours the bundled `WheelsSSE` client's `lastEventId` url parameter, not just the `Last-Event-ID` header, so a reconnect replays the events it missed out of the box — you no longer have to forward `params.lastEventId` from the action (the header still wins when both are present). The channel `events` filter now trims whitespace, so `events="a, b"` matches `b` (#4375)
- `wheels start` stops before starting anything when the port in `lucee.json` is taken, names the Wheels server that holds it (with `cd <its folder> && wheels stop`), and suggests a free `--port`. It used to warn and then fail with LuCLI's `Use: lucli server stop <name>`.
- `wheels new` without `--port` no longer gives every app port 8080: it takes the first port from 8080 that is free and not pinned in another project's `lucee.json`.
- The `wheels-starter-app` package is published to ForgeBox under the release version. Its `box.json` carried a fixed `1.0.0`, which the build never replaced, so every release overwrote ForgeBox's `1.0.0` entry and no 4.x starter app was listed under its own version. The release build's package check now fails on a version mismatch instead of printing a warning. (#3908)
- The starter app's README Quick Start works again: it creates the schema from the migrator page (`/wheels/migrator`) instead of `wheels migrate latest`, which needs a `wheels start` server, and restarts the server afterwards so the seeded settings load.
- The starter app's migrations create their schema again: nine `default=''` options on string and text columns, rejected since 4.1.0, are gone, and the migration header examples and the `create-table` snippet no longer teach the pattern.
- The starter app's tests run again: its `tests/runner.cfm`, an old copy of the framework's core-test runner that set the datasource from the folder name ("Datasource [public] doesn't exist"), is replaced by the app template's runner, which runs the specs on the `starterApp_test` datasource.
- `wheels stop` now stops the project's running server even when it runs under an older registration name (after a `lucee.json` `name` change), instead of stopping only the current name and leaving the old-name server running, and it deletes the live server's start token rather than a stale registration's (#3994)
- Apps running under a subfolder (`/app1` as an IIS virtual directory or Tomcat context pointing at `public/`) no longer lose the subfolder in five places (#3948):
  - `/app1/index.cfm` with an empty `PATH_INFO` now routes to the root instead of an `App1` controller.
  - `?reload=true` redirects back under `/app1`.
  - `tests/runner.cfm` falls back to the `/wheels` mapping when `/app1/wheels/...` does not exist.
  - The debug bar's environment-switch links keep `index.cfm` in Partial URL rewriting.
  - The debug bar's offline-docs links point under the subfolder.
- `TenantMigrator.migrateAll()` lists a tenant whose migration step failed under `failed` (with `error`, `version`, `direction` and the full `output`) instead of `success`. The migrator reports a failed step in its output rather than throwing, so the tenant used to be counted as migrated. The migrator's output text is unchanged; `Migrator.$lastStepFailure()` exposes the failed step as data.
- The `wheels.Test` (RocketUnit) deprecation message now links the `v4-1-0` testing guides instead of the stale `v4-0-0` path. ([#3968](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3968))
- Test-runner URLs beyond `/wheels/core/tests` and `/wheels/app/tests` now bind the isolated test application too: the `testbox` and `tests_testbox` runner paths are matched (case-insensitively, anchored at the start of the path), so a run reached through a convention path such as `/wheels/testbox` no longer executes specs against the live application. On an app configured for isolation, a runner reached through a route that does not bind the isolated scope (a custom route) is now refused with a `409` naming the canonical runner path; apps without the isolation include keep their existing live-scope behaviour (#4379)
- `TestClient` keeps and returns cookies correctly on Adobe CF. Adobe hands repeated `Set-Cookie` headers over as a struct, and the client walked its keys instead of its values, so no cookie was kept and every request started a new session. And cookies went back through `cfhttpparam type="cookie"`, which URL-encodes again, so a value the server had already encoded (such as an encrypted token) arrived double-encoded. The client now reads every `Set-Cookie` shape and sends the jar back as one `Cookie` header, values exactly as the server set them; a value set with `withCookie()` is URL-encoded once.
- A `wheels.wheelstest.TestClient` created inside a test run and pointed at the test server now sends its requests to the test application, as `$testClient()` already did. Before, a client built with `new wheels.wheelstest.TestClient()` or an app subclass reached the live application and its primary datasource while the spec code used `<datasource>_test`.
  - The test server is a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`) or the host of a configured `testClientBaseUrl`.
  - A client aimed at any other host is unchanged, and so is one created outside a test run, such as in a scheduled task or a script.
  - Pass `testContext=false` to address the live application.
- The test-context path trigger binds the isolated test application only for a request whose path targets a runner endpoint (`/wheels/core/tests` or `/wheels/app/tests`), matched at the start of the path after stripping the query string and rejecting `..` / `//` segments (#4348)
- A request that ends with `abort` inside a Wheels transaction (`invokeWithTransaction()`, a nested call that joins it, a savepoint unit, or `withAdvisoryLock(transaction = true)`) now rolls the transaction back on every engine, as it already did on Lucee and Adobe ColdFusion. On BoxLang the writes made before the abort were kept
- `t.uniqueidentifier()` now creates a typed UUID column on every database: `UUID` on PostgreSQL, CockroachDB and H2, `CHAR(36)` on MySQL, SQLite and Oracle (SQL Server keeps `UNIQUEIDENTIFIER`). It previously emitted DDL with no type and SQL Server's `newid()` default, which failed everywhere else; `newid()` now becomes each database's own UUID generator. PostgreSQL `uuid` columns now bind as `cf_sql_other`, so finders on a `uuid` column, including ones created outside Wheels, work instead of failing with `operator does not exist: uuid = character varying`. Blank native UUID primary keys are now generated on PostgreSQL, CockroachDB and H2 too, and `generateUUID()` now always returns a version 4 UUID from a cryptographically strong source: on RustCFML it used to return a zero-filled value (#4094)
- `wheels upgrade apply` names old framework backups still inside `vendor/` (`vendor/wheels.bak-*` left by earlier versions), which make the framework log an error on every start, and says to move or delete them. When the backup can't be made (a separately mounted `vendor/`, for example), it says nothing was changed and gives the workaround: copy `vendor/wheels/` yourself, then use `--nobackup`.
- `wheels upgrade apply` now updates a CommandBox app's `box.json` `wheels-core` version (an exact version or a `^`/`~` range, in `dependencies` and `devDependencies`) to the framework it installed, and prints the change. Before, the old pin stayed, and a later `box install` copied the old framework back over the new `vendor/wheels/`, leaving a mix of both versions. Other specs (`4.x`, a channel, a `forgebox:` spec) are left alone with the exact line to change. If `box.json` isn't valid JSON, `apply` refuses before changing anything.
- `wheels upgrade check` on 3.x-era apps: a 3.0 GA app (no version in `vendor/wheels/box.json`) now reports its real version instead of `unknown` and no longer sees findings twice; checks for 3.x code that 4.x removed (WireBox, `renderPage()`, old test bases) still run after the framework swap, while checks for 4.0 default changes and for things that are only deprecated (`plugins/`, which `wheels new` scaffolds, and `paginationLinks()`) are skipped for an app already on 4.x, so a stock 4.x app reports nothing breaking; `Missing csrfCookieEncryptionSecretKey` only fires for apps that set `csrfStore="cookie"`; and the RocketUnit base class (`wheels.Test`, which still runs on 4.x) is now an advisory instead of a breaking finding, so it no longer fails the CI gate, with fix text that says to convert the tests before switching to `wheels.WheelsTest` (the `wheels.Testbox` alias, also advisory, can switch directly) (#3939)
- `wheels upgrade check --to=<version>` now says when the CLI running it is older than `<version>`, because its checks and app template only know releases up to its own version. It recommends upgrading the CLI first, where it used to report "All Clear" for an incomplete upgrade. This is a warning only; the exit code doesn't change. JSON output gains `cliVersion`, `cliBehindTarget` and `warnings`. The app-template drift finding now points at the upgrade guides for the target version instead of naming a 4.0-era change (#4154).
- `wheels upgrade check`: the legacy plugins check no longer passes a 3.x app just because `plugins/` is empty or missing. It also reports plugin packages declared in `box.json` (installed under `plugins/`, or `cfwheels-*` packages) and code that reads `application.wheels.plugins`.
- `wheels upgrade check`: "Direct WireBox references" now scans `public/Application.cfc`, where 3.x apps bootstrap WireBox, and skips third-party packages under `app/` (a `box.json` install path, or any folder with its own `box.json`, such as `app/lib/logbox/`). It used to miss the app's own bootstrap and flag the package's code instead.
- Under CommandBox, a request whose path contains a backslash (`\` or `%5C`) now gets a 400 instead of a 500 from the servlet container. The fix is a new "Reject a backslash in the path" rule in the shipped `public/urlrewrite.xml` (and the `wheels new` template). Existing CommandBox apps can copy that rule from a new app's `public/urlrewrite.xml` into their own, ahead of the "Wheels pretty URLs" rule. `wheels start` (LuCLI on Tomcat) already answers 400 (#4114).
- Validation `condition` / `unless` now handles the expression shapes that used to evaluate silently. A bare name, on its own or on the left of a comparison (`condition="isActive"`, `condition="status != 'draft'"`), is resolved as `this.<name>` (a property value or a method call) when the record has it. Before, a lone name evaluated to `false` and skipped the rule, and a left-hand name was compared as its own text, so the rule always ran. A name the record doesn't have, a `this.` reference on the right of a comparison, or an expression that isn't one value or a complete `left operator right` comparison now throws `Wheels.InvalidValidationCondition`. A lone `true` or `false` evaluates to its value, and a bare word on the right of a comparison stays a string literal. The validation guide now documents the supported forms (#3928)
- Validation `condition` / `unless` expressions that use a `this.` reference now fail closed for forms the evaluator never really supported, instead of silently skipping or mis-running the rule: a `this.` reference on the **right** of a comparison (e.g. `this.total gt this.limit`), and the word-form operators **`is`**, **`and`** and **`or`** on the `this.` path, now throw `Wheels.InvalidValidationCondition` naming the supported replacement. **Upgrade note:** replace `is` with `eq` (or `==`), `and` with `&&`, `or` with `||`, and put any `this.` reference on the left side of a comparison. ([#3964](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3964))
- A `where` condition that compares a function call with a value (e.g. `ABS(id) = 5`, `UPPER(title) = 'X'`, `UPPER(title) IS NULL`) now runs, with the value bound as a parameter, instead of failing with "Array index [1] out of range"
- With `include`, a `where` condition that compares a column with a function call (e.g. `id = ABS(1)`) now qualifies the column with its table instead of failing as an ambiguous column when the joined tables share its name
- A `where` string with a quoted pattern followed by `ESCAPE '...'`, or with lowercase or mixed-case `and` / `or` between quoted values, now runs instead of failing with an "invalid hexadecimal String" error
- `where=`: a literal compared with another table's column inside a subquery (`id IN (SELECT book_id FROM accesses WHERE user_id = 1)`) now throws `Wheels.UnbindableWhereValue`, naming the condition (bound values shown as `?`, quoted values hidden) and the alternatives: `whereIn()` with a value array, or running the subquery first. Before, it failed with `Array index [1] out of range`. `Wheels.ColumnNotFound` from a `where=` now explains the rule too. `where=` accepts model columns compared with values, so a condition with no column, such as `where="1 = 0"`, isn't supported: for no rows use `whereIn()` with an empty array, and for every row leave the condition out.
- A CFML date interpolated into a finder's `where` string works again. Its ODBC escape form (`{ts '2000-01-01 00:00:00'}`, `{d '...'}`, `{t '...'}`) failed with `invalid hexadecimal String` when quoted (`where="createdAt >= '#since#'"`) or `the value for cfqueryparam cannot be determined` when bare; the date is now bound as its value (#3941)
- Fixed a data-loss bug where a `deleteAll` or `updateAll` matching zero rows, run inside a raw `transaction {}` block, silently rolled back the entire enclosing transaction on Lucee and Adobe. A zero-row bulk operation is now correctly treated as success rather than a failed operation. Wheels-managed transactions (`transaction()` / `invokeWithTransaction`) were never affected. ([#3944](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3944))
- A legacy-format CSRF cookie is no longer misread when decrypting it with the configured cipher returns a value that happens to parse as JSON (a number, say): the cookie's payload must now be the struct the cookie is written with, carrying an authenticity token, and the token reader ignores any other JSON.

### Security

- The `/wheels/*` development tools (info, routes, docs, test runners, packages, the deprecated HTTP MCP endpoint, …) now require the request's socket peer (`REMOTE_ADDR`) to be a loopback address, and are refused outright in the `production` environment. A dev server reached through Docker port publishing, where requests arrive from the Docker gateway, can allow specific peers with `set(devToolsAllowedRemoteAddresses="172.17.0.1")` (exact IPv4/IPv6 addresses or IPv4 CIDR ranges; honoured in development and testing only); publish the port on `127.0.0.1` when you do. **Behaviour change** for Docker-hosted development: `/wheels/*` returns 403 from the host until the gateway address is listed. The dev tools and `/wheels/cli` now treat only IP literals as loopback (a zone-scoped IPv6 loopback included); host names in `X-Forwarded-For` are not resolved. ([GHSA-6v6c-p375-vxw9](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-6v6c-p375-vxw9))
- On MySQL and MariaDB, a string value that Wheels writes into the SQL text instead of binding it (every value with `parameterize=false`, and literals a `where` string leaves in place, such as function arguments) is now written as a hex literal when it contains a backslash, so it reads the same whatever the `sql_mode`; other values are unchanged. A backslash in those positions is now an ordinary character, as it already was in bound values: if you wrote `\\` there to mean one backslash, write `\`. `LIKE ... ESCAPE '\\'` and `ESCAPE '\'` both still mean a single backslash. The query-builder guide now lists which `where` values are bound. ([GHSA-mvr9-2h5x-w6cq](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-mvr9-2h5x-w6cq))
- `hasManyCheckBox()` nested saves now set both keys on a new composite-key join row. Before, only the parent key was set and the insert failed with a NOT NULL error (#3884) ([GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq))
- `hasManyCheckBox()` now works with a surrogate-`id` join model (the shape `wheels generate model` creates). The posted keys are matched on the join model's foreign keys, so checking a box adds the row, unchecking removes it, and the box shows as checked for an existing row. Before, the first key was read as the join row's `id`, so unchecking a box could delete an unrelated join row (#3885) ([GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq))
- `hasManyCheckBox()` no longer throws `Element ID is undefined in ARGUMENTS` on Adobe ColdFusion when it's called without an `id` ([GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq))
- Nested properties now only update or delete child rows that belong to the record being saved. A nested key that names a row of another record is treated as not found (it isn't changed, moved or deleted), the child's foreign key always comes from the parent's saved key, and posted primary-key values aren't kept on a new child except the join row's own foreign keys. This applies to `hasMany`, `hasOne` and nested `belongsTo` associations, including custom `joinKey` associations; keys are compared exactly (string keys such as `01` and `1` are different, and BIGINT keys are compared without rounding), and nested struct keys above 2^31 on BIGINT columns are looked up instead of being treated as new rows. A composite-key join model needs a `belongsTo` for each key column; without one, adding a row through nested properties now raises `Wheels.InvalidNestedKey` naming the column (it used to fail in the database with a NOT NULL error) ([GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-6979-65wv-7pxq))
- **Behaviour change:** `/wheels/app/tests` (and `wheels test`) now **default to the `<datasource>_test` database and refuse to run if it is absent**, instead of silently falling back to the primary datasource — so test writes never reach your real database. An app with no `<datasource>_test` that runs its tests gets a clear refusal (HTTP 409 / a red "Test run failed before any spec ran" message) telling you to create the `<datasource>_test` datasource, pass `useTestDB=false` (`wheels test --no-test-db`), or set `allowTestsAgainstPrimaryDatasource=true`. A default `wheels new` (SQLite) app already has a `<name>_test` datasource, so `wheels new` → `wheels test` is unaffected; only apps that configure a datasource without a matching `_test` (e.g. `wheels new --no-sqlite`) need to act. Precedence: an explicit `useTestDB=true` always requires the test database; `useTestDB=false` runs against the primary datasource; and `set(allowTestsAgainstPrimaryDatasource=true)` in `config/settings.cfm` lets an **omitted** flag (e.g. from an older CLI that cannot send `useTestDB=false`) run against the primary datasource with a warning. ([GHSA-xcj9-wm25-4cg4](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-xcj9-wm25-4cg4))
- The test-runner application isolation (`<name>_wheelsTest`, #3374) is now selected only in the `development` and `testing` environments. The runner path is matched against the request path. The `X-Wheels-Test-Context` header and `WHEELS_TEST_CONTEXT` cookie must carry the per-process runner secret and come from a loopback client. An isolated application refuses to start outside development and testing before any of its start-up code runs, and the core and app test runners refuse to run in production. **Existing apps:** test-context isolation needs `WHEELS_ENV` set to `development` or `testing` (the `wheels new` template sets it). Without it, specs run against the live application scope, and the runner logs an isolation-off warning and sends an `X-Wheels-Test-Isolation: off` response header. ([GHSA-xcj9-wm25-4cg4](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-xcj9-wm25-4cg4))
- Path-containment guards that confine a resolved target to a root directory — database dump output, zip extraction, package manifest mappings, dev-UI asset and offline-docs serving, and image-tag source resolution — now compare canonical paths **exactly** (case-sensitively) with a separator-qualified boundary, through a shared `wheels.PathGuard` helper. Both the root and the candidate are canonicalised the same way, so a root configured in a different case than its on-disk spelling still resolves its own descendants. The always-on lexical checks and the existing authorization gates are unchanged. ([GHSA-m63x-8m98-225v](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-m63x-8m98-225v))
- `findByKey()`, `exists(key=...)`, `updateByKey()` and `deleteByKey()` now combine the key with an incoming scope chain, query builder or `where` argument (AND) instead of replacing it, so a key outside the scope or `where` returns `false` or matches nothing. Lookups with no `where` are unchanged. `exists()` no longer throws in development when it gets both `key` and `where`: it checks the key within the `where`, the same in every environment. ([GHSA-2qh9-qcxq-f8qc](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-2qh9-qcxq-f8qc))
- `wheels test --filter=<SpecName>` now decides whether a spec file is under the spec root (`tests/specs`, or `vendor/wheels/tests/specs` with `--core`) with an exact, separator-qualified comparison of canonical paths, the same rule as the framework path guards. ([GHSA-m63x-8m98-225v](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-m63x-8m98-225v))
- `LocalDisk` storage now validates object keys per path segment instead of rejecting any key that contains `..` anywhere. A legitimate name with two dots inside a segment — `reports/q3..final.pdf`, `a..b.txt`, `v1..2` — can now be stored, while the key check is tightened: it rejects any segment that is made only of dots and/or whitespace (`.`, `..`, `...`, `. .`, and — because Windows strips trailing dots and spaces — `.. `) and any drive-letter prefix (`C:/…`, `C:foo`). Every key stays relative to the root: a leading `/`, a trailing `/`, doubled `//` and a UNC/network prefix (`//server/share`, `\\server\share`) are normalised to a relative key under the root, and keys are never URL-decoded, so `%2e%2e%2f` stays a literal segment and can't turn into `../`. As defence in depth the resolved path is lexically canonicalised (resolving `./`, `../` and `//`) and confirmed inside the root with a trailing-separator compare, so `/root-other` can't pass as inside `/root`. The containment check is lexical and uniform on every engine including the JVM-free RustCFML; it does not resolve symlinks (a symlink under `root` is still followed and trusted — see [#4020](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/4020)). ([#3912](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3912))
- Apps generated by `wheels deploy init` ran in development mode in production, showing development error details: the generated `config/environment.cfm` hardcoded `set(environment="development")`, so the image's `WHEELS_ENV=production` had no effect. The generated config now reads `WHEELS_ENV`: unset or empty means `development`, and any value other than `development`, `testing`, `maintenance` or `production` stops the app from starting instead of running with development-style error output. `config/settings.cfm` now reads `WHEELS_DATASOURCE` with the app name as its default. This applies to `wheels new` and the `wheels-base-template` download. `wheels info` and the `wheels test` preamble recognise the new `dataSourceName` form. **Existing apps:** templates don't reach an existing app, so replace `config/environment.cfm` with the generated version (see the Environments and Configuration guide), or hardcode `set(environment="production");` on production servers. A `WHEELS_ENV` line in `.env` wins over the process environment, so make sure a production server's `.env` doesn't say `development`. (#3946)
- A per-tenant `config` override can no longer switch on `showDebugInformation` or `enablePublicComponent` for a tenant's requests. `$get()`'s tenant-override guard already excluded `showErrorInformation`, but not the other two debug flags it is grouped with everywhere else, so a per-tenant config could expose debug output or the public-component surface for that tenant. All three debug flags are now non-overridable per-tenant. ([#3880](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3880))

---

# [4.1.2](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/releases/tag/v4.1.2) => 2026-10-01

### Added

- Migrations can opt out of the per-step transaction with `this.useTransaction = false`. The migrator then runs `up()`/`down()` without a transaction and records or removes the version only after the step succeeds, so a migration that touches a second datasource runs on Adobe ColdFusion, which rejects mixed datasources inside a transaction. When Adobe raises that error, the migrator's report now names the opt-out. The default per-step transaction is unchanged (#3772)
- Added a `baseUrl` setting for a canonical host. `set(baseUrl="https://your-host")` in `config/settings.cfm` makes `urlFor()`, `linkTo()`, `redirectTo(onlyPath=false)`, and asset URLs build every absolute URL from that scheme/host/port instead of the incoming request — so emailed links and links generated in background jobs no longer depend on how the request arrived. Explicit `host`/`protocol`/`port` arguments still win, and leaving it unset keeps the historical request-derived behaviour. In `production` Wheels logs a one-time reminder when it is unset. (#3838)

### Changed

- The minimal "Application Error" page shown when Wheels fails to initialize now follows the app's `showErrorInformation` setting once startup has got far enough to set it. When it's on (every environment except `production`, by default), the page shows the root cause in full: type, message, detail, the event and the tag context, with Adobe CF's event-handler wrapper unwrapped. **Behaviour change:** when it's off, the page shows only the pointer to `wheels.log` and **no longer prints the exception message**, so a production startup failure's text no longer reaches the browser. Set `showErrorInformation` to `true` to see it on the page. The full failure is still written to `wheels.log` in every environment, and that log line now carries the whole tag context (up to 20 frames), not only the first frame. A failure before the settings load still shows the bare message, as before. Every value is HTML-encoded. Existing apps must copy the change into their own `public/Application.cfc`: replace `$renderMinimalError()` and `$logStartupFailure()`, replace `$startupFailureFrame()` with `$startupFailureFrames()`, and add `$startupFailureShowDetail()`, `$startupFailureDetailHtml()` and `$startupFailureRoot()` from the current `wheels new` template (#3671)
- `set(baseUrl=...)` is now parsed once when the application starts, so a malformed value stops the application from starting with `Wheels.IncorrectConfiguration` instead of failing the first absolute URL a request builds. The parser also rejects a query (`?`), fragment (`#`), user info (`@`), a backslash, an unbracketed IPv6 host, an invalid host character and a port outside 1-65535, and accepts a bracketed IPv6 host (`https://[::1]:8443`). New apps from `wheels new` get a commented `set(baseUrl=...)` line in `config/settings.cfm` ([#3842](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3842))
- `wheels generate auth`: the generated `Sessions.delete()` (logout) now ends the session only for a POST (the logout form, which sends `_method=delete`) or a real `DELETE` request. A `GET` or `HEAD`, which the default wildcard route also maps to `/sessions/delete`, redirects to the login page without logging out. **Apps generated with an earlier version should add the same guard** at the top of `delete()` in `app/controllers/Sessions.cfc`: (#3819)
  ```cfm
  if (!isPost() && !isDelete()) {
  	redirectTo(route="login");
  	return;
  }
  ```
- The migrator dev-tool endpoints and the GUI pages that issue their anti-CSRF token now require the request's `Host` header to name the local machine, in addition to the existing loopback-socket and no-forwarded-client checks. `localhost`, any `*.localhost` name (RFC 6761), `127.0.0.0/8`, and `[::1]` are accepted by default. **If you reach the migrator under a custom local hostname (for example `myapp.test` via `/etc/hosts`, or a Valet/Herd-style `*.test`), it now returns `403` until you allow it** with `set(devToolsAllowedHosts="myapp.test")` (comma-delimited, empty by default) — the same one setting the dispatch-level dev-tools gate uses. The `403` body states this fix. (#3840)
- `$prependUrl` (used by `urlFor(onlyPath=false)` and asset URLs) still derives an absolute URL's `http`/`https` scheme from the client-controlled `X-Forwarded-Proto` header as before, so an app behind a TLS-terminating proxy keeps its `https://` URLs. When the header is the only reason for `https://` and the app has not set `set(trustProxyHeaders=true)`, Wheels now logs a one-time warning to `wheels.log`. **Wheels 4.2 will require `trustProxyHeaders=true` to honour `X-Forwarded-Proto` for the scheme and otherwise ignore it** (matching `isSecure()`); apps behind a trusted reverse proxy should set `trustProxyHeaders=true` now. The real socket TLS state is unaffected (#3835)

### Fixed

- The `apt`/`dnf` `wheels` launcher no longer uses a pre-set `JAVA_HOME` that points at a JDK older than 21. It now checks the version, prints `wheels: ignoring JAVA_HOME=<path> (Java N); Wheels needs Java 21+` to stderr, and uses the Java 21 the package installed. Previously every command except `wheels --version` crashed with `UnsupportedClassVersionError` on machines, including GitHub's Ubuntu runners, that export `JAVA_HOME` at Java 17. The fallback probes also skip an older `default-java` or alternatives `java`. On 4.1.1, run `unset JAVA_HOME` or point `JAVA_HOME` at a Java 21+ install as a workaround (Refs #3728)
- On Adobe ColdFusion with Oracle, reading a table's column metadata no longer leaves JDBC statements open. Every `cfdbinfo(type="columns")` call there retained about seven statements, with their row buffers, on the pooled connection for the connection's lifetime (enough to exhaust the heap of a long test run). `$dbinfo(type="columns")` now reads the same fields from the Oracle data dictionary (`ALL_TAB_COLUMNS` in the current schema, plus the primary-key constraint) with one ordinary query. A table the dictionary can't see in the current schema (reached through a synonym, or owned by another schema) still goes through `cfdbinfo`, as before. Other engines and databases are unchanged (#3732)
- `wheels test` can run a single spec file. `--filter` / `--directory` accept a spec file's dotted path (`tests.specs.models.UserSpec`) or its bare name (`UserSpec`), which is looked up anywhere under `tests/specs/`. A name that matches more than one file stops with the list of dotted paths instead of guessing. The app and core test runners run a `directory=` that names a spec file as that one bundle, where it used to discover 0 bundles, so the same works when calling the runner URL directly and with `bash tools/test-local.sh <area>/<Spec>`. A run that discovers no bundles no longer prints a green `0 passed` line above its failure (#3759)
- On BoxLang, a reload (`?reload=true&password=…` or the CLI's `X-Wheels-Reload-Password` header) no longer stalls for the reload lock's timeout (180 s per wave, 360 s and more seen) while the application has active sessions, and each session's `onSessionEnd` handler now runs during the reload instead of being skipped with a lock-timeout error. BoxLang ends sessions on worker threads inside `applicationStop()`, while the reload request holds the exclusive reload lock; `$simpleLock()` now records the restart in the application scope, and a session end that finds that marker runs without waiting for the lock the parked restart holds. The fix is in the framework: existing apps need no change to `public/Application.cfc` (#3770)
- `wheels test --filter=<SpecName>` no longer misnames a spec reached through a symlink into a sibling folder whose name starts with the spec root's (such as `tests/specsOld`): it used to produce a wrong dotted path like `tests.specs.ld.FooSpec` and run 0 bundles. A spec folder reachable twice through a symlink inside the spec root is also no longer reported as an ambiguous match with two identical paths (#3800)
- The CLI's RustCFML backend now downloads the engine build the framework is tested against (`v0.693.0`, from `tools/rustcfml/ENGINE_VERSION`) instead of the stale `v0.637.0` pin in `RustCFMLEngine.cfc`. The daily engine auto-bump now moves both pins together, and a CLI spec fails if they ever differ (#3812)
- On an Intel Mac, `wheels start --engine=rustcfml` and `wheels engines rustcfml install|start` now say that RustCFML publishes no macOS Intel (x86_64) build and to use the default engine (`wheels start`), instead of failing on a 404 download (#3815)
- `processRequest()` no longer changes the cached controller class's CSRF settings. Its `csrf` option (default `ignore`) now applies only to the controller instance it builds, so real requests to a controller that a spec exercised with `processRequest()` keep their configured CSRF protection instead of skipping it until the next reload (#3843)
- The per-request query cache now keys on the datasource a query actually runs against, after tenant resolution. An app that switches tenant (or datasource) within one request no longer receives another datasource's cached `findAll()`/`count()` result for an identical finder call (#3844)
- `sendFile(file="reports/2026/q1.zip")` finds the file under the `filePath` folder on disk (`public/files`) again when the app runs in a subdirectory whose URL rewrites fold `/public/` out of the URL, or that sets `set(subpath=...)`. The folder was built from `rootPath`, the app's URL path, so in those layouts it pointed one level above `public/` and `sendFile()` threw `Wheels.FileNotFound`. It is now built from the web root's folder on disk. Root-anchored and mapping paths, full disk paths and an absolute `directory` behave as before ([#3852](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3852))
- CLI commands that fail now exit non-zero, and the MCP tools report them as errors (`isError`), instead of printing a message and reporting success. This covers `wheels generate` refusals (the file already exists, an invalid name, an unknown property or test type, a route that exists, which now exits 1) and missing arguments, including a bare `wheels generate`; `wheels create`, `wheels destroy`, `wheels db` and `wheels upgrade` with no arguments; `wheels migrate pretend|forget` without a version; `wheels doctor` with status CRITICAL; `wheels analyze` outside a project; `wheels destroy` of a name that was never generated (it also no longer writes a drop-table migration); `wheels docs` with an unknown action and `wheels docs status` outside a project; a deploy config whose `servers` lists no hosts; and `wheels start --engine=rustcfml` when the port is already in use or the server exits right after starting. Error messages are printed once instead of twice. Scripts that relied on these commands exiting 0 after printing usage now need the arguments they were missing. Each failure message is complete on its own (usage line, doctor's issues), because MCP clients receive only the message. (#3863)
- `wheels coverage` on a warm dev server now also counts controller and model `config()` methods. Wheels runs `config()` once per class and caches the result, so on a server that had already loaded them those methods never ran during the coverage run. Coverage mode now drops the cached classes so they are rebuilt from the instrumented code. (#3864)
- `wheels coverage` no longer reports 0% when the dev server has already served the app. With Lucee's default `inspectTemplate=once` the server kept running the code it had compiled before coverage instrumented `app/`, so no counter ever ran. Coverage mode now clears Lucee's compiled-page pool before the suite runs. The clear is server-wide, so other apps on the same dev server recompile on their next request. (#3859)
- `dateField()` now emits a bare `yyyy-mm-dd` value for its `<input type="date">`. Bound to a `date`/`datetime` property that carries a time component (for example a `datetime` column), it previously rendered the full value (e.g. `2026-10-01 00:00:00`), which browsers cannot display in a date control, so the field showed blank. An already-bare date, an explicit non-date value, and an empty value are left unchanged. (#3856)
- `wheels docs fetch` now verifies the downloaded docs bundle against the SHA-512 checksum published next to it on the release before unpacking, and exits non-zero with a clear message when the checksum is missing or does not match, or the download or unpack fails, leaving any existing install in place instead of a half-unpacked docs directory (#3823)
- The local docs mirror that `wheels docs fetch` puts in `public/wheels-docs/` (about 300 MB) is no longer committed or shipped by accident. New apps list it in `.gitignore`, `wheels deploy init` lists it in `.dockerignore`, and the mirror now carries its own `.gitignore` so existing apps keep it out of git too. In an existing app's `.dockerignore`, add `public/wheels-docs` yourself. If the mirror was already committed, untrack it with `git rm -r --cached public/wheels-docs`. (#3855)
- `wheels docs fetch` no longer deletes a `public/wheels-docs` folder the app keeps for itself, and editing the app's docs mirror no longer changes the shared docs cache under `~/.wheels/docs/<version>/`. The mirror is now a real copy instead of hardlinks, built beside the target and renamed into place. It is replaced only when it is a docs mirror (it holds the bundle's `manifest.json`) and only when missing or out of date. A `public/wheels-docs` without `manifest.json` is left alone with a message saying so. If the mirror can't be copied or swapped in, the command now exits non-zero instead of reporting success. This matches the Linux package wrapper (#3821) (#3848)
- `Wheels.TableNotFound`, `Wheels.DataSourceNotFound`, and `Wheels.ColumnNotFound` error pages now return HTTP **500** instead of 404.
