These signal a misconfigured or unmigrated deploy (a server fault), so a 404 hid them from monitoring and made an unmigrated deploy look like missing pages. Genuine client not-found errors (record, route, view, action) still return 404. (#3862)
- A missing or invalid CSRF token (`Wheels.InvalidAuthenticityToken`) error page now returns HTTP **403** instead of 500, so a forged or expired-form POST is not counted as a server error. (#3862)
- `wheels generate app <name> --dry-run` (and the MCP `generate` tool with `type=app` and `dry-run`) no longer creates the project. It printed "Dry run — nothing will be written" and then wrote the whole app, including a `vendor/wheels` copy and a `.env` with generated passwords. It now lists the folder it would create and writes nothing. (#3858)
- The documented example job now runs. It called `sendEmail()` (and the background-jobs guide also called `model()`), but `wheels.Job` extends nothing, so neither was in scope and the job failed with `No matching function [SENDEMAIL] found`. `wheels.Job` now has a `model()` that delegates to `application.wo.model()`, so `model("User")` works inside `perform()`. `sendEmail()` stays a controller function: the example job in the `app/jobs/README.md` scaffold, the `Job.cfc` header, the consumer `CLAUDE.md` and the background-jobs guide now send mail through a mailer (`new app.mailers.UserMailer().sendWelcome(user)`). The scaffold README also drops its "Requirements" section, which told users to generate a `create_wheels_jobs_table` migration (an empty TODO migration). The `wheels_jobs` table is created automatically on first use (#3860)
- The Linux `.deb`/`.rpm` `wheels` command no longer deletes and re-copies `public/wheels-docs` on every run from an app root, which wiped anything you had put there, briefly removed the docs assets from a running dev server, and hardlinked the copy so editing it changed the shared `~/.wheels/docs` cache. It now copies the offline docs only when the mirror is missing or its `manifest.json` no longer matches the installed docs (so existing mirrors still refresh on upgrade), leaves a `public/wheels-docs` without a `manifest.json` alone, and warns instead of aborting your command if the copy fails (#3821)
- The MCP tools (`wheels mcp wheels`) no longer return ANSI colour codes in their text. CLI colour now appears only on an interactive terminal: it is dropped under the MCP server, when output is piped or redirected, and when `NO_COLOR` is set. (#3869)
- Error pages now treat **404 as an allow-list** of the client-triggerable not-found types (`Wheels.RouteNotFound`, `Wheels.RecordNotFound`, `Wheels.ViewNotFound`, `Wheels.FileNotFound` from `sendFile()`, `Wheels.ActionNotAllowed`, `Wheels.ActionParameterMissing`) rather than mapping *any* `Wheels.*NotFound` to 404. Every other `*NotFound` — a missing package, model, method, filter, association, Vite asset/manifest, and any future type — is a server-side config/code fault and now returns HTTP **500** so monitoring sees it, instead of masquerading as a 404. (This generalises the `Table`/`DataSource`/`Column` → 500 fix.) (#3872)
- An unsupported requested format (`Wheels.FormatNotAcceptable`, e.g. a `.ext`/`?format=` the action cannot answer) error page now returns HTTP **406 Not Acceptable** instead of 500. (#3872)
- `wheels packages add --offline` (and `WHEELS_OFFLINE=1`) no longer downloads the package tarball. Offline mode already refused the registry index and manifest fetches, but with a cached manifest the install still went to the network; it now fails with the offline message before any download (#3867)
- `wheels packages add` and `update` now install the stable release instead of an equal pre-release (e.g. `1.2.0` over `1.2.0-rc.1`), an exact pin such as `@1.2.0` no longer matches `1.2.0-rc.1`, and pre-release versions are only considered when the pin names one (`@1.2.0-rc.1`); the CLI's SemVer comparison now orders pre-release labels per SemVer 2.0.0, and `update` no longer downgrades an installed pre-release that is newer than the latest release (#3829)
- `reload()` no longer truncates fractional values: it used to cast every numeric value that fit in 32 bits to an integer, so a decimal column holding `149.25` reloaded as `149` (and `-3.75` as `-3`). The object then saw the column as changed, and the next `save()` wrote the truncated value back to the database, even when only another field was edited. Only integer columns holding a whole number are normalized now. (#3818)
- The CLI's RustCFML backend now verifies the engine binary against a pinned sha256 for its release asset before using it: a download that doesn't match is deleted and refused with `Wheels.RustCFML.ChecksumMismatch`, a cached binary is re-checked instead of trusted, and the binary is only renamed into place once verified. A failed download now reports `Wheels.RustCFML.InstallFailed` with its URL instead of a "Can't cast Complex Object Type Struct to String" error. The pins live in `tools/rustcfml/ENGINE_SHA256` (mirrored in `RustCFMLEngine.cfc`), and the daily engine auto-bump refreshes them from the release's published digests (#3824)
- `wheels generate scaffold` with a `boolean` property, such as `published:boolean`, no longer generates specs that fail on SQLite with "Published is not a number". The generated test data used `true`; a migration's boolean column is `INTEGER` on SQLite, so the model's automatic numericality validation rejected it. The generated specs now use `1`, which that validation accepts and which `BIT`/`BOOLEAN` columns on the other databases store as true. In specs generated with an earlier version, change `"published": true` to `"published": 1` (#3868)
- `wheels generate scaffold`: a key that can't belong to the model, such as `GET /posts/abc` for an integer primary key, now returns 404 instead of a 500 `Wheels.InvalidValue` error. The generated `requireRecord()` filter treats `Wheels.InvalidValue` from its `findByKey()` lookup as a missing record and rethrows any other error. The framework's check on typed key values is unchanged. **Controllers generated with an earlier version** should wrap the lookup in their `requireRecord()` the same way; the current version is in `app/snippets/CRUDContent.txt` of a new `wheels new` app (#3868)
- `wheels generate scaffold`: a request for a record that doesn't exist (`GET /posts/999999`) now returns 404 in production too. The generated `requireRecord()` filter threw `Wheels.RecordNotFound`, which only the development error page turns into a 404, so production served its generic 500 page. The filter now calls `$throwErrorOrShow404Page()`, which sets 404 and shows the error in development or `app/events/onmissingtemplate.cfm` in production. **Controllers generated with an earlier version** should replace the `Throw(` call in their `requireRecord()` with `$throwErrorOrShow404Page(` (same arguments), and apps that keep `app/snippets/CRUDContent.txt` should make the same change there so later scaffolds pick it up (#3868)
- `sendEmail(file="reports/q3.txt")` attaches the file from the `filePath` folder on disk (`public/files`), as the argument docs say. Only a bare file name was resolved there; a relative path with a subfolder went to `cfmailparam` unchanged, which looked for it next to the framework's own templates and failed with "the file doesn't exist". Relative attachments now resolve the same way as `sendFile()`'s files; absolute paths and URLs are passed through as before ([#3852](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3852)) (#3857)
- `wheels new`: the scaffolded session cookie is now `Secure` whenever the request that creates the session arrives over HTTPS, directly or through a TLS-terminating proxy that sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, as well as for `WHEELS_ENV=production`. It used to depend on `WHEELS_ENV=production` alone. An app switched to production through `config/environment.cfm` (the scaffolded `.env` still says `development`) therefore issued its session cookie without `Secure`. The `this.sessionCookie` block has been in the app template since 4.0.4 (#2927), so **apps created with 4.0.4-4.1.1 should update `this.sessionCookie.secure` in `public/Application.cfc`** to: (#3830)
  ```cfm
  secure: (structKeyExists(variables, "currentEnv") && currentEnv == "production")
  	|| (IsBoolean(cgi.server_port_secure) && cgi.server_port_secure)
  	|| cgi.https == "on"
  	|| cgi.http_x_forwarded_proto == "https"
  ```
  (or set `this.sessionCookie.secure = true;` in `config/app.cfm` when the site is HTTPS-only). Apps created with 4.0.0-4.0.3 have no `this.sessionCookie` block at all and should add the whole block (httpOnly, sameSite and secure) from the template. `wheels generate auth` also adds a spec that a `GET` to the logout action does not end the session.
- On SQLite and H2, boolean columns created by migrations (`t.boolean()`, `addColumn(columnType="boolean")`) are now real boolean columns on the model. SQLite created them as `INTEGER` and H2 as `TINYINT(1)`, so the model treated them as integers: automatic validation rejected `true` and `"true"` with "is not a number", and a validated `save()` failed. The migrators now emit `BOOLEAN`, which both adapters read as a boolean, so `true`, `"true"` and `"false"` pass automatic validation and save. This applies to new columns only. Existing `INTEGER` / `TINYINT` boolean columns keep the previous behaviour until a migration converts them, which keeps the stored 1/0 values (on SQLite it rebuilds the table): `changeColumn(table="posts", columnName="published", columnType="boolean");`. The new definition replaces the old one, so pass `default` and `allowNull` again if the column had them (#3870)
- After a failed `wheels start` (for example a port that is already in use), the next `wheels start` no longer refuses with "registered to a different project: <unknown>". The failed start left a registration folder that names no project; it is now treated as left over and cleaned. `wheels start` also exits non-zero when it refuses to run, both outside a Wheels project and when the server name is registered to another project, so scripts and CI can tell. (#3861)
- `wheels start` cleans a leftover registration only when it has exactly the shape a failed start leaves (LuCLI's config file and nothing else). A stopped server registration of another project with the same name but no recorded project path (from plain LuCLI or before 4.0) is no longer wiped; `wheels start` refuses and points to `--force` instead. (#3865)
- An invalid `transaction` mode (e.g. a typo such as `save(transaction="commti")`) no longer leaves the connection marked as having an open transaction. The mode was rejected only after that marker was set, so if the app caught the error, every later save/update/delete on the same connection in that request silently ran with no transaction. `transaction="rollback"` then persisted its changes. The mode is now validated before any transaction state changes. (#3817)
- A request for a format the action does not provide now answers `406 Not Acceptable` instead of `200` with an empty body. On a controller that only `provides()` html, `GET /posts/2.json`, `/posts/2.xml` or `/posts/new.json` used to return an empty `200`. When the `format` param (the route's `.[format]` segment or `?format=`) names a format outside the action's `provides()` / `onlyProvides()` list, or a provided `json` / `xml` format the action never renders (no template and no `renderWith()` call), and the action rendered nothing itself, the automatic render now throws `Wheels.FormatNotAcceptable` with status 406 in development and returns a plain-text `406 Not Acceptable` response when `showErrorInformation` is off. Html requests, formats the action renders, `renderWith()` (which still falls back to the html view) and formats negotiated from the `Accept` header are unchanged (#3866)
- `wheels upgrade apply` no longer silently downgrades the framework when the app's `vendor/wheels/` is newer than the CLI's bundled copy (for example, an app created with a newer CLI or a `wheels-be` snapshot). It now refuses before changing anything, names both versions, and suggests installing a newer CLI; pass the new `--allow-downgrade` flag to install the older framework anyway. The summary now says `upgraded`, `downgraded` or `reinstalled` to match what actually happened (#3831)
- `wheels upgrade check` without `--to=` now exits non-zero (`Wheels.UpgradeCheckFailed`) when the latest Wheels release can't be looked up, for example offline or when GitHub rate-limits the request. It used to print a warning and exit 0 without scanning anything, so a CI gate passed and `--strict` was bypassed. The message names the reason and suggests `--to=<version>`; `--format=json` prints a `{"success": false, "error": ...}` document before exiting non-zero (#3827)
- `wheels upgrade check` without `--to=` can reach GitHub again. The latest-release lookup was sent over the CLI's local dev-server transport, which speaks plain HTTP, so it could not reach `https://api.github.com` and every check failed with `Could not determine the latest Wheels version from GitHub`. It now uses the CLI's HTTPS client; calls to the local dev server are unchanged (#3854)
- `wheels upgrade check --offline` (and `WHEELS_OFFLINE=1`) no longer calls GitHub to look up the latest release. `upgrade check` now accepts `--offline`, and the HTTP client that `wheels packages` and `upgrade check` use refuses requests while offline mode is on, so without `--to=<version>` the check fails with the offline message and asks for a target version instead of reaching the network. With `--to` it scans as before (#3871)
- `wheels deploy` and `wheels deploy setup` now build and push the image before deploying it, as the guides describe. They log in to the registry on this machine and on every host (the password from `.kamal/secrets` travels only on stdin), run `docker buildx build --push`, then pull on the hosts. Pass `--skip-push` when the image for that version is already pushed. If no registry password resolves, the login is skipped with a warning (#3874)
- kamal-proxy now receives the proxy `host` (`--host`) and `ssl` (`--tls`) settings and the health-check interval, and the health-check interval and timeout are sent as durations (`3s`); TLS and host-based routing were silently dropped before (#3874)
- `wheels deploy rollback` sends the proxy cutover only to roles that run kamal-proxy, and stops the other versions on every host, so a rollback no longer fails on worker hosts or leaves old and restored containers running side by side (#3874)
- `--destination=<name>` now fails when `config/deploy.<name>.yml` does not exist, instead of silently deploying the base configuration (#3874)
- `wheels destroy <Name> --force` and `wheels destroy model <Name> --force` no longer generate a drop-table migration when there was no model to remove. That migration dropped a table that was never created, so the next `wheels migrate latest` failed.
- Package tarballs built on macOS, which carry `._*` AppleDouble metadata files, now install with `wheels packages add`. Those metadata files are skipped and never copied into `vendor/` (#3875)
- `wheels packages update` and `wheels packages add --force` keep the installed package when moving the verified new version into `vendor/` fails, for example on a full disk or a permissions error. The installed copy is renamed aside, restored if the move fails, and removed once the new copy is in place (#3826)
- `whereBetween()` on a date column no longer fails on Oracle (ORA-01843); each bound is now handled like a `where()` comparison, typed by the column (#3877)

### Security

- The CLI can now prove its dev server with an application-level challenge, as well as OS socket introspection. `wheels start` gives each server a per-start token, readable only by the starting OS user, in its registry directory (`<LUCLI_HOME>/servers/<name>/wheels-cli.token`). Before a command sends anything secret, the CLI checks on that same connection that the server can answer an HMAC challenge bound to the connection's addresses and ports. That lets state-changing commands work where `/proc`, `lsof` and `netstat` can't see the listener. A wrong answer fails closed. A framework without the challenge is verified through the OS as before. No token is issued on Windows, or when the registry directory is group- or world-writable. Port-forwarded setups (`docker-proxy`, `ssh -L`) can't pass the check, just as OS introspection can't prove them (#3769)
- With `set(csrfStore="cookie")`, the authenticity token is now compared exactly and in constant time (`$secureCompare()`), instead of with CFML `==`, which ignores case and stops at the first differing character. The default session store (`CsrfVerifyToken()`) is unchanged. (#3825)
- The password-less `/wheels` development endpoints (the GUI pages, docs viewer, test runners, and the deprecated HTTP MCP) now require the request's `Host` header to name the local machine, and reject a non-loopback `X-Forwarded-For` hop, before any handler runs. This is a Host-level gate at the public-component dispatch: it does **not** check the socket address, so a Wheels dev server reached through a Docker port mapping (where the socket source is the bridge gateway, not loopback) keeps working. `localhost`, any `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, and `[::1]` are accepted by default; add other local names with `set(devToolsAllowedHosts="my.host")`. The mutating endpoints keep their existing loopback-socket and CSRF/password checks on top. These endpoints remain blocked entirely in production. (#3850)
- The deprecated HTTP MCP endpoint (`/wheels/mcp`) is now read-only: only the read-only `analyze` tool can be listed or called there. Mutating and command-executing tools (`generate`, `migrate`, `test`, `validate`, `server`, `reload`, `develop`) are refused with a message pointing to the canonical stdio MCP server (`wheels mcp wheels`). This removes an unauthenticated file-write path that was reachable over the development-only HTTP surface (including via a GET whose query promoted it to a POST). The stdio MCP server keeps all tools. (#3849)
- The migrator create-migration endpoint (`/wheels/migrator/templates`) now enforces the same localhost-only, no-forwarded-client, and anti-CSRF request-header checks as the migrator command endpoint. The three gate helpers are factored into a shared `vendor/wheels/public/migrator/_guard.cfm` include so both dev-tool endpoints stay in lockstep, and the create GUI now sends the `X-Wheels-Csrf-Token` header with its request. (#3837)
- The migrator template-create endpoint no longer reflects an unknown template name into its HTML response. `createMigration` now allow-lists the requested template against the snippet files that actually exist (rejecting unknown names, embedded markup, and path-traversal attempts with a message that lists the valid templates instead of echoing the input), and the result is HTML-encoded at output. This closes a reflected-HTML/XSS path in the development-only migrator GUI. (#3845)
- The Wheels CLI now validates the names and values it turns into paths and commands. `wheels deploy` accepts a version (`--version`/`--release`, `rollback <version>`) and a destination only as 1-128 letters, digits, dots, underscores or hyphens, and writes audit log entries quoted. Deploy config load also validates the app and accessory images and every server and accessory host, and service, role and accessory names no longer accept a trailing line feed. Values resolved from `.kamal/secrets`, and any `${VAR}` interpolated into `env.clear`, are redacted from every dry-run and printed deploy command and from remote-failure messages; loading a config that interpolates into `env.clear` warns and recommends `env.secret`, because `env.clear` values travel on the `docker run` command line of every host. `wheels deploy init` creates `.kamal/secrets` owner-only (0600), adds it to `.gitignore`, never overwrites an existing one, and its template recommends `$(cmd)` and `${VAR}` lookups instead of literal values. `wheels packages` accepts only plain package names, resolves every install, removal and cache path directly under `vendor/` or the cache directory, and requires `--yes` for `remove`. Package tarballs are extracted into a staging directory and moved into `vendor/<name>/` only when they contain exactly that one directory with a `package.json` and no links; a `--force` reinstall keeps the installed package until the new one has downloaded and verified. `wheels generate` validates every name it writes into generated code (model, controller, action, view, helper and helper function, migration, route, test, policy, `generate auth` model and `generate admin` model, plus the names `generate admin` reads back from model introspection) and refuses to write any file outside the project, including through an existing symlinked file or directory; `wheels destroy` likewise refuses to delete or rewrite through a symlink that points outside the project. Model, table, property and association names (`--belongsTo`/`--hasMany`/`--hasOne`) and property types must be plain identifiers (letters, digits and underscores, starting with a letter, up to 128 characters), enum values may also contain hyphens, and `wheels new` accepts an app name only as letters, digits, underscores and hyphens starting with a letter. Route names may also contain hyphens, and hyphens in migration and property names become underscores (`create-users-table` → `create_users_table`, `display-name` → `display_name`) with a note. Scaffolded and admin views now HTML-encode record data: `wheels generate scaffold` printed the record's display field (for example the title) unencoded in the show page's `<h1>` and other fields unencoded on the index page, so a record holding markup ran it in the browser of anyone viewing it. `wheels generate admin` printed its index and show fields outside `<cfoutput>` (as literal text) and unencoded. In apps already generated with `wheels generate scaffold`, wrap the `<h1>` value in `app/views/<plural>/show.cfm` and the field values in `app/views/<plural>/index.cfm` in `encodeForHTML()`, for example `<h1>#encodeForHTML(post.title)#</h1>`. Regenerate admin views with `wheels generate admin <Model> --force`. `wheels generate --dry-run` (including `generate admin`) now creates no directories and writes no files, and the error for a path outside the project no longer shows absolute paths. With the RustCFML engine, `wheels stop`, `wheels start` and the server status check only act on the recorded process when it is still this project's RustCFML server: after a crash left the state file behind, a reused process ID could belong to an unrelated program, which `wheels stop` killed. A recorded process that is no longer running is forgotten; one that is running but isn't this project's RustCFML server is kept and reported by process ID on `wheels stop` and status, and `wheels start` replaces the record. `wheels destroy` names are now checked like `wheels generate` names (a quote or `../` in the name used to end up in the generated drop-table migration). ([GHSA-cghp-86c6-6fjc](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-cghp-86c6-6fjc))
- Error emails no longer go to an address derived from the request. The framework used to default `errorEmailAddress` to `webmaster@` plus the last two labels of the Host header of whichever request started the application, and production turns `sendEmailOnError` on. So a request with a crafted Host at a cold start could redirect every later error email to a domain of the requester's choosing. `errorEmailAddress` now defaults to `""`, and with no recipient configured no error email is sent: Wheels writes a one-time warning to `wheels.log` instead. **Apps that relied on the default must set `errorEmailAddress` (or `errorEmailToAddress`) explicitly.** The sender falls back to the recipient when `errorEmailFromAddress` is not set. ([GHSA-8gcv-3v9h-7gj2](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-8gcv-3v9h-7gj2))
- Values passed to dynamic finders (`findOneByEmail()`, `findAllByLastName()`, `findOrCreateBy*()`), the query builder's `where(property, value)` and `where(property, operator, value)`, `findByKey()`, `updateByKey()` and `deleteByKey()` on string and UUID keys, and `validatesUniquenessOf()` are now bound as query parameters. These APIs used to quote the value into a WHERE string and parse that string again, so a value containing a quote could change the shape of the WHERE clause, for example to match rows other than the ones asked for, or to pass a uniqueness check it should fail. Each value is now bound as a single parameter whatever it contains, and a value with an apostrophe (`O'Brien`) still matches as before. `whereBetween()` now applies each bound as a typed comparison. Numeric columns were not affected. ([GHSA-96rm-h22v-925x](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-96rm-h22v-925x))
- IP-based debug access (`set(allowIPBasedDebugAccess=true)` with `debugAccessIPs`) now grants the debug GUI and error details to the allowed client's own request only. It used to switch `showErrorInformation`, `showDebugInformation` and `enablePublicComponent` on in the shared `application.wheels` scope for the length of that request, so a concurrent request from any other client could render its error page with full exception detail. The grant now lives in the request scope (`$applyIPDebugAccess()`), and every framework check reads it through `$get()`. Apps with the setting off (the default) are unaffected. **Apps that use IP-based debug access must also update `public/Application.cfc`:** in `onRequestStart()`, replace the `// IP-based access to public Component/debug GUI` block (the `debugIPAccess` bookkeeping and the `// Conditional override for allowed IPs` `if` that follows it; in apps generated by `wheels new` 4.x, the body of `$applyDebugIpAccessOverrides()` and its call) with `application.wo.$applyIPDebugAccess();`. In `onRequestEnd()`, replace `application.wheels.showDebugInformation &&` with `application.wo.$get("showDebugInformation") &&`. Until you make that edit, upgrading alone still protects you: Wheels snapshots these three flags at application start and serves the snapshot (plus the per-request grant) for them, ignoring the per-request writes the old block makes to the shared scope, and logs a one-time warning to update `Application.cfc`. The bundled debug-bar template is also gated on the per-request grant, so a concurrent request never sees another client's debug bar. ([GHSA-8r22-vwcc-v55m](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/security/advisories/GHSA-8r22-vwcc-v55m))

---

# [4.1.1](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/releases/tag/v4.1.1) => 2026-09-28

### Added

- `WHEELS_SERVER_FALLBACK=false`, in the environment or the project's `.env`, stops read-side CLI commands (`wheels db status`, `db version`, `migrate info`, …) from falling back to whatever server answers on a common port (8080, 60000, 3000, 8500) when this project's own port isn't serving. Without it, such a command can silently read a different app's server (#3693)

### Changed

- CLI boolean flags accept only `true` or `false`: `--force=yes`, `--force=1`, or any other value now fails with `Wheels.InvalidArguments` instead of being read loosely (a bare `--force` still means true, and `--no-force` false). Numeric options reject non-numbers, and a string option given as a bare flag (e.g. `--to` with no value) is an error (#2963)
- CLI commands and MCP tools reject unknown named arguments and named arguments with no value (an empty MCP string arrives as null) with `Wheels.InvalidArguments` naming the argument, instead of ignoring them. The global `--offline` flag is still accepted by every command (#2963)
- `deploy` is no longer exposed as an MCP tool: it runs SSH, image pushes, and container restarts on remote hosts, so it stays CLI-only like `new`, `start`, and `setup` (#2963)
- The MCP `test` tool now prints a warning when its `timeout` is not a positive number, e.g. `Warning: ignoring invalid timeout "x"; using 900s`. The run still goes ahead with the default, as before; it just no longer falls back silently (#2963)
- `wheels generate app <name> --offline` now passes `--offline` through to `wheels new`, which skips its update check; before, generate consumed the flag and `new` reset it (#2963)
- **Upgrade note (4.0.x → 4.1.x) — validation `condition`/`unless` expressions now fail closed.** A `condition`/`unless` string that the evaluator cannot parse (a compound `&&`/`||` expression, or a function call with arguments outside the read-only whitelist: `StructKeyExists`, `IsNull`, `IsNumeric`, `IsSimpleValue`, `IsStruct`, `IsArray`, `IsBoolean`, `IsDate`, `Len`, `ListFind`, `ListFindNoCase`, `ListContains`, `ListContainsNoCase`) now throws `Wheels.InvalidValidationCondition` — an HTTP 500 on the save that triggers it. 4.0.x caught the same parse failure and returned `false`, which *skipped* the validation entirely: app authors should treat a 4.1 upgrade as the moment previously-inert validations start being enforced, and audit any `condition`/`unless` that names a property inside a function call. Two real cases surfaced on one production app during the 4.1 upgrade: `condition="StructKeyExists(this, 'requestFor') && this.requestFor == 'Engine Part'"` (500 on every save) and `condition="ListFind('24,32,63,67,117,167,191', this.countryId)"` (silently inert — the validation never ran). Fail-closed is deliberate: silently skipping a validation hides data-integrity holes, which is exactly how both of those survived for years. `wheels-dev/wheels#3634` (#3634)
- CI and Linux packages take the LuCLI runtime from one pin, `tools/lucli.json`, and verify each download against a pinned SHA-256 and the version it reports; the Homebrew and Scoop updaters read the same pin. Once the channels ship the fork runtime (with the module-timeout fix, LuCLI #130), a terminal `wheels test --timeout=<seconds>` reaches the test command; `--test-timeout` keeps working on every runtime.
- Update the experimental RustCFML engine pin to v0.691.0 with a Linux-verified baseline; keep JVM-only system-property expectations capability-aware

### Performance

- Flash locking is now scoped to where the data lives: session-stored flash takes a per-session lock instead of one application-wide lock, so users no longer queue behind each other on every request, and cookie-stored flash (request-local) is no longer locked at all. The end-of-request `$flashClear()` also skips its exclusive write when the session flash is already empty. Under a 128-user load test this lifted throughput 10–14%.

### Fixed

- `wheels generate` accepts an explicit `dry-run=false` (sent by MCP clients as `--no-dry-run`) instead of failing with `Unknown flag`, and a failed dry run no longer leaves later `generate` calls in the same MCP session writing nothing. `wheels analyze` rejects an unknown target instead of silently analyzing everything (#2963)
- Fixed-choice CLI and MCP parameters now reject values outside their set instead of silently falling back: `routes` `format`, `seed` `mode` (an unknown mode used to generate rows), `test` `reporter`, `analyze` `target`, `destroy` `type` (which also exits non-zero now), the `db`/`packages`/`upgrade` verbs, `upgrade` `format`, `migrate` `action`, and the `generate`/`create` types. MCP tool schemas advertise the valid values as a JSON Schema `enum`, from the same `ArgSpec` declaration the parser enforces (#2963)
- `wheels migrate diff` on a missing model and a refused `wheels migrate rename-system-tables` now exit non-zero, and `wheels test` exits non-zero on any runner response without test counts (such as a missing-datasource error) (#2963)
- `migrate` and `create` MCP tool calls now honour every advertised parameter. Both rebuilt argv from named keys in struct order, so `{action: "info", "dry-run": false}` failed as `Unknown migration action: --no-dry-run`, `forget`/`pretend` lost their `version`, `diff` lost its `model`, and `{name, type}` reached `create` as `Unknown create type: --name=x`. A later MCP call no longer stays offline because an earlier one passed `offline`, and a bare `--name`-style flag no longer binds the literal `true` as a positional value (#2963)
- MCP tool calls now honour positional parameters. LuCLI's MCP server passes every `tools/call` argument by name, and `ArgSpec.parse()` bound positionals only from typed tokens, so advertised properties such as `destroy`'s `type`/`name` and `analyze`'s `target` were silently ignored. `db`, `packages`, and `reload` also advertise real input schemas now, built from the same `ArgSpec` their parsers use (#2963)
- `wheels test` (and the MCP `test` tool) now exits non-zero when the test runner refuses before running anything, e.g. a failed test-database migration. The runner answers with a `{success: false, error, message}` document that has no test counts, which the CLI used to read as a clean run; it now prints the runner's explanation and fails (#2963)
- Model validations: `condition`/`unless` expressions that combine clauses with `&&`/`||` no longer throw `Unsupported operator in condition`. `$evaluateConditionString()` now splits a compound boolean at the top level (quote- and paren-aware, with `&&` binding tighter than `||`) and evaluates each side with the existing single-clause machinery, and it recognises a small whitelist of read-only function calls with arguments, including the idiomatic `StructKeyExists(this, 'property')` and the list predicates `ListFind`/`ListFindNoCase`/`ListContains`/`ListContainsNoCase` (so legacy conditions of the shape `ListFind('a,b,c', this.id)` keep working instead of newly throwing). Previously any such expression fell through to the three-token `$evaluateLogicalExpression()` parser, which read the wrong token as the operator; because 4.1.0 made that path fail closed, the symptom changed from a silently skipped validation to a thrown `Wheels.InvalidValidationCondition` (HTTP 500 on save). The widened grammar also accepts a leading `!` on any supported clause (`!StructKeyExists(this, 'x')`, including inside a compound expression), parenthesised groups (`(a || b) && c`), and symbolic operators (`==`, `!=`) within a compound clause. A whitelisted function argument that names a property absent from the instance now resolves to an empty value instead of throwing, so `StructKeyExists(this, 'a') || StructKeyExists(this, 'b')` short-circuits as written rather than 500ing on the first clause. A whitelisted function called with the wrong number of arguments reports an arity error rather than being described as unsupported. Note: a function-call condition outside the whitelist still throws here whereas 4.1.0 silently returned false — see the PR description for that remaining upgrade hazard (#3634)
- `paginationLinks()` no longer adopts an ambiguous ambient route name, so a paginated `index` action reached through the catch-all `wildcard()` route (the default scaffolded routes) no longer throws `Wheels.RouteNotFound` — which rendered as a whole-page 404 in production, with nothing logged, because the route error path aborts. The ambient `request.wheels.params.route` name from the #942 fix is now adopted only when it resolves to a single named route; the `wildcard` name registers one candidate per method x pattern, and `paginationLinks()` supplies neither a method nor the route's path variables, so `$findRoute()` could never disambiguate it. Falling through to the controller/action URL produces the same link the wildcard route serves (#3638)
- `paginationLinks(route="")` is now a real opt-out: an explicitly empty `route` is treated as "no route" instead of being passed to `$findRoute()`, which threw `Wheels.RouteNotFound` for the empty name and gave applications no way to suppress the ambient-route adoption in the meantime (#3638)
- `PackageLoader` resolves a package's entry-point CFC deterministically instead of taking whichever `*.cfc` the directory listing returns first. A package with more than one root CFC now declares it with `"main": "Sentry"` in `package.json`, or is matched by the package name (`wheels-seo-suite` → `SeoSuite.cfc`); anything still ambiguous fails loudly and names the candidates instead of loading the wrong component (#3639)
- `PackageLoader` now verifies that each package mapping alias it registers is actually resolvable by the engine (`ExpandPath()` probe) and reports the ones the engine ignored through `getUnresolvedMappings()`, plus one boot log line. Lucee ignores `application.mappings` written after application start, so a package referencing a sibling alias used to fail at first use with a bare "could not find component" while the loader reported the alias as registered (#3639)
- A `.env` value of numeric 1 is no longer coerced to the boolean `true`. The generator template, the bundled examples and the `config dump` / `get settings` / `reload` CLI commands all tested booleans with `==`, which Lucee compares NUMERICALLY — `"1.0" == "true"` is true — so a numeric setting such as a sample rate of `1.0` arrived as a boolean and silently took its default instead (#3641)
- Adobe ColdFusion 2025 no longer breaks the framework's own debug surfaces: the debug bar's complexity panel, the development error page's code frame, the docs viewer's extended examples, and legacy RocketUnit failure messages all called `HtmlEditFormat()`, a BIF Adobe CF 2025 removed (`Variable HTMLEDITFORMAT is undefined`). Each call now goes through `$encodeForDisplayText()`, which keeps using the BIF where the engine provides it (it preserves `/`, so file paths stay readable) and falls back to an equivalent local escaper otherwise. On the compat matrix the complexity panel alone produced twelve `Variable HTMLEDITFORMAT is undefined` errors on every adobe2025 leg, and a new structural guard (`HtmlEditFormatGuardSpec`) fails the suite if a direct BIF call reappears anywhere under `vendor/wheels` (#3645)
- `create()` with an explicit primary key now works on SQL Server. `model("Post").create(id = 41, ...)` used to fail with `Cannot insert explicit value for identity column ... when IDENTITY_INSERT is set to OFF`, while every other supported database accepted it. When an INSERT's column list carries the primary key, the SQL Server adapter now sends it in one batch with `SET IDENTITY_INSERT <table> ON` before and `OFF` after, both guarded by a `sys.identity_columns` lookup so a natural or UUID key stays a plain INSERT. The OFF runs even when the INSERT hits a constraint violation, so a failed insert cannot leave the setting on for the pooled connection (#3647)
- Timestamp values read back from the database are normalized before the framework measures elapsed time against them, fixing Oracle on Adobe CF: the Oracle JDBC driver returns TIMESTAMP columns as `oracle.sql.TIMESTAMP`, which is not a `java.util.Date`, so `IsDate()` rejected it — `RateLimiter`'s database-backed token bucket read every bucket as permanently empty or full, and `JobWorker.getMonitorData()` returned a non-date `oldestPending`. The new `$normalizeDbTimestamp()` helper accepts a CFML date, plain or boxed epoch milliseconds (Adobe + sqlite-jdbc), a `java.util.Date`, Oracle's `oracle.sql.TIMESTAMP` (bridged through `timestampValue()`) and fractional-second datetime strings, and replaces the SQLite-only inline epoch conversion in `JobWorker` (#3649)
- `insertAll()` no longer fails on Oracle under Lucee with `ORA-03048: SQL reserved word 'ROWID' is not syntactically valid following '... FROM dual RETURNING'`. A cfquery `result` attribute makes Lucee request generated keys from the driver, and the Oracle driver implements that by appending `RETURNING ROWID INTO ?` to every INSERT, which Oracle rejects after the `INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM dual UNION ALL` bulk shape. Nothing on the bulk paths reads the result or a key, so `insertAll()` and `upsertAll()` now run their statements without one (`$performQuery(..., $captureResult = false)`); the Lucee + Oracle core suite goes from four `bulkOperationsSpec` errors to green (#3653)
- Table names containing `$` (e.g. `admin$users`) work again: 4.1.0's `$dbinfo()` identifier check rejected them and stopped the app from initializing. `$` is now accepted after the first character there and in the AutoMigrator, ORDER BY / GROUP BY dot-notation, and QueryBuilder identifier checks, so a paginated `findAll()` on a `$` table works too; those identifier checks still reject quotes, `#`, and a leading `$` (#3669)
- When Wheels fails to initialize, the minimal "Application Error" page no longer points at a log entry that does not exist: `onError()`'s fallback now writes the failure to `wheels.log` (type `error`) before rendering, walking `RootCause`/`Cause` so the real type, message, detail and first tag-context frame are logged even when Adobe CF wraps them in an event-handler exception. The page itself stays minimal and now names `wheels.log`. Existing apps must copy the change into their own `public/Application.cfc`: replace `$renderMinimalError()` and add `$logStartupFailure()`, `$startupFailureCause()`, `$startupFailureField()` and `$startupFailureFrame()` from the current `wheels new` template, and pass `arguments.EventName ?: ""` as the second argument at both `$renderMinimalError()` calls in `onError()` (#3671)
- `where` clauses no longer split on an uppercase `AND` / `OR` inside an identifier: a table-qualified condition such as `ORDER_AND_ITEMS.id = 1` (or a paginated `findAll()` on such a model) resolved a bogus `_ITEMS.id` property and threw `Wheels.ColumnNotFound`, and a condition starting with a property like `ORDER_NO` lost its leading `OR`. Only standalone `AND` / `OR` keywords now separate conditions (#3675)
- `wheels test --test-timeout=<seconds>` sets the test run's time budget from the terminal. The documented `wheels test --timeout=<seconds>` never reached the command there: LuCLI's own global `--timeout` option takes it (a non-integer is rejected by LuCLI, and a valid value was silently swallowed), so only `WHEELS_TEST_TIMEOUT` worked. MCP clients keep passing `timeout`; precedence is the explicit value, then `WHEELS_TEST_TIMEOUT`, then 900, and a value that is not a positive number warns and falls back as before (#3678)
- `wheels test` again finds the project's own running server when the project directory's name differs from the `name` in its `lucee.json` (worktrees, clones into a differently named directory, edited `lucee.json` names). The server registry now resolves the name the way LuCLI registers it — `lucee.json`'s `name`, falling back to the directory basename — and also accepts any registration whose `.project-path` points at the project. `wheels start`'s "already running" / "registered to a different project" checks use the same resolution (#3679)
- `wheels stop` now forwards `--name=<server>`, `--config=<file>` and `--all` to `wheels server stop` (the space form `--name <server>` works too); it previously dropped them, so a project with more than one registered server could not be stopped with `wheels stop` (#3680)
- An immediate `wheels test` re-run after a timed-out run no longer collides with the abandoned run still executing server-side. The core (`/wheels/core/tests`) and app (`/wheels/app/tests`) runners used to treat any request arriving mid-run as a re-entrant sub-request and skip their shared lock, so two suites ran at once against the same test database. An overlapping run now waits on the lock and then runs normally. Only sub-requests that echo the in-progress run's token (`wheelsTestRun`) skip the lock. The CLI's timeout message now says the abandoned run is still executing (#3683)
- On H2 (the 1.3.172 driver Lucee bundles), a query builder `offset()` without `limit()` returned rows in the wrong order. The builder asks for "no limit" as `LIMIT 2147483647`, and H2 1.3.172 mis-sorts once `LIMIT + OFFSET` passes the 32-bit maximum. The H2 adapter now clamps the limit so the sum stays at that maximum, which also covers an explicit huge `limit()` with an offset (#3684)
- The repository's demo app now keeps its H2 database under its own root (`db/h2/wheels-dev.h2.db`). Its datasource used the relative path `jdbc:h2:file:./db/h2/wheels-dev`, which resolves against the server process's working directory: for a LuCLI server that is the Lucee Express install, so every demo-app server on the machine, from any checkout or worktree, shared (and locked) one file at `~/.wheels/express/<lucee-version>/db/h2/wheels-dev.h2.db` (or `~/.lucli/express/<lucee-version>/db/h2/` for servers started with a plain `lucli` binary). To keep existing demo data, stop the demo servers and copy that file into `db/h2/` of the checkout that should own it; otherwise the demo database starts empty. Apps created with `wheels new` already anchor their SQLite/H2 paths to the app root and are unaffected (#3689)
- `insertAll()` works on BoxLang with Oracle. BoxLang asks the driver for generated keys on every INSERT, and for the `INSERT ... SELECT` statement that Oracle bulk inserts use, the driver failed with `ORA-17009: Closed statement` (inside a transaction, only `Error closing connection: Connection is closed`). The statement now runs as an anonymous PL/SQL block, so no key request is made (#3715)
- With Oracle on BoxLang and Adobe 2023, model finders returned datetime columns as raw `oracle.sql.TIMESTAMP` driver objects, which `DateFormat()`, `DateCompare()`, output, concatenation and `SerializeJSON()` could not handle. They are now CFML dates, to the millisecond, as on every other engine and database (#3719)
- `wheels generate controller Pages index` (and `wheels generate view`) now write the plain view template for `index`/`show`/`new`/`edit` actions instead of the scaffold CRUD views, which linked to resource routes such as `newPage` that a plain controller never registers — so `GET /pages/index` threw `Wheels.RouteNotFound` and returned 404. Scaffolds still get the CRUD views (#3722)
- `wheels generate model <Name> --belongsTo=<Parent>` (CLI, `g` alias and the MCP `generate` tool) now adds the foreign-key column to the generated migration, as `scaffold` and `api-resource` already did — `<parent>_id` when `useUnderscoreReferenceColumns=true` (the `wheels new` default), `<parent>Id` otherwise. The migration previously had no FK column, so the association's default foreign key named a missing column and `wheels seed` failed. A column you list yourself, in either shape, is not duplicated (#3723)
- Wheels now initializes each `request.wheels` key when it is missing, even if `request.wheels` already exists. On Adobe CF 2025 the engine raises an error while loading the app's datasources during startup; the app's `onError` then created `request.wheels`, Wheels skipped initializing the request, and the first request after a restart failed with `Element WHEELS.HTTPREQUESTDATA.HEADERS is undefined in REQUEST`. What `onError` recorded is kept (#3726)
- On Adobe ColdFusion 2025 without the optional graphqlclient package, the first request after a start no longer fails with HTTP 500 because of the engine's pre-start "The graphqlclient package is not installed." notice. `onError()` recognizes that exact notice, logs it to `wheels.log`, and lets the request continue. A clean first request also needs the framework fix for #3730. Existing apps on Adobe 2025 that do not install graphqlclient must add the guard to their own `public/Application.cfc` by pasting the block at the top of `onError()` from the current template. Installing the package (`cfpm install graphqlclient`) is the alternative (#3726)
- The first request after a cold start no longer fails with HTTP 500 on Adobe ColdFusion 2023 and 2025. `onApplicationStart` failed with `java.util.EmptyStackException` at `NeoPageContext.popSuperScope` while creating the internal `wheels.Public` component. The cause is an Adobe engine bug, triggered the first time a Global mixin creates a Global subclass. Wheels now works around it with no application changes. Health checks and readiness retries usually hid it by absorbing that first request. ([#3730](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/issues/3730))
- Background jobs: `enqueue()`, `enqueueIn()` and `enqueueAt()` now persist the job class with the casing of the file on disk. On a case-insensitive filesystem (macOS, Windows, or a macOS checkout bind-mounted into Docker) a miscased `new app.jobs.sendwelcomeemailjob()` resolves, and Adobe CF reported its metadata name with the caller's casing, so the row named a class a case-sensitive Linux worker could not instantiate (`Wheels.JobClassNotFound`). The name is now canonicalized segment by segment against the directory listing before it is written (#3731)
- Migrator: a migration whose `up()` throws is no longer silent. `migrateTo()` folds the failure into the string it returns rather than throwing, so the application-start auto-migrate path (`autoMigrateDatabase`) discarded it entirely — one unrunnable migration stalled every migration queued behind it while the app booted healthy and nothing was logged. The migrator now records the failed step (direction, version, file, engine message and detail) to the `wheels` log at the point of failure, so every entry point sees it
- Routing: `$cgiScope()` now actually strips a leading `/index.cfm/` from `path_info`. The cleanup discarded the result of `Replace()`, so on servers that report the front controller in the path (e.g. IIS returning a blank `cgi.path_info`, where the fallback copies `request_uri` such as `/index.cfm/users/list`) the router received `index.cfm/users/list` and matched no route. The strip is anchored to the leading segment and case-insensitive, so a later segment like `/docs/myindex.cfm/x` is left untouched.
- `wheels destroy <model>` no longer generates a drop-table migration that fails with "The parameter [name] to function [dropTable] is required": the template now emits `dropTable(name=...)`, and `Migration.dropTable()` accepts `tableName` as an alias of `name`, so migrations already generated by earlier `wheels destroy` runs now migrate without edits
- The `down()` of the migration from `wheels generate snippets soft-delete` no longer calls `removeIndex()` with an unsupported `columnNames` argument; it passes the explicit `indexName` that `up()` creates
- `linkTo(method="delete")` now adds `rel="nofollow"` instead of the misspelled `rel="no-follow"`, which crawlers do not recognise as a link type — so delete links were never actually marked as not-to-be-followed. A caller-supplied `rel` is still preserved (e.g. `rel="external nofollow"`)
- The `wheels mcp wheels` tool list no longer advertises `dbmigrate`. It is the historical alias for `migrate` (like `d` for destroy and `g` for generate, which were already hidden), and it has no `mcpToolSpecs()` entry, so MCP clients saw it as a no-argument twin of `migrate`. The stdio `tools/list` on the pinned LuCLI runtime (0.6.1) now carries one entry per command, with the declared input schema on every ArgSpec-backed tool (#2963)
- View helpers: `paginationLinks(prependOnFirst=false)` no longer emits a mangled first item (e.g. a stray `-item">1</li>`) when the first page's `prependToPage` markup differs in length from the raw argument — the current page with `addActiveClassToPrependedParent=true` (which injects `active `), markup whose attributes are stripped by sanitization, or `encode=true` markup whose numeric entities are decoded. The helper now strips exactly the prefix it emitted for the first page, mirroring how `appendOnLast=false` already uses the sanitized suffix.
- Pagination helpers: `params` (string or struct) is no longer dropped when `pageNumberAsParam=false`. `nextPageLink()`, `previousPageLink()`, `firstPageLink()`, `lastPageLink()`, `pageNumberLinks()` and `paginationNav()` (including `viewStyle` presets) now keep the query string on route-segment page links, so `nextPageLink(route="postsPaged", pageNumberAsParam=false, params="sort=title")` on `posts/page/[page]` renders `/posts/page/3?sort=title` instead of `/posts/page/3`, matching legacy `paginationLinks()`.
- Query builder: `offset()` is now applied. It was stored but never passed to the finder, so `model("User").orderBy("id").limit(10).offset(20).get()` returned rows 1-10. `get()`/`findAll()` and `first()`/`findOne()` now emit SQL `OFFSET` (with `limit()` as the row count, or every remaining row when no limit is set) and default to primary-key order when no `orderBy()` is given; on SQL Server the limit is clamped to the rows left after the offset so the last page is correct. `count()`, `exists()`, `updateAll()`, `deleteAll()`, `findEach()` and `findInBatches()` ignore the offset. `afterFind` callbacks now also run on query results fetched with an offset.
- Routing: with `URLRewriting="Off"`, `urlFor()` / `linkTo()` no longer double-encode custom route variables that end up in the query string. Route `blogs/[slug]` with `urlFor(route="blog", slug="a b")` now produces `slug=a+b` (the server receives `a b`) instead of `slug=a%2Bb` (the server received `a+b`); with HTML-attribute encoding (`linkTo`, `startFormTag`, …) the value is also HTML-encoded once instead of twice, which previously split the value on the `&` of the `&#x2b;` entity.
- Routing: typed constraint helpers (`whereNumber()`, `whereAlpha()`, `whereMatch()`, etc.) chained after an **unnamed** route with optional segments now constrain every variant that route registers. Previously only the last-registered (shortest) variant was inspected, so `.get(pattern="archive(/[year])", to="archive##index").whereNumber("year")` constrained nothing and `/archive/abc` dispatched with `year="abc"`, and `.get(pattern="posts/[id](.[format])", ...).whereNumber("id")` left `/posts/abc.json` unconstrained. Named routes were unaffected.
- `singularize()` no longer mangles ordinary words ending in `i`: `safari`, `spaghetti`, `pi` and `api` previously became `safarus`, `spaghettus`, `pus` and `apus` (and `virus`/`octopus` lost their trailing `s`) because the Latin-plural rule was written as the character class `([octop|vir])i$` instead of an alternation. The rule is now `(octop|vir|cact|radi|foc)(us|i)$`, so `octopi`, `viri`, `cacti`, `radii` and `foci` still singularize to their `-us` forms.
- Honor case-insensitive spec names as well as IDs in testSpecs filters; name filters no longer silently skip every spec.
- `wheels new` scaffolds ship an up-to-date `CLAUDE.md`: the template copy had drifted about 74 lines behind `docs/consumer-ai/CLAUDE.md` (missing auth, storage, DI and seeding guidance) and wrongly said `wheels dbmigrate diff` does not exist; `ship-consumer-docs.sh check` now fails when the two copies differ
- A model call made with `transaction = "none"` (or `false`) that throws no longer disables transaction handling for the rest of the request. `invokeWithTransaction()` sets the request's open-transaction marker for those modes too, and a throw from the invoked method skipped the reset, so every later `save()`/`create()`/`update()` in that request took the "already open" path and ran with no transaction, and `transaction = "rollback"` stopped rolling back. The commit/rollback branch already reset the marker on errors (#3302); the `none`/`false` branch now does the same
- Model: `updateAll(instantiate=true, validate=false)` now skips validations as documented. The instantiate path called each object's `update()` without forwarding `validate`, so validations always ran and records failing them were silently left unchanged (and excluded from the returned count).
- Model: `upsertAll()` no longer overwrites the `createdAt` of an existing row when a record conflicts on `uniqueBy`. The framework-stamped create timestamp was included in the `ON CONFLICT` / `ON DUPLICATE KEY` / `MERGE` update clause, so every upsert that hit an existing row reset its `createdAt` to now; it is now only written on insert while `updatedAt` is still refreshed. A `createdAt` you put in the records yourself is still written on conflict. H2's `MERGE ... KEY` form rewrites every column and is unchanged.
- Model validations: a leading `!` in a compound `condition`/`unless` expression now negates only the clause it prefixes, matching CFML precedence (`!` binds tighter than `&&`, which binds tighter than `||`). Previously `$evaluateConditionString()` stripped the `!` and inverted the whole remaining expression, so `!isNew() || this.force eq 1` was evaluated as `!(isNew() || this.force eq 1)` and the validation ran or was skipped on the wrong records. A negated parenthesised group such as `!(a || b)` still negates the whole group
- WebSocket upgrades to `/ws/<name>` now reach [lucee/extension-websocket](https://github.com/lucee/extension-websocket) (used by the `wheels-websockets` package at `/ws/wheels`) under the `wheels new` Tomcat rewrite rules and the `wheels deploy init` Dockerfile. Previously the front-controller catch-all rewrote the upgrade to `/index.cfm/ws/...` before Tomcat's WsFilter could match the endpoint, so the client got a Wheels 404 instead of a `101`. The new pass-through is gated on the `Upgrade: websocket` header, so ordinary HTTP routes under `/ws/` keep reaching the router. **Existing apps are not updated automatically** — `wheels start` only copies the template into a project that has no `rewrite.config`, and it copies the template bundled with the CLI you run, so deleting the file regenerates these lines only from a CLI release that includes this fix (on 4.1.0 and earlier it regenerates the old rules). Add the two lines — the `RewriteCond` and the rule — by hand to your project-root `rewrite.config`, just above the `# Route everything else through the front controller` catch-all; apps deployed with a Dockerfile from `wheels deploy init` need the same two lines added to the `printf` rule list before `'RewriteRule ^/(.*)$ /index.cfm/$1 [L]'`:
  ```
  RewriteCond %{HTTP:Upgrade} ^websocket$ [NC]
  RewriteRule ^/ws/.*$ - [L]
  ```
  CommandBox/Undertow (`urlrewrite.xml`) is unchanged: on Undertow the extension accepts the upgrade but never runs the listener, so there is no working WebSocket path there yet (#3676, wheels-dev/wheels-websockets#1)
- Migrations whose `up()` does its work with raw `queryExecute()` or `cfquery`, for example DDL against a second datasource, and then calls `announce()` are recorded in the migrator versions table again. 4.1.0 treated them as announce-only and never recorded them, so they re-ran on every migrate, and a re-run that fails (such as `CREATE TABLE` on an existing table) stalled every migration behind it. Only a migration that keeps the inherited `NOT IMPLEMENTED` placeholder `up()`/`down()` is left untracked now, and the migrator logs a warning when it skips one. After upgrading, a version 4.1.0 left unrecorded runs once more and is then recorded. If that re-run fails because its work is already applied, mark it as applied with `wheels migrate pretend <version> --yes`

### Security

- The Wheels CLI could send state-changing commands, console code and the application's reload password to a local server it had not verified as the current project's own, for example another application listening on the project's configured port or on a common development port. Commands that change state or evaluate code (`migrate` write actions, `seed`, `db reset`, `reload`, `console`, `jobs work`, `generate admin`, `coverage`, `test`, `browser test`) now require the server this project started with `wheels start` (or `wheels engines rustcfml start`), and refuse otherwise, naming the port. The server is verified as that registered process, the only process listening on its port, and, for every request, the process that accepted the CLI's connection before anything is sent on it. Requests go to the address the server binds rather than resolving `localhost`; `console` never falls back to probing ports; read-only commands that use an unverified server say so. The CLI now sends the reload password in an `X-Wheels-Reload-Password` request header instead of the URL query string, keeping it out of access and proxy logs. `public/Application.cfc` accepts the header alongside the existing `?password=` parameter. Existing apps should copy the header-mapping block (the `X-Wheels-Reload-Password` block near the top of `onRequestStart()`, before the reload lock) from the current template into the same place in their own `public/Application.cfc`; until they do, the CLI falls back to the query string for that project's own verified server only and prints a notice. `.env` detection now reads only a line-start `PORT` and `WHEELS_RELOAD_PASSWORD`/`RELOAD_PASSWORD`, not another key that ends the same way such as `DB_PORT` (GHSA-x3cm-2j3q-jgg4)
- `redirectTo(url=...)`, `redirectTo(back=true)` and `startFormTag()`'s CSRF-token check no longer accept an absolute or protocol-relative URL that puts the current host in front of another one as userinfo. `$isSafeRedirectUrl()` took the host as everything before the first `:`, `/`, `?` or `#`, so it read `https://<your-host>:x@evil.com/` as same-host. Browsers read `<your-host>:x` as credentials and go to `evil.com`. That let an open redirect through with `allowExternalRedirects=false`, and made `startFormTag()` send the authenticity token to that host. The check now isolates the URL's authority (host and port) first and rejects any URL whose authority contains userinfo (`@`).

---

# [4.1.0](https://github.com/wheels-dev/wheels/releases/tag/v4.1.0) => 2026-09-17

### Added

- `wheels upgrade check` now diffs app-owned template files (`public/Application.cfc`, `public/index.cfm`) against the CLI's bundled app template and reports any drift as an advisory — surfacing framework-side hardening (such as the Adobe teardown guards in `onError`/`onSessionEnd`) that a `vendor/wheels/`-only swap never delivers to existing apps (#3379)
- Added `enableSession()` — one-line session-auth wiring for `config/services.cfm`.
- Added `wheels migrate diff` (alias `dbmigrate diff`) — preview or `--write` AutoMigrator schema diffs with rename hints.
- Added `wheels generate --dry-run` — print would-be generated files without writing anything.
- Added offline mode (`--offline` / `WHEELS_OFFLINE=1`) — skips the CLI update check and fails fast on registry network calls.
- Added `boot` deploy config section (Kamal-compatible `limit`/`wait`).
- Added `wheels doctor` checks for legacy `wheels.Test` specs, legacy `plugins/`, and raw `params.` mass assignment.
- Fixed the debug bar showing `0.0.0-dev` on installs where `BuildInfo.cfc` ships unstamped (now falls back to the sibling manifest version).
- Added `Injector.toFactory()` — bind a name to a closure that builds the instance (receives the container, honors singleton/request-scoped flags).
- Added route `bindBy=` — bind a resource route's `:key` segment to a non-primary-key column via the parameterized dynamic finder (e.g. `bindBy="slug"`).
- Added `bcryptHash()`, `bcryptVerify()`, and `bcryptNeedsRehash()` global helpers — pure-CFML, OpenBSD/htpasswd/jBCrypt-compatible bcrypt password hashing with no Java objects, CFX, or external libraries. RustCFML, which ships `bcryptHash()`/`bcryptVerify()` as native builtins, uses those for the same API.
- Added a "Code Complexity" panel to the development debug bar that shows cyclomatic complexity per file in `app/` — computed statically (1 + decision points) and cached for the application lifetime, so it costs one scan per reload. Complements the maintainer-side complexity gate (`tools/code-quality/cfml-complexity.py` + `.github/workflows/code-quality.yml`) and the function-coverage tool (`tools/code-quality/cfml-coverage.py`); test coverage for CRAP scoring is a separate `wheels coverage` run.
- `set(massAssignmentStrict=true)` fail-closes mass assignment when a model has neither `accessibleProperties()` nor `protectedProperties()`; the default remains open for compatibility
- `linkTo(sanitizeHref=true)` blanks caller-supplied `javascript:` / `data:` hrefs. Default remains `false` (no public-behavior change)
- Added a `wheels coverage` command that instruments `app/` with function-level coverage counters, runs the app test suite against the running server, and reports a CRAP ranking (cyclomatic complexity × test coverage) of your most change-risky files. The instrumentation is reverted automatically afterward. Pairs with the debug bar's Complexity panel: complexity is static and always visible; `wheels coverage` adds the test-coverage half of the CRAP score on demand.
- Development error page now includes a Copy button that copies a JSON dump of the exception (type, message, suggested action, file and line, source snippet, and app/framework stack frames) to the clipboard for pasting into a coding agent (#3552)
- CLI generators (`model`, `scaffold`, `api-resource`) accept Rails-style column size modifiers (`title:string{50}`, `price:decimal{10,2}`) and emit the custom `limit` / `precision` / `scale` in the generated migration
- CLI generators emit `validatesLengthOf(property=..., maximum=N)` on the generated model when a string-like property (`string`, `varchar`, `text`, `binary`) uses a `{N}` size modifier
- `wheels console` now has real line editing: ← → move the cursor, ↑ ↓ recall history (persisted across sessions in `~/.wheels/console_history`), Home/End jump to the line ends. Previously arrow keys printed raw escape sequences. Piped sessions (`printf ... | wheels console`) are unchanged
- Debug bar: Copy buttons on the message blocks worth pasting into a coding agent — the reload-refused notice, configuration warnings, deprecations, the Warnings section, failing migration output and failing test output. Each copies plain text behind a short header rather than raw markup
- Debug bar: tab labels collapse to icons below the width where they stop fitting, with the tab name shown in a hover tooltip, instead of clipping the close button off-screen
- Debug bar: new Routes, Migrate, Tests and Packages tabs. Routes lists the app's route table, Migrate shows migration state with hold-to-confirm rollback/redo/reset, Tests runs the app suite and can re-run a single bundle, and Packages lists installed packages plus any that failed to load. Each is lazy-loaded on open, and the Migrator/Tests/Packages cards were removed from the Tools tab since the tabs supersede them
- The Linux `.deb` / `.rpm` packages now ship the offline documentation bundle and unpack it into `~/.wheels/docs/<version>/` on first run, so the guides and API reference work without an internet connection. The bundle is also mirrored into an app's `public/wheels-docs/` when `wheels` is run from inside a project, since the dev server's URL rewriting only routes extension-less paths to the front controller
- The guides and API reference are now served from a local copy of the documentation instead of guides.wheels.dev / api.wheels.dev, so they stay readable with no internet connection. The bundle is fetched once — `wheels docs fetch`, or automatically by the Homebrew formula on install/upgrade — and served at `/wheels-docs/guides/` and `/wheels-docs/api/`, with matching Guides and API tabs in the debug bar
- Added a RustCFML engine backend to the CLI: `wheels engines rustcfml install|start|stop|status` downloads the pinned JVM-free RustCFML binary and runs a Wheels app's `public/` directory on it. This is a separate lifecycle from the Lucee `wheels start`/`stop` path; the framework itself has run on RustCFML since 4.0, and this adds the CLI-side dev-server convenience (caveats apply — no JVM, so `CreateObject("java", …)` and any `java.*` app code won't run)
- `wheels generate scaffold <Child> --belongsTo=<parent>` now wires conventional existing parent scaffolds: it adds the inverse `hasMany`, merges children into the parent `show()` finder's static `include` list, and adds related-record links using the child's display field plus an Add link. Parent writes respect `--dry-run` and are reported as modifications, not newly created files. Existing custom associations and edited related blocks are preserved, including on `--force`; missing files or ambiguous custom code are reported for manual wiring rather than rewritten. API scaffolds never modify parent HTML controllers or views.
- `wheels setup agents` writes `.mcp.json` (Claude Code) and `.opencode.json` (OpenCode) so AI assistants can query the app through the Wheels MCP server. It merges rather than overwrites — other servers you already list are preserved, an existing `wheels` entry is corrected, hand-added keys on it are left alone, and every file is validated before any is written. Malformed JSON is left untouched and reported instead of replaced

### Changed

- Guides for `wheels test` now state the CLI throws `Wheels.TestsFailed` on `directoryRejected`, `bundlesDiscovered=0`, and compile-skipped specs — not only Fail/Error — so a vacuous or rejected scope is not documented as exit 0 (#3083)
- Legacy CommandBox `wheels test` / `browser:test` refuse with a deprecation `error()` then `return` and no longer invoke TestBox (`testbox run`). Unrouted `vendor/wheels/controllers/Tests.cfc` is removed; `/wheels/core/tests` and `/wheels/app/tests` stay on `Public.cfc` (#3083)
- `JwtService.decode()` now requires an `exp` claim by default (`requireExpiry=true`). Pass `requireExpiry=false` to accept signed tokens with no expiry. `maxRefreshAge` stays `0` (unbounded refresh) unless a positive window is set
- Channel `publish()` throws `Wheels.Channel.PublishFailed` when the database INSERT fails instead of returning `persisted:false`
- `$getChannelEngine()` throws `Wheels.Channel.UnknownAdapter` for a name that is not `memory` or `database`
- The memory channel engine replays retained events after `lastEventId` on subscribe
- `channelSSETag(events=)` registers `addEventListener` for each named event so they are not dropped by `onmessage`
- Empty and whitespace-only channel names throw `Wheels.Channel.InvalidName`
- `processAction()` returns `false` when a verification aborts or a before filter returns `false` (the halt signal was previously always `true`)
- `processRequest()` accepts opt-in `csrf="exception"` / `csrf="abort"`; the historic test-helper default remains `ignore` and production `protectsFromForgery()` is unchanged
- Database adapters no longer coerce the bound string `"null"` after `IS` / `IS NOT` to SQL NULL, drop MySQL TEXT/float `DEFAULT`, leave boolean `yes`/`no` unquoted, advertise fake SQLite advisory locks, default unknown foreign-key actions to `CASCADE`, emit asymmetric empty-string defaults, map unknown column types silently, or fall back to Oracle `MAX(ROWID)` / SQL Server `@@IDENTITY`
- The unquoted SQL keyword `NULL` and a CFML/Java null still bind as SQL NULL (so an absent uniqueness-scope property does not send the string `[NULL]` to an integer `cfqueryparam`)
- Injector rebind of an existing name resets singleton and request-scoped flags and drops `request.$wheelsDICache`
- `asSingleton()` / `asRequestScoped()` throw `Wheels.Injector` when no mapping is in progress. `map(b).asSingleton().to(...)` flags `b`, not the previous key
- `getMappings()` returns a `Duplicate` copy. Mutating the return value does not change later `getMappings()` or the live mappings table
- `AuthMiddleware` accepts `genericErrors=true` to emit a generic `Unauthorized` JSON body; the default 401 still includes `authResult.error`
- `TenantResolver` accepts `failClosed=true` to 403 unmatched tenants; unmatched requests still proceed on the default datasource unless opted in
- `updateRecord()` / `removeRecord()` accept `all=true` for an intentional full-table write; empty `where` now throws
- `AutoMigrator.diff()` accepts `allowColumnRemoval=false` and reports leftover columns on `unmappedColumns` instead of scheduling drops
- `t.references()` accepts `referenceColumn` (default remains `"id"`)
- Tenant structs may pass `userName` / `password` for per-tenant migrator credentials
- `validatesUniquenessOf` now defaults `includeSoftDeletes` to `false`, so a soft-deleted value can be reused unless the caller opts in (`includeSoftDeletes=true`)
- Query builder docs now state that single-argument `where()` / `orWhere()` is a raw-SQL passthrough; the 2- and 3-argument forms remain the parameterized contract
- `authorize()` / `can()` throw `Wheels.Policy.UnknownAction` when the policy has no method for the action, instead of treating a typo as a deny
- `$currentUserForPolicy()` no longer swallows a throwing DI `currentUser` into the authenticator user, or a throwing authenticator into guest `""`
- `seedOnce()` binds unique-property values in the uniqueness WHERE so an apostrophe (`O'Brien`) no longer creates a duplicate row on the second call
- `generateSeeds()` wraps the write in a transaction and rolls back on any model failure, so a mixed list cannot leave rows
- LocalDisk `$resolve` throws `Wheels.Storage.InvalidKey` for empty and slash-only keys instead of concatenating onto `root`
- LocalDisk `url()` and `signedUrl()` RFC3986-encode object keys the same way S3 does (spaces and reserved characters)
- `S3Disk.delete()` HEADs first and returns false when the object is missing. An existing object still deletes and returns true
- `S3Disk.put()` sends a signed `x-amz-acl` (`public` maps to `public-read`; default and `private` send `private`)
- `signedUrl()` / `presignGetUrl()` throw `Wheels.Storage.InvalidExpiresIn` when `expiresIn` is outside `1..604800`
- `S3Signer.signedHeaders()` accepts an optional `range` argument so the official AWS GET Object header-auth vector can be pinned. The no-range canonical request is unchanged
- LocalDisk `$resolve` and S3Disk `$request` are public so tests can call and override them. `S3Disk.delete()` still returns true after any 2xx
- `hasManyRadioButton()` now forwards extra HTML attributes (`class`, `rel`, …) the same way `hasManyCheckBox()` does, matching its documented passthrough
- `buttonTo(encode="attributes")` now encodes attribute values the same way `select()` does (`encode=false` still leaves them raw)
- Documented that `sendEmail(writeToFile=)` writes the rendered text/HTML body only — not a MIME `.eml` — so Outlook shows raw HTML tags on a `.eml` path while live `cfmail` delivery still renders. Restored v4 `sendEmail` examples and replaced the v3 `.eml` sample with `.html` (#3529)
- Debug bar: clicking the X now slides the bar left to a logo-sized black strip with the stylized red W, instead of a floating "Debug" button. Clicking the collapsed logo expands the bar back across the bottom of the page (#3547)
- `/wheels/api` now serves the prebuilt Starlight API reference rather than rendering it from the installed framework's source comments, so what you read locally is identical to api.wheels.dev instead of a second rendering of it. `public/docs/core.cfm` is retained because `/wheels/ai` still derives its condensed API summary from it, but it no longer backs a browsable page
- `wheels new` apps now style flash messages as colored boxes instead of bare text: `success` (created/updated/deleted) renders in a green box, `notice` in a blue box, and `warning` in an amber box — matching the existing red validation-error box, with icon prefixes and dark-mode variants
- `wheels generate auth` now hashes passwords with bcrypt via the framework's `bcryptHash()` / `bcryptVerify()` / `bcryptNeedsRehash()` helpers (bundled jBCrypt on JVM engines, native builtins on RustCFML) instead of the PBKDF2-based `passwordHasher` service. The generated `User` model stores a `passwordHash` column (renamed from `passwordDigest`), no longer registers a `passwordHasher` service, and equalizes login timing with a bcrypt derivation. Existing generated apps keep their own code (generated files are code-you-own); the `wheels.auth.PasswordHasher` service remains available for apps that prefer PBKDF2.
- Scaffolded views now render every action as a button. `Edit`, `New <model>` and `← all <models>` were plain links sitting beside a `Delete` button; they all carry simple.css's `.button` class now, so links and form buttons are visually identical. The action row is a `<div>` rather than a `<p>` because `buttonTo` emits a `<form>`, which a paragraph cannot legally contain
- `wheels generate scaffold` now emits full CRUD controller specs (`index`, `new`, `create`, `show`, `edit`, `update`, `delete`) with `processRequest` assertions and `model().create()` test data, matching the spirit of Rails scaffold controller tests. Sample attribute structs use quoted colon keys (`{"title": "MyString"}`) so CFML keeps the real property names. The app-test runner now delays `redirectTo()` (same as the core runner) so create/update/delete specs cannot `cflocation`-abort `wheels test` — that leak rendered `show.cfm` with `findByKey()=false` and crashed with `there is no property with name [TITLE] found in [boolean]`. Mutating actions assert HTTP 303. Re-generate existing apps with `--force` to pick up the richer specs.
- Scaffolded CRUD controllers now set a success flash message on create, update, and delete (e.g. "Post was created successfully."), which the default layout renders via `flashMessages()`. This matches the feedback Rails/Laravel scaffolds provide out of the box.
- Scaffolded (and new-app) forms now stack labels above full-width inputs and nest validation errors in the field block, styled in red with an icon and `Error:` prefix so the cue is not color-only (#3549, #3550)
- Scaffolded apps now bundle simple.css locally in `public/stylesheets/simple.css` (MIT, ~10 KB) instead of loading it from a CDN, so fresh apps and demos render with the default styling offline. It remains a classless stylesheet — remove the `<link>` in `app/views/layout.cfm` (and the file) to use your own CSS, with no markup cleanup needed.
- The scaffolded starter page now offers two calls to action instead of six — the guides and the API reference — and both open in a new tab so following the docs doesn't navigate away from the app. The API card points at `/wheels/api` (the browsable reference) rather than `/wheels/ai`, which serves JSON
- `wheels start --port=<n>` now actually starts on that port for Lucee apps; the flag was previously parsed and then discarded, so the server silently booted on the port pinned in `lucee.json`. The shutdown port is now derived from the requested port (the next free one), so two projects can no longer collide on it — that collision surfaced as LuCLI's `port conflicts detected:` with an empty list
- `wheels new` now scaffolds a richer starter page at `/`: the Wheels mark, a **Welcome to \<app\>** heading, the runtime details (Wheels version, engine, datasource, environment), and cards linking to the guides, routes, migrator, tests, system info, and API reference. It replaces the short prose line and three-item list, and is deliberately styled to be replaced before deployment
- The scaffolded starter page now uses the official Wheels wordmark instead of an inline SVG mark with a text label. It ships in two files — the default for light backgrounds and a white-lettering inverse for dark ones — swapped automatically via `prefers-color-scheme`, so the logo stays legible in either theme

### Deprecated

- `wheels.Test` (RocketUnit) now emits a one-time deprecation warning; removal is planned for Wheels 5.0.

### Performance

- Model instances are now created with a direct constructor call instead of the DI container's resolve/auto-wire path plus a reflective invoke, and the per-instance class-struct read no longer takes a named lock — roughly 1.5x faster model materialization on top of the previous #3213 work (#3462).
